# Research log: heavy-hydraulics

Newest first.

## 2026-11-19 · Milestone 7z: a second Casappa family, Polaris 30, read from its vector curves to test whether a catalogue family other than Danfoss bends (7y's Next y; predictions written before any 7z number was computed)
- Why: 7y's Next (y) asked for a catalogue in the 30 to 45 cm<sup>3</sup>/r range that draws input power at 100 bar or less. Screen (2026-11-19, before any number): Marzocchi's GHP catalogue (2017) draws curves only for GHP1 (to 13.8 cm<sup>3</sup>/r); its GHP3 shares ALP3's displacements and the PDF stops at GHP2's ports; FCIP cast iron (2022) and Elika draw no power curves; the ALP-GHP multiple-pump catalogue has none. Casappa's download server (`/c/downloads/download/<n>/`) holds Polaris PH (PH20 only, to 33 cm<sup>3</sup>/r, 50 to 250 bar), Kappa 40 (61 to 151 cm<sup>3</sup>/r: 129 L/min or more at 2100 r/min, above both bend flows) and **Polaris PL 06 T A** (2024; PLP 30, 22 to 91 cm<sup>3</sup>/r, pages 18 to 20): flow at 20 bar and at each size's maximum, and power and torque at 50, 100, 150, 200 (some) and the maximum pressure, as vector paths in the same layout as the Kappa catalogue `digitise_casappa_kappa30.py` reads. At 2100 r/min the bend's 64 and 79 L/min fall between PLP 30.27 and 30.43, inside the family, so nothing is held or spun.
- Disclosed before the run: Polaris 30 is the same maker as the KP30 family already in the panel, so it is a second family, not an independent maker; its curves are at 50 &deg;C with ISO VG 46 oil (KP30: 36 cSt at 40 &deg;C), and the charts carry a 2003 drawing date. No Polaris number (flow, power, b) has been computed or read off a chart; only the page images were looked at to find the layout.
- Method fixed now: `experiments/digitise_casappa_polaris30.py` (vector paths, as the Kappa reader) writes `data/casappa_plp30_curves.csv` in the shared schema; `experiments/gear_pump_polaris.py` builds `Gear(rows, sizes)` and reads P<sub>idle</sub>(64), P<sub>idle</sub>(79) and b at 7j's central condition (PVG32 neutral reading, 2 L/min standby, idle speed ratio 1), with the same top-band check as 7y, against the seven families of `catalogue_screen.py`. "Bends" means b &gt; 1.55, as 7y.
- **Predictions:**
  - PZ1: Polaris 30 reads flat (b &le; 1.55). Prior 80%.
  - PZ2: |b(PLP 30) &minus; b(KP 30)| &lt; 0.10 (KP30 1.373). Prior 50%.
  - PZ3: PLP 30's P<sub>idle</sub>(64) is within 25% of KP30's 2.373 kW. Prior 60%.
  - PZ4: its top-band (7y) b differs from its full b by more than 0.10. Prior 55%.

### Outcome of 7z (`experiments/digitise_casappa_polaris30.py`, `data/casappa_plp30_curves.csv`, `experiments/gear_pump_polaris.py`, `experiments/gear_pump_polaris_results.txt`, `tests/test_gear_pump_polaris.py` 4 tests; numpy only, under a second; no Hugging Face data)
- **Digitising checks:** every chart's frame was found and its grid matched (flow chart 11 intervals of 20 L/min; power charts 10 to 16 intervals, each matching the top label read from the page image); the 20-bar flow lines' slopes equal the printed displacements to 0.1% (ratio Q<sub>20</sub>/(V n) 1.0000 to 1.0004 at 2100 r/min). A one-off read of PLP 30.34's dashed torque curves at 1500 r/min (right axis from its labels, M = 6 (P<sub>grid</sub> &minus; 30)) gives an input power 4.5 to 6.4% below the solid power curves at 50 to 240 bar, so the catalogue's two curve sets disagree by about 5%; the torque curves are not used. Overall efficiency at 150 bar and 2100 r/min is 0.665 to 0.735, below KP30's 0.73 to 0.80 at 1500 r/min. PLP 30.43's zero-pressure drag (0.788 kW) breaks the size trend (0.665 to 2.654 kW); it lies outside the bend's sizes (30.27, 30.34, 30.38).
- **Result** (central condition): PLP 30 P<sub>idle</sub>(64) **2.650 kW**, P<sub>idle</sub>(79) **3.372 kW**, **b 1.272**: flat, the lowest b of the eight families. With it the non-Danfoss families span b 1.272 to 1.373 (median 1.295, seven families from three makers), Danfoss 1.781. Top band: P(64) +63.6%, b 1.131 (&Delta;b &minus;0.141).
- **PZ1 held** (b 1.272 &le; 1.55). **PZ2 refuted**, narrowly: |b(PLP 30) &minus; b(KP 30)| = 0.101 against a 0.10 bound. **PZ3 held** (P(64) ratio 1.117). **PZ4 held** (&Delta;b &minus;0.141). Three of four.
- Reading: the one catalogue found in range that draws 50 and 100 bar curves does not bend; it sits with the other six. Since 7q the gear-pump results have treated the Danfoss bend as a property of real pumps that the catalogue panel under-samples ("a data gap, not a modelling one", 7u). After 7z the panel reads it the other way: one family in eight bends, and it is the only one. Whether that is Danfoss's pump or Danfoss's curves (its rated-pressure curve starts near 1000 r/min, 6m) cannot be told from catalogues; a bench measurement of a Group 3 SNP3 at 20 to 50 bar would. The selection rules tuned for a bending family (7r to 7t's bend tables) should be described in the paper as fitted to one family, not as a class of pumps.
- Limits: same maker as KP30 (Casappa), so not an independent maker; ISO VG 46 against KP30's 36 cSt oil; 2003 charts; one central condition (the other 21 conditions not rerun); the paper (manuscript, supplement) is not updated in this step.
- Next: (p) one paragraph in the supplement's S2 and the manuscript's limitation 5 saying the bend is one family in eight, with `check_numbers.py` rows; (y) a bench measurement of a Danfoss Group 3 pump at low pressure, or any second maker's catalogue with low-pressure curves (Turolla, Bucher, Settima not yet tried); (e2) the owner picks a venue; (a) a multi-unit measured dataset.

## 2026-11-19 · Milestone 7y: where a second measured bending family could come from, and whether a catalogue that draws power only near rated pressure can supply one (7t's Next y; predictions written before any 7y number was computed)
- Why: 7t's Next (y), the step every gear-pump result since 7q has waited on: a second family whose measured idle drag bends like Danfoss's (b = P<sub>idle</sub>(79)/P<sub>idle</sub>(64) = 1.781, against 1.29 to 1.37 for the other catalogue families). 7x's (e3), reading MDPI's and SAGE's AI rules, was probed first: mdpi.com, sagepub.com, acm.org and ieeeaccess.ieee.org all returned 403 from this container on 2026-11-19, so it stays open.
- Screen (2026-11-19, before any number): Danfoss GearMe Aluminum Group 2 (3.9 to 25.2 cm<sup>3</sup>/r, about 52 L/min at 2100 r/min, below the bend's flows), Group 4 (no performance graphs), Group 0/1, 2 Flex and 2 Compact (too small or no curves); Parker PGP030 to 076 (input power at 172 bar only); Turolla Alu Group 3 (host returned HTML) and Danfoss Rugid Group 2.5 (empty download), unread. Magister Hydraulics' GP3K catalogue (20 to 90 cm<sup>3</sup>/r, pp. 62 to 66, vector-drawn) is the one new family in range with input-power curves, but it draws them only in a band within 50 bar of each size's maximum (250 to 300 bar on GP3K20 to 28, 170 to 230 bar on GP3K71 to 90) and one flow line per size, so its idle drag would be an extrapolation over 150 to 250 bar.
- Method fixed now: `experiments/catalogue_screen.py`. On the seven families whose catalogues draw low-pressure curves (Casappa KP30, Danfoss SNP3, Marzocchi ALP3, Rexroth AZPG, AZPU, AZPN, AZPF held), cut each size's power curves to the same top band (curves at or above its highest drawn pressure minus 50 bar; the top two if fewer), keep the flow lines as drawn, and recompute P<sub>idle</sub>(64), P<sub>idle</sub>(79) and b at 7j's central idle condition with the model's own reading (`Gear.idle`). A family "bends" if b &gt; 1.55 (midway between Danfoss and the next family). No Hugging Face data needed.
- **Predictions:**
  - PY1: Danfoss's top-band b differs from its full b by more than 0.10. Prior 60%.
  - PY2: the median |&Delta;b| over the seven families exceeds 0.10. Prior 45%.
  - PY3: the top-band P<sub>idle</sub>(64) errs by more than 25% in at least four of seven families. Prior 55%.
  - PY4: at least one family changes class (bends/flat) at b = 1.55. Prior 35%.

### Outcome of 7y (`experiments/catalogue_screen.py`, `experiments/catalogue_screen_results.txt`, `tests/test_catalogue_screen.py` 3 tests; numpy only, under a second; no Hugging Face data)
- **Top-band against full reading** (central condition; P in kW): Casappa P(64) 2.373 to 3.866 (+62.9%), b 1.373 to 1.275; **Danfoss** P(64) 2.418 to 1.706, P(79) 4.308 to **&minus;0.019**, b **1.781 to &minus;0.011**; Marzocchi +3.3%, b 1.295 to 1.206; Rexroth AZPG P(64) 1.766 to 0.073 (&minus;95.9%), b 1.314 to **5.914**; AZPU 1.693 to 0.649, b 1.341 to **2.620**; AZPN &minus;13.0%, b 1.281 to 1.203; AZPF &minus;18.8%, b 1.295 to 1.311. Median |&Delta;b| 0.098 (max 4.600), median |P(64) error| 29.5%; the zero-pressure drag P(0) errs by a median of +22% to &minus;363% per family.
- **Class at b = 1.55:** three of seven change class. The one measured bending family reads flat; two flat families read as bending more steeply than Danfoss.
- **PY1 held** (&Delta;b &minus;1.793). **PY2 refuted** (median 0.098, just under 0.10: four families move by less than 0.10, three by 1.3 to 4.6, so the median hides the failure). **PY3 held** (four of seven above 25%). **PY4 held** (three of seven). Three of four.
- Reading: a catalogue that draws input power only within 50 bar of rated pressure cannot say whether a family bends. Its drag is a straight line carried 150 to 250 bar below the last drawn curve, and the slope of that line between two high-pressure curves 30 to 100 bar apart is set by how the curves are drawn there, not by the pump's drag. On the seven families where the answer is known, the extrapolation flips the class of three, including Danfoss itself, and gives a negative absorbed power once. So Magister GP3K, the one new family in range found here, cannot be the second bending family, and neither can any catalogue drawn that way. Of the catalogues this container reached, none in the excavator's flow range draws low-pressure power curves, so (y) needs a bench dataset or a catalogue like Danfoss's, Casappa's or Rexroth's that draws a 50 or 100 bar curve.
- Limits: one band width (50 bar, Magister's) and one rule for keeping the top two; flow lines left as drawn, so the test isolates the drag extrapolation and understates a real top-band catalogue's error (Magister also draws one flow line per size and its curves are at 16 mm<sup>2</sup>/s and 60 &deg;C, not the others' 28 to 30 mm<sup>2</sup>/s). The screen covers the catalogues found and reachable in one session (seven Danfoss, Parker, Magister, Turolla); Casappa Polaris and Marzocchi GHP catalogues on directindustry were not opened.
- Next: (y) open Casappa Polaris PL/PH and Marzocchi GHP group 3 catalogues and check each for a power curve at 100 bar or less in the 30 to 45 cm<sup>3</sup>/r range; (e3) MDPI's and SAGE's AI rules from an unproxied browser; (e2) the owner picks a venue; (a) a multi-unit measured dataset.

## 2026-11-19 · Milestone 7x (paper step): which venue that allows disclosed AI use fits this paper, and the portfolio's venues read
- Why: 7w's VENUE.md option 2 ("Which one fits fluid-power work best was not researched in this step") and its register's 10 programmes whose venues were not yet read. Choosing a venue is the owner's; reading the venues' rules is not. No result changes.

### Outcome of 7x (`paper/VENUE.md`; `research/_shared/venue_policies.json`, `venue_policy.py --venue`, `test_venue_policy.py`; no experiment run)
- **Pages read on 2026-11-19** (quotes and URLs in the register): NeurIPS 2025 LLM policy, ICML 2026 call for papers, CoRL and RSS author instructions (all allow LLM use; CoRL requires a disclosure at submission, RSS encourages one; all desk-reject invented references); Springer Nature's "AI use in manuscript preparation" (red, not permitted: "writing results or discussion independently; generating scientific claims or interpretations"); Taylor & Francis ("does not allow generative AI to create a first draft of a manuscript or sections of a manuscript"); Wiley (disclose; AI "not a replacement for their own expertise and judgement"); Cambridge University Press (declare and explain). **Refused or not found:** ACM, SAGE, OUP, MDPI, Wiley's fuller AI page and sciencedirect.com scope pages (403); AIAA's author page names no policy; MLCAD's page states none; the EC 2026 and Demographic Research URLs tried were 404. These are entered as `unchecked` with the reason, never guessed.
- **Portfolio:** `venue_policy.py` now reads 22 programmes as 1 prohibits (heavy-hydraulics), 17 with a disclosing venue, 4 unchecked (aerolog-scaling: AIAA; llm-coding-inference: SAGE; verifiable-training-mechanisms: ACM; verified-dse: MLCAD/ICCAD), 0 unmatched. theism-formal-evidence's second venue (*Int. J. Philosophy of Religion*, Springer) prohibits; its first (*Religious Studies*, Cambridge) allows declared use.
- **This paper:** the eleven `refs.bib` entries with a journal or proceedings field sit in *Energies* (3), *IEEE Access*, *Automation in Construction*, *Proc. IMechE Part I*, *Applied Engineering in Agriculture*, *Research in Agricultural Engineering*, *Science*, one minor journal and an ICE symposium. Classified: arXiv and *IEEE Access* disclose; *Automation in Construction* disclose with human ownership; *IEEE/ASME T-Mech* **mixed** (a new `--venue` verdict: ASME prohibits, IEEE discloses); *Energies* and *Proc. IMechE* unchecked (403).
- Tests: `_shared/test_venue_policy.py` 7 (3 new: unchecked entries carry a reason and no date, ids unique, `--venue` runs; the fixture gains an unmatched row and a Springer/T&F row that must read `prohibits`), all pass. `paper/venue_check.py` unchanged, 3 of 6.
- Reading: the useful line is not "disclose or not" but "may AI write the results and discussion if disclosed". Springer Nature and Taylor & Francis, between them publishing much of mechanical engineering, say no, so 7w's "IEEE, Elsevier and arXiv allow disclosed use" understated how narrow the field is. For this paper the honest options today are an arXiv preprint and an IEEE journal.
- Limits: one day's reading of publisher-wide pages; individual journals can be stricter (Cambridge says so). Five publishers' pages could not be read from this container. Fit to scope is judged from where the cited work appeared, not from the journals' scope pages, which returned 403.
- Next: (e2) the owner picks a venue (VENUE.md); (e3) read MDPI's and SAGE's rules from an unproxied browser; then the format pass for the chosen venue; (y) a second measured bending family; (a) a multi-unit measured dataset.

## 2026-11-19 · Milestone 7w (paper step): the target venues' rules on generative AI, and the manuscript against IJFP's format
- Why: 7v's Next (e) was "the journal template, author block and its word limit (owner)". Reading the template's pages is not an owner step, so this step read them. It changes no result.

### Outcome of 7w (`paper/VENUE.md`, `paper/venue_check.py`, `paper/manuscript.md`, `tests/test_venue_check.py`; portfolio tool `research/_shared/venue_policy.py` with `venue_policies.json` and its test; no experiment run)
- **Both named venues forbid AI-generated content.** IJFP's submission checklist, a box authors tick: "The submission has not been generated by AI tools ... AI tools may be used to improve grammar, but not to generate 'original' content." (Its publication-ethics page, read the same day, asks instead for AI use to be declared; the pages disagree, and the checklist is what authors sign.) ASME's AI position statement: "ASME prohibits the use of generative AI in the creation of content for journal submissions." Whether the Bath/ASME symposium follows the journal statement was not checked. This paper was generated by AI agents, so it cannot honestly go to either. IEEE (disclose in the acknowledgments), Elsevier (declaration, with the analysis owned by a human author) and arXiv (report the use; signing authors take full responsibility) allow disclosed use. Quotes and URLs in `paper/VENUE.md`.
- **Portfolio check:** `research/_shared/venue_policy.py` matches every open programme's venue column in `PROGRAMMES.md` against the register: heavy-hydraulics is the only one of 22 whose venues all prohibit; 11 name an IEEE or Elsevier venue (disclose); 10 name venues not yet in the register (ICML, NeurIPS, CoRL, ACM EC, MLCAD, AIAA and others). ASCE's page returned 403 and is marked unchecked.
- **IJFP format** (River Publishers' author guidelines PDF and `riverk.cls`): title at most 80 characters (ours 164), a 250-word abstract, keywords, a notation list, a figure and table caption list, PDF in the journal template, 500 dpi images, author photographs and CVs. No word or page limit is stated. The journal is open access with a charge after acceptance (owner).
- **Manuscript:** abstract 356 to **248** words, keeping the loader result, the 0.821 crossing, the held-out Rexroth range, the eight-family and 308-case RMS and the catalogue-against-bench contrast, and dropping the narrative clauses; keywords added; a new "AI use" section states that the agents generated the code, analysis and text and that whoever signs takes responsibility; the target-venue line now says "open" and points to VENUE.md. `check_numbers.py --paper manuscript.md` 181 claims, 0 missing, 0 orphans; draft.md 339, 0 missing; `build_tex.py --source manuscript.md` 0 problems; `venue_check.py`: 3 of 6 IJFP checks pass; the title, notation list and caption list fail and are left for whichever venue the owner picks. Tests: `tests/test_venue_check.py` 5, `tests/test_paper_tex.py` 10, `_shared/test_venue_policy.py` 4, all pass.
- Reading: the programme's 300-plus hours of work were aimed at two venues whose rules it could never meet; no earlier milestone read the venues' author pages. The finding is cheap to act on (pick a venue that allows disclosed AI use) and expensive to miss (a box ticked falsely at submission). The same check now runs for every programme.
- Limits: the register holds six publishers' pages read on one day; policies change, so `venue_policies.json` dates each entry. Matching a programme's venue text to a publisher is by substring and can miss a venue named differently.
- Next: (e2) the owner picks a venue (VENUE.md, options); then the format pass for it (title, notation list, caption list from `build_tex.py`); (y) a second measured bending family; (y5) a two-parameter row; (a) a multi-unit measured dataset.

## 2026-11-19 · Milestone 7t: read the bend's anchor off the measured curves (7s's Next y4; predictions written before any 7t number was computed)
- Why: 7s's Next (y4). 7s showed 7r's bend table holds when a steeper synthetic curve keeps its level at 64 L/min (0.0052 to 0.0053 in (A)) and fails when it keeps it at 79 L/min (0.0059, worse than owh's 0.0056). Which anchor is right is a property of real pumps, and the 13 measured donor variants carry it: when a measured idle curve bends more, does its level at a 64 L/min pump stay put? 7t reads the anchor off the donors, per fold, and rebuilds the table with it.
- Disclosed before the run: no 7t level, slope, anchor, pick, feature or score has been computed or looked at; the per-variant bends b are 7s's (printed there), the levels P(64) have not been printed by any milestone I have read. 7s's results file must reproduce byte for byte first on the rebuilt caches.
- Method fixed now: `experiments/gear_pump_anchor.py`, on 7j to 7s's 22 conditions and 4312 cells, 7r's grid &beta; = 1.2 to 1.9 by 0.1, donors, e<sub>m</sub>, grid lookup and owds form unchanged. **Anchor family:** a power bend pivoting at q*(&lambda;) = 64 (79/64)<sup>&lambda;</sup>: P(q) (q/q*)<sup>&gamma;</sup>, &gamma; = ln(&beta;/b<sub>j</sub>)/ln(79/64); &lambda; = 0 is 7r's shape p, &lambda; = 1 is 7s's shape v. If measured curves follow P<sub>j</sub>(q) = A (q/q*)<sup>&gamma;<sub>j</sub></sup>, the slope of ln P<sub>j</sub>(64) on ln b<sub>j</sub> across pumps is &minus;&lambda;. **Estimates** (central idle curve, donors of the held-out maker group only, so the held-out pump enters only through its own data-sheet bend): &lambda;<sub>pool</sub>, minus the OLS slope over the donor variants; &lambda;<sub>fe</sub>, the same slope within maker groups (only the AZPF and AZPN families have two or more variants, which differ by drive speed and gear). No clipping. **Rules:** owh, owdm (references); owdb (&lambda; 0) and owdbv (&lambda; 1) as 7s; owdbq (&lambda;<sub>pool</sub>) and owdbf (&lambda;<sub>fe</sub>). **Scores** exactly as 7j to 7s: (A) 308 diagonals; (B1) nine half-speed conditions; (B2) S = 0.20 and 0.50. **Diagnostics:** per variant P(64), P(79), b; per fold &lambda;<sub>pool</sub>, &lambda;<sub>fe</sub> and pivots; Danfoss's own ln P(64) residual from its donors' pooled line (enters no fit); Danfoss-fold row medians by &beta;; (A) over a fixed sweep &lambda; = &minus;0.5 to 1.0 by 0.25.
- Disclosures: the bent curves stay synthetic; the anchor family is one-parameter (power law, pivot moves); the pooled slope mixes the bend with whatever else sets a family's drag level at 64 L/min (size, make), so it is the noisier read; the within-family slope rests on two families whose variants differ by modelled drive transforms, not separate measurements.
- **Predictions:**
  - PT1: owdb and owdbv reproduce 7s's 0.0052 and 0.0059 in (A). Prior 99%.
  - PT2: Danfoss-fold &lambda;<sub>pool</sub> lies in [&minus;0.5, 0.5] (closer to anchor 64 than 79). Prior 45%.
  - PT3: Danfoss-fold &lambda;<sub>fe</sub> lies in [&minus;0.5, 0.5]. Prior 45%.
  - PT4: owdbq's (A) RMS is below owh's 0.0056. Prior 50%.
  - PT5: owdbf's (A) RMS is below owh's 0.0056. Prior 50%.
  - PT6: owdbq and owdbf within 0.0003 in (A). Prior 45%.
  - PT7: Danfoss's ln P(64) lies below its donors' pooled line (its own picks at 58.5 L/min suggest small sizes are cheap for it). Prior 65%.
  - PT8: owdbq beats owh in all three scores (A, B1, B2). Prior 40%.

### Outcome of 7t (`experiments/gear_pump_anchor.py`, `experiments/gear_pump_anchor_results.txt`; numpy and h5py; 7s's results file reproduced byte for byte first (about 14 min with the data and cell caches rebuilt from Hugging Face), then 7t in under 10 min on the cache; 6 new tests plus a verdict test, `tests/test_gear_pump_anchor.py`; manuscript section 3.5, README and `paper/check_numbers.py` updated)
- **Measured levels** (central condition, kW for a 64 L/min pump): Danfoss 2.418 with b 1.781; the catalogue families 1.69 to 2.48 with b 1.29 to 1.37. The four AZPN variants share P(64) 1.776 exactly: the spin and step-up drives charge their extra drag only above the family's largest size (75.6 L/min), so they raise P(79) and b but never P(64). The AZPF's largest size is 58.9 L/min, so its drives raise P(64) as well.
- **Anchor estimates, Danfoss's fold** (13 donors): &lambda;<sub>pool</sub> **+0.105** (pivot 65.4 L/min), &lambda;<sub>fe</sub> **&minus;0.219** (pivot 61.1). The within-family read is the mean of two artefacts of the drive model: the AZPN family alone gives &lambda; 0.000 (azpf's fold), the AZPF family alone &minus;1.003 (azpn's fold). In every other fold &lambda;<sub>pool</sub> is &minus;0.52 to &minus;0.75, because those folds include Danfoss, whose high level and high bend drive the pooled slope; over all 14 variants &lambda;<sub>pool</sub> is &minus;0.565.
- **Danfoss against its donors' pooled line:** ln P(64) residual **+0.251** (2.418 kW measured, 1.882 on the line): Danfoss is dearer at 64 L/min than a pump of its bend should be on the donors' evidence, not cheaper.
- **Rows** (Danfoss's fold, median pick at &beta; 1.8): &lambda; 0 68.0, &lambda; 1 72.0, &lambda;<sub>pool</sub> 68.5, &lambda;<sub>fe</sub> 67.0 (own picks 58.5). Danfoss kW<sub>table</sub>/kW<sub>m</sub>: 1.193, 1.301, 1.206, **1.165**.
- **(A), 308 diagonals:** owh 0.0056, owdm 0.0047, owdb 0.0052, owdbv 0.0059, owdbq **0.0052**, owdbf **0.0050** (max per condition 0.0057, bias &minus;0.0019). **(B1):** owdbq 0.0055, owdbf 0.0053 (owh 0.0059). **(B2):** owdbq 0.0055, owdbf 0.0053 (owh 0.0057). **Danfoss (A):** owdbf **0.0054**, owdbq 0.0068, owh 0.0061: under owdbf a builder-side table beats the two-price rule on Danfoss for the first time.
- **Sweep (diagnostic, fixed &lambda; in every fold):** (A) falls steadily as the pivot moves down, 0.0059 at 79 L/min, 0.0052 at 64, 0.0051 at 60.7, **0.0050 at 57.6** (&lambda; &minus;0.5); Danfoss 0.0110, 0.0063, 0.0052, 0.0046. The score has no interior minimum in the registered range, so it cannot itself say where the true anchor is; it says only that lower is better for the one pump the table helps.
- **PT1 held** (0.0052, 0.0059). **PT2 held** (+0.105). **PT3 held** (&minus;0.219). **PT4 held** (0.0052). **PT5 held** (0.0050). **PT6 held** (0.0002). **PT7 refuted** (+0.251). **PT8 held** (owdbq beats owh in A, B1, B2). Seven of eight.
- Reading: the measured donors rule out 7s's failing anchor. Neither estimate puts the pivot anywhere near 79 L/min for the fold that matters (65.4 and 61.1 L/min), so the anchor a builder can read without Danfoss is 7r's or a little below it, and with that anchor the bend table keeps beating the two-price rule in all three scores (owdbq 0.0052, 0.0055, 0.0055; owdbf 0.0050, 0.0053, 0.0053). Two cautions keep this a sensitivity. First, the better estimate (owdbf) rests on how two families' modelled drive transforms move their drag, which is a statement about where each catalogue ends (75.6 against 58.9 L/min), not about how a pump's bend relates to its level. Second, PT7's refutation says Danfoss sits 29% above the donors' line at 64 L/min: its curve is high everywhere and steep, which no one-parameter bend of a donor captures, so the rows still charge it 1.17 times its own-pick drag. The robust finding is negative and useful: the pivot-79 reading 7s had to leave open is not what the measured families show. The two-price rule stays the headline form until a measured second bending family tests the table.
- Limits: as 7s; a one-parameter (power-law) anchor family; 13 donors from seven maker groups; within-family variation is modelled drive transforms of two catalogue families; the sweep stops at &lambda; &minus;0.5 by registration.
- Next: (y) a second bending-curve family, measured (tests 7o's tilt, 7r's table and the anchor at once); (y5) a two-parameter row (bend and level residual against the donors' line, PT7's +0.251) to price Danfoss's height as well as its bend; (z) a two-feature tilt; (p) the geared AZPN's residual against a measured PTO gearbox map; (a) a multi-unit measured dataset; (e) the journal template and author block (owner).

## 2026-11-19 · Milestone 7s: does 7r's bend table survive a different bend shape? (7r's Next y3; predictions written before any 7s number was computed)
- Why: 7r's Next (y3). 7r's table beat the two-price rule (0.0052 against 0.0056 in (A)) with rows built on one synthetic shape, a power of flow pivoting at 64 L/min, (q/64)<sup>&gamma;</sup>. 7r's own reading put its 0.0004 margin "well inside what a different synthetic shape could move". 7s rebuilds the rows with three other shapes that give the same two-point bend b and asks whether the margin over owh holds.
- Disclosed before the run: no 7s curve, pick, feature, fit or score has been computed or looked at. 7r's results file must reproduce byte for byte first on the rebuilt caches.
- Method fixed now: `experiments/gear_pump_bendshape.py`, on 7j to 7r's 22 conditions and 4312 cells, 7r's grid &beta; = 1.2 to 1.9 by 0.1, donors, e<sub>m</sub>, row construction, the grid lookup (nearest b(k)) and the owds form unchanged; only the shape of the bent curve changes. For donor j with bend b<sub>j</sub> and target &beta;: **(p) power, pivot 64** (7r): P(q) (q/64)<sup>&gamma;</sup>, &gamma; = ln(&beta;/b<sub>j</sub>)/ln(79/64). **(h) hinge:** P(q) unchanged up to 64 L/min, P(q) (q/64)<sup>&gamma;</sup> above (same &gamma;), so the bend acts only above the lower quartile pick. **(v) power, pivot 79:** P(q) (q/79)<sup>&gamma;</sup>, the same bend with the level held at 79 L/min instead of 64 (a level shift of the whole curve by (64/79)<sup>&gamma;</sup>). **(x) exponential:** P(q) exp(&kappa;(q &minus; 64)), &kappa; = ln(&beta;/b<sub>j</sub>)/15, level held at 64. All four give b = &beta; exactly; standby kW unchanged. **Rules:** owh, owdm, owdtp as references; owdb (7r's, shape p), owdbh, owdbv, owdbx2 (shapes h, v, x), each the owds form with the fold's test rows reading its table feature and training rows 7n's kW<sub>s</sub>. **Scores** exactly as 7j to 7r: (A) 308 diagonals; (B1) nine half-speed conditions held out; (B2) S = 0.20 and 0.50 held out. **Diagnostics:** per shape the Danfoss-fold row median pick at each grid &beta;, Danfoss's kW/kW<sub>m</sub> at the central condition, per-variant (A).
- Disclosures: every shape is synthetic; the four were chosen as the obvious one-parameter alternatives (where the curve is anchored, and power against exponential in flow), not tuned; training rows read 7n's own picks; donors keep their own efficiency shapes.
- **Predictions:**
  - PS1: owdb reproduces 7r's 0.0052 in (A). Prior 99%.
  - PS2: owdbh's (A) RMS is below owh's 0.0056. Prior 45%.
  - PS3: owdbv's (A) RMS is below owh's 0.0056. Prior 45%.
  - PS4: owdbx2's (A) RMS is below owh's 0.0056. Prior 60% (close to the power law over 64 to 79 L/min).
  - PS5: the four shapes' (A) RMS span at most 0.0005. Prior 55%.
  - PS6: all four shapes' (A) RMS are below owdtp's 0.0061. Prior 65%.
  - PS7: the hinge row's median pick for Danfoss's fold at &beta; = 1.8 is at least 7r's 68.0 L/min (the hinge leaves small sizes' drag unchanged, so it pulls picks down less). Prior 60%.
  - PS8: at least three of the four shapes beat owh in all three scores (A, B1, B2). Prior 35%.

### Outcome of 7s (`experiments/gear_pump_bendshape.py`, `experiments/gear_pump_bendshape_results.txt`; numpy and h5py; 7r's results file reproduced byte for byte first (13.8 min with the data and cell caches rebuilt from Hugging Face), then 7s in 2.4 min on the cache; 6 new tests plus a verdict test, `tests/test_gear_pump_bendshape.py`; manuscript section 3.5, README and `paper/check_numbers.py` updated)
- **Rows by bend** (Danfoss's donors, e<sub>m</sub> 0.8075): the three shapes anchored at 64 L/min move the median pick the same way, 74.5 L/min at &beta; 1.2 to 67.0 (p), 67.5 (h, x) at 1.9, and all three give **68.0 at 1.8**. The pivot-79 shape barely moves it: 73.0 at 1.2, **72.0 at 1.8**, 71.5 at 1.9. Holding the level at 79 lowers a steeper curve's drag at every smaller size, so the bend no longer pushes the optimum down.
- **Danfoss at the central condition**, kW over its leak-free own-pick drag kW<sub>m</sub>: p 1.193, h 1.204, x 1.198, **v 1.301** (7q's pooled table 1.329). For the other 13 variants the four shapes agree within 0.03.
- **(A), 308 diagonals:** owh 0.0056, owdm 0.0047, owdtp 0.0061; owdb (p) **0.0052**, owdbh **0.0053**, owdbx2 **0.0053**, owdbv **0.0059**. **(B1):** p, h, x 0.0056, v 0.0062 (owh 0.0059). **(B2):** p 0.0055, h and x 0.0056, v 0.0064 (owh 0.0057).
- **Per variant (A):** Danfoss 0.0063 (p), 0.0066 (h), 0.0065 (x), 0.0110 (v); owh 0.0061. The other 13 variants move by at most 0.0006 between shapes.
- **Post hoc (not registered), without Danfoss** (286 diagonals): owh 0.0056, owdb, owdbh, owdbx2 0.0052, owdbv 0.0053: the shape matters only for the one pump the table needs it for.
- **PS1 held** (0.0052). **PS2 held** (0.0053). **PS3 refuted** (0.0059 against 0.0056). **PS4 held** (0.0053). **PS5 refuted** (span 0.0007). **PS6 held** (all four below 0.0061). **PS7 held** (68.0). **PS8 held** (p, h and x beat owh in all three scores). Six of eight.
- Reading: 7r's result survives a change of functional form but not a change of anchor. Power, hinge and exponential, all holding the curve's level at 64 L/min, give 0.0052 to 0.0053 in (A) and beat the two-price rule in all three scores; their rows agree to half a litre per minute. Holding the level at 79 L/min instead makes a steeper row cheaper at small sizes, its picks stay near 72 L/min, and the table falls back to about 7q's pooled table (Danfoss 0.0110, (A) 0.0059, worse than owh). So the table's gain rests on one modelling choice: that a pump that bends more pays more drag above its lower quartile pick, not less below it. Danfoss's measured curve, read against its donors, is the only evidence on which choice is right, and it cannot be used without leaking into its own fold. The two-price rule stays the builder's form; a measured second bending family (y) would now also settle the anchor.
- Limits: as 7r; four shapes, chosen as the obvious one-parameter alternatives; one bending family.
- Next: (y) a second bending-curve family, measured (tests 7o's tilt, 7r's table and now 7s's anchor); (y4) read the anchor off the measured families' own curves (how the level at 64 and at 79 L/min moves with b across the 13 variants) as a builder-side way to pick it; (z) a two-feature tilt; (p) the geared AZPN's residual against a measured PTO gearbox map; (a) a multi-unit measured dataset; (e) the journal template and author block (owner).

## 2026-11-18 · Milestone 7r: a pick table by bend, with rows the model builds for synthetic bending curves (7q's Next y2; predictions written before any 7r number was computed)
- Why: 7q's Next (y2). 7q's pick table failed because no donor bends like Danfoss (b 1.781 against 1.281 to 1.497), so every row put its picks near 72 L/min. Until a second bending family is measured, a publisher can still print a row for a bend no measured pump has: bend each donor's own idle curve to a target b, run the motion model on that donor with the bent curve, and tabulate the picks. A builder reads the row nearest their pump's data-sheet bend. This is a sensitivity (the rows rest on synthetic curves, not a second measured family), registered against owh.
- Disclosed before the run: no 7r curve, pick, feature, fit or score has been computed or looked at. 7q's results file must reproduce byte for byte first on the rebuilt caches.
- Method fixed now: `experiments/gear_pump_bendtable.py`, on 7j to 7q's 22 conditions and 4312 cells unchanged. **Bent curve:** for donor j and target bend &beta;, P<sub>j</sub><sup>&beta;</sup>(q) = P<sub>idle,j</sub>(q) (q/64)<sup>&gamma;</sup>, &gamma; = ln(&beta;/b<sub>j</sub>)/ln(79/64), on the central idle curve, so b of the bent curve is exactly &beta; and its level at 64 L/min is the donor's own; standby kW unchanged. **Rows:** &beta; on the grid 1.2 to 1.9 in steps of 0.1; row(&beta;; k) = equal-share mean over every donor j outside k's maker group of j's picks with P<sub>j</sub><sup>&beta;</sup> at 7p's e<sub>m</sub>(k). **Features** (k's own idle curve, each condition, weight fixed at the central setting): kW<sub>b</sub> over the grid row nearest b(k); kW<sub>bx</sub> over a row built at &beta; = b(k) exactly. Danfoss's curve enters no row except as the held-out pump's own data-sheet bend. **Rules:** owh, owdm, owdtp (7q's pooled table) as references; owdb and owdbx, the owds form whose held-out fold's test rows read kW<sub>b</sub> or kW<sub>bx</sub> while training rows keep 7n's kW<sub>s</sub> (as 7p and 7q). **Scores** exactly as 7j to 7q: (A) leave-one-condition-and-maker-family-out over 308 diagonals; (B1) nine half-speed conditions held out; (B2) S = 0.20 and 0.50 held out. **Diagnostics:** per grid &beta; the pooled row's median pick (donors of Danfoss's fold); per variant b, the grid &beta; used, the row median and kW<sub>b</sub>/kW<sub>s</sub>, kW<sub>b</sub>/kW<sub>m</sub> at the central condition.
- Disclosures: the bend is 7q's two-point ratio and the power-law bend is one choice of shape among many; the bent curves are synthetic (no pump was measured with them); the donors keep their own efficiency shapes; training rows read 7n's own picks.
- **Predictions:**
  - PR1: the pooled row's median pick (Danfoss's donors) does not rise as &beta; goes from 1.2 to 1.9, and at &beta; = 1.8 it is at most 65 L/min (Danfoss's own at e<sub>m</sub>: 58.5). Prior 55%.
  - PR2: Danfoss's kW<sub>b</sub>/kW<sub>m</sub> at the central condition lies in [0.95, 1.10] (7q's t1 read 1.467 of kW<sub>s</sub>). Prior 45%.
  - PR3: owdb's (A) RMS is below owh's 0.0056. Prior 50%.
  - PR4: owdb's (A) RMS is below owdtp's 0.0061. Prior 65%.
  - PR5: Danfoss's per-variant (A) RMS under owdb is at most owh's 0.0061. Prior 40%.
  - PR6: owdb's (A) RMS is above owdm's 0.0047 (the synthetic table does not reach the leak-free own picks). Prior 75%.
  - PR7: owdb's B1 RMS is at most owh's 0.0059. Prior 45%.
  - PR8: owdb and owdbx differ by at most 0.0003 in (A) (the 0.1 grid is fine enough). Prior 70%.
### Outcome of 7r (`experiments/gear_pump_bendtable.py`, `experiments/gear_pump_bendtable_results.txt`; numpy and h5py; 7q's results file reproduced byte for byte first (about 20 min with the data and cell caches rebuilt from Hugging Face), then 7r in 2.2 min on the cache; 4 new tests plus a verdict test, `tests/test_gear_pump_bendtable.py`; manuscript section 3.5, README and `paper/check_numbers.py` updated)
- **Rows by bend** (Danfoss's donors, e<sub>m</sub> 0.8075): the pooled row's median pick falls steadily with &beta;, 74.5 L/min at 1.2, 72.0 at 1.4, 69.5 at 1.6, **68.0 at 1.8**, 67.0 at 1.9; the share of picks below 64 L/min rises from 0.235 to 0.373. Bending a donor's curve does move the optimum down, but by about 1.1 L/min per 0.1 of bend, not to Danfoss's own 58.5.
- **Danfoss at the central condition:** grid row 1.8 (b 1.781), kW<sub>b</sub>/kW<sub>s</sub> 1.197 (7q's pooled table 1.329, nearest donor 1.467), kW<sub>b</sub>/kW<sub>m</sub> 1.193. For the other 13 variants the bend row reads 0.955 to 1.116 of kW<sub>s</sub>, close to the pooled table (most sit on rows 1.3 or 1.4, near their donors' own bends).
- **(A), 308 diagonals:** owh 0.0056, owdm 0.0047, owdtp 0.0061, **owdb 0.0052** (max per condition 0.0060, bias &minus;0.0020), owdbx 0.0053. **(B1):** owdb 0.0056 (owh 0.0059, owdm 0.0049). **(B2):** owdb 0.0055 (owh 0.0057).
- **Per variant (A):** Danfoss 0.0063 under owdb (owh 0.0061, owdtp 0.0122, owdm 0.0066); bench, Rexroth, AZPF and the spun and geared AZPF at or below owdm; the held and spun AZPN worse than owh (0.0035 and 0.0046 against 0.0016 and 0.0022), as under every own-pick form since 7n.
- **Post hoc (not registered), without Danfoss** (286 diagonals): owh 0.0056, owdm 0.0045, owdtp 0.0053, owdb 0.0052: for the 13 other variants the bend row adds 0.0001 over the pooled table, so nearly all of owdb's gain over owdtp is Danfoss's.
- **PR1 refuted** (monotone, but 68.0 at 1.8). **PR2 refuted** (1.193). **PR3 held** (0.0052 against 0.0056). **PR4 held** (against 0.0061). **PR5 refuted** (0.0063 against 0.0061). **PR6 held** (above 0.0047). **PR7 held** (0.0056 against 0.0059). **PR8 held** (0.0052, 0.0053). Five of eight.
- Reading: a qualified positive, as a sensitivity. With a row for the bending class, a builder-side table beats the two-price rule in all three scores (A 0.0052 against 0.0056, B1 0.0056 against 0.0059, B2 0.0055 against 0.0057) and edges 7o's tilt (0.0053), and it does so without learning anything from Danfoss: Danfoss's curve enters only as its own data-sheet bend. It removes the table's Danfoss failure (0.0122 to 0.0063) but does not turn Danfoss into a gain: a power-law bend of a straighter curve moves the donors' picks a third of the way (72.5 to 68.0 L/min, against 58.5), so the row still overcharges Danfoss's drag by a fifth and the fit's bias stays at &minus;0.0020. The part of the own picks a bend does not carry is the rest of Danfoss's shape (its drag level and how its efficiency falls at small sizes). The margin over the two-price rule (0.0004) is about the size 7o's tilt reached and well inside what a different synthetic shape could move, so the two-price rule stays the builder's form; the table is the candidate to test on a measured second bending family.
- Limits: the bent curves are synthetic, and the power law is one shape among many; one bending family to test it on; the bend is one two-point ratio; donors keep their own efficiency shapes; training rows read 7n's own picks.
- Next: (y) a second bending-curve family, measured (now the test for both 7o's tilt and 7r's table); (y3) a second synthetic shape for the bent rows (a bend applied above 72 L/min only, or a level shift with the bend) as a robustness check on 7r's 0.0052; (z) a two-feature tilt; (p) the geared AZPN's residual against a measured PTO gearbox map; (a) a multi-unit measured dataset; (e) the journal template and author block (owner).

## 2026-11-18 · Milestone 7q: a pick table by drag-curve class, the builder-side stand-in for 7p's leak-free own picks (7p's Next x2; predictions written before any 7q number was computed)
- Why: 7p's Next (x2). 7p's leak-free own-pick read (owdm, 0.0047 in (A)) still runs the full motion model on the held-out pump. A publisher who has run the model on the families it has can tabulate their picks once; a builder then looks up the row whose idle drag curve looks most like their pump's and averages their own curve over those sizes. 7q asks whether that table keeps any of owdm's gain over the two-price rule (owh 0.0056).
- Disclosed before the run: no 7q donor, feature, fit or score has been computed or looked at; the bend ratio of each variant's idle curve has not been tabulated for this registration (7l/7m report qualitatively that Danfoss's bends). 7p's results file must reproduce byte for byte first on the rebuilt caches.
- Method fixed now: `experiments/gear_pump_table.py`, on 7j to 7p's 22 conditions and 4312 cells unchanged. **Drag-curve class:** b(k) = P<sub>idle,k</sub>(79 L/min) / P<sub>idle,k</sub>(64 L/min) on the central-condition idle curve (7m's pick quartiles), a number a builder reads off the data sheet. **Table rows:** for held-out variant k, each donor j outside k's maker group contributes its own picks at k's leak-free efficiency e<sub>m</sub>(k) (7p), w<sub>j</sub>(q) = share of the 384 geometries picking q for pump j. **Features** (k's own idle curve, each condition, weight fixed at the central setting): kW<sub>t1</sub> with the weight of the single donor nearest in |log b|; kW<sub>t3</sub> with the mean weight of the three nearest; kW<sub>tp</sub> with the mean weight of every donor (no class). **Rules:** owh, owdw, owds, owdm (7p) as references; owdt1, owdt3, owdtp, the owds form whose held-out fold's test rows use the table feature while training rows keep 7n's kW<sub>s</sub> (as owdm). **Scores** exactly as 7j to 7p: (A) leave-one-condition-and-maker-family-out over 308 diagonals; (B1) nine half-speed conditions held out; (B2) S = 0.20 and 0.50 held out. **Diagnostics:** per variant b, the nearest donor, and kW<sub>t1</sub>/kW<sub>s</sub>, kW<sub>tp</sub>/kW<sub>s</sub> at the central condition.
- Disclosures: the training rows still read 7n's own picks (the publisher's side, which does have the model); donors' picks use their own efficiency shape and idle curve, so the table carries shape as well as drag; e<sub>m</sub> is 7p's.
- **Predictions:**
  - PQ1: Danfoss's kW<sub>t1</sub>/kW<sub>s</sub> at the central condition is at least 1.05 (no other variant bends like it, so no donor picks as small). Prior 65%.
  - PQ2: owdt1's (A) RMS is below owh's 0.0056. Prior 45%.
  - PQ3: owdtp's (A) RMS is below owh's 0.0056. Prior 40%.
  - PQ4: owdt1's (A) RMS is at most owdtp's (the class helps). Prior 50%.
  - PQ5: none of owdt1, owdt3, owdtp reaches owdm's (A) RMS. Prior 75%.
  - PQ6: Danfoss's per-variant (A) RMS under owdt1 is above owh's 0.0061. Prior 65%.
  - PQ7: owdt1's B1 RMS is at most owh's 0.0059. Prior 45%.
### Outcome of 7q (`experiments/gear_pump_table.py`, `experiments/gear_pump_table_results.txt`; numpy and h5py; 7p's results file reproduced byte for byte first (15.2 min, data and cell caches rebuilt from Hugging Face), then 7q in 2.5 min on the cache; 5 new tests, `tests/test_gear_pump_table.py`; manuscript section 3.5, README and `paper/check_numbers.py` updated)
- **Bends:** b runs 1.281 (AZPN held) to 1.497 (AZPN geared at 98%) for 13 variants; Danfoss is alone at **1.781**. Its nearest donors are the AZPN geared at 98%, the spun AZPF and the geared AZPF.
- **Table picks:** the donors' picks at e<sub>m</sub> have medians of 69.5 to 75.5 L/min whatever the class; Danfoss's own picks at e<sub>m</sub> sit at 58.5. Read over the table, Danfoss's drag is 1.467 of its own-pick drag (t1; t3 1.339, tp 1.329); for the other 13 variants t1/kW<sub>s</sub> is 0.959 to 1.138.
- **(A), 308 diagonals:** owh 0.0056, owdm 0.0047, **owdt1 0.0075** (bias &minus;0.0030), owdt3 0.0060, owdtp 0.0061. **(B1):** owdt1 0.0076, owdt3 0.0063 (owh 0.0059). **(B2):** owdt1 0.0084 (owh 0.0057).
- **Per variant (A):** Danfoss 0.0198 under owdt1 (owh 0.0061, owdm 0.0066); Casappa, AZPU and the spun and geared AZPF at or below owdm; the held, spun and geared AZPN worse than owdm by 0.0007 to 0.0035.
- **Post hoc (not registered), without Danfoss** (286 diagonals): owh 0.0056, owdm 0.0045, owdt1 0.0055, owdt3 0.0051, owdtp 0.0053.
- **PQ1 held** (1.467). **PQ2 refuted** (0.0075). **PQ3 refuted** (0.0061). **PQ4 refuted** (t1 0.0075 against tp 0.0061). **PQ5 held** (best 0.0060). **PQ6 held** (0.0198). **PQ7 refuted** (0.0076). Three of seven.
- Reading: a clear negative for the table, and it locates the oracle's gain. Every table row, nearest or pooled, puts its picks near 72 L/min, because the donors' drag curves do not bend; the one pump whose picks the optimum moves (Danfoss, down to 58.5) has no donor like it, and averaging its steep curve over 72 L/min picks charges it half again the drag it pays. Danfoss alone turns the table from a small gain into a loss (post hoc, without it the three-nearest table scores 0.0051 against owh's 0.0056, half of owdm's margin). Nearest-by-bend is worse than pooling (PQ4 refuted): with one outlier class the "nearest" donor is the most extreme of the rest, and a single donor adds its own shape noise. So the own-pick gain lives in the pumps the optimum sizes down, and a table can only carry it if it has a row for that drag class. That points back to (y), a second bending family, now as the condition for any builder-side form, not only for 7o's tilt. The two-price rule stays the builder's form.
- Limits: as 7p; one bending family; the bend is one two-point ratio; the donors' picks carry their own efficiency shapes; training rows read 7n's own picks.
- Next: (y) a second bending-curve family, which both 7o's tilt and 7q's table now need; (y2) until one exists, a table row built by the model for a synthetic bending curve (Danfoss's bend applied to a donor's level), registered against owh, as a sensitivity rather than a result; (z) a two-feature tilt; (p) the geared AZPN's residual against a measured PTO gearbox map; (a) a multi-unit measured dataset; (e) the journal template and author block (owner).

## 2026-11-18 · Milestone 7p: does the own-pick oracle leak the answer? Own picks read at an efficiency the held-out cell never saw (7o's Next x; predictions written before any 7p number was computed)
- Why: 7o's Next (x). 7n's own-pick read kW<sub>s</sub> beat the two-price rule by a quarter (0.0042 against 0.0056 in (A)), but it reads each variant's picks at the grid E nearest that variant's own central diagonal E<sub>50</sub>, the quantity (A) then predicts. Part of the gain may be that leak, not the drag the pump pays. 7p recomputes the held-out variant's own picks at an efficiency index that does not use its own E<sub>50</sub>, keeping everything else of 7n fixed, and asks how much of the quarter survives.
- Disclosed before the run: no 7p pick, feature, fit or score has been computed or looked at; how far each variant's catalogue index E sits from its E<sub>50</sub> has not been looked at for this registration. 7n's results file must reproduce byte for byte first on the rebuilt caches.
- Method fixed now: `experiments/gear_pump_leak.py`, on 7j to 7o's 22 conditions and 4312 cells unchanged. **Leak-free pick efficiency** for variant k, two versions: (m) e<sub>m</sub>(k) = the grid E nearest the mean central diagonal E<sub>50</sub> of the variants outside k's maker group (what the training families say); (c) e<sub>c</sub>(k) = the grid E nearest k's own catalogue index E (the 7e index a builder reads off the data sheet). **Feature:** kW<sub>s,m</sub> and kW<sub>s,c</sub>, 7n's kW<sub>s</sub> with the picks taken at e<sub>m</sub> or e<sub>c</sub> (weight fixed at the central setting, read on each condition's own idle curve). **Rules:** owh, owdw, owds (7n) as references; owdm and owdc, the owds form, in which the held-out fold's test rows use kW<sub>s,m</sub> or kW<sub>s,c</sub> while the training rows keep 7n's kW<sub>s</sub> (so only the predicted cells lose the leak); owdcc, the owds form with kW<sub>s,c</sub> on every row (a consistent builder-side-efficiency rule, still the model's picks). **Scores** exactly as 7j to 7o: (A) leave-one-condition-and-maker-family-out over 308 diagonals; (B1) nine half-speed conditions held out; (B2) S = 0.20 and 0.50 held out. **Diagnostics:** per variant the own-pick median at E<sub>50</sub>, e<sub>m</sub> and e<sub>c</sub>, and kW<sub>s,m</sub>/kW<sub>s</sub>, kW<sub>s,c</sub>/kW<sub>s</sub> at the central condition.
- Disclosures: the picks still need the full motion model (an oracle on that count, as 7n); the pooled weight w in owdw keeps its BASE families' E<sub>50</sub> (as 7k to 7o).
- **Predictions:**
  - PX1: Danfoss's own-pick median at e<sub>m</sub> is below the pooled 72 L/min (7n at E<sub>50</sub>: 63.5). Prior 60%.
  - PX2: owdm's (A) RMS is at most 0.0049 (at least half the quarter survives). Prior 55%.
  - PX3: owdc's (A) RMS is at most 0.0049. Prior 40%.
  - PX4: owdm and owdc both beat owh's 0.0056 in (A). Prior 60%.
  - PX5: owdcc's (A) RMS is below owh's 0.0056. Prior 50%.
  - PX6: owdm's B1 RMS is at most 0.0056 (owh 0.0059, owds 0.0047). Prior 50%.
### Outcome of 7p (`experiments/gear_pump_leak.py`, `experiments/gear_pump_leak_results.txt`; numpy and h5py; 7n's results file reproduced byte for byte first (13.6 min on 4 cores with the data and cell caches rebuilt from Hugging Face), then 7p in about 3 min on the cache; 4 new tests, `tests/test_gear_pump_leak.py`; manuscript section 3.5, README and `paper/check_numbers.py` updated)
- **Pick efficiencies:** e<sub>m</sub> is 0.8075 to 0.8125 (mean distance from each variant's own E<sub>50</sub> grid point 0.0095); e<sub>c</sub>, the catalogue index, is 0.76 (Casappa) and 0.8475 to 0.925 for the rest (mean distance 0.0914): a pump's own efficiency sits far from its crossing.
- **Own picks at e<sub>m</sub>:** medians within 1.5 L/min of 7n's for 13 of 14 variants; Danfoss's falls further, 63.5 to 58.5 L/min. kW<sub>s,m</sub>/kW<sub>s</sub> at the central condition is 0.950 to 1.047 (Danfoss 1.003). At e<sub>c</sub> the medians rise to 75.5 to 90.5 L/min (Casappa 69.5) and kW<sub>s,c</sub>/kW<sub>s</sub> is 0.833 to 1.392.
- **(A), 308 diagonals:** owh 0.0056, owdw 0.0056, oracle owds 0.0042, **owdm 0.0047** (max per condition 0.0056, bias &minus;0.0001), owdc 0.0135 (bias &minus;0.0100), owdcc 0.0101. **(B1):** owdm 0.0049 (owh 0.0059, owds 0.0047). **(B2):** owdm 0.0047 (owh 0.0057, owds 0.0043).
- **Per variant (A), owdm against owds:** worse on Casappa 0.0024 (0.0012), AZPN and its spun and geared variants by 0.0004 to 0.0017, bench 0.0016 (0.0012); better on Marzocchi 0.0011 (0.0021), AZPU 0.0010 (0.0013), AZPF 0.0022 (0.0025); Danfoss unchanged at 0.0066 (owh 0.0061).
- **PX1 held** (58.5). **PX2 held** (0.0047). **PX3 refuted** (0.0135). **PX4 refuted** (owdc). **PX5 refuted** (owdcc 0.0101). **PX6 held** (0.0049). Three of six.
- Reading: the leak is real but minor. Of the 0.0014 by which 7n's oracle beat the two-price rule in (A), 0.0005 goes when the held-out pump's picks no longer see its own crossing, and 0.0009 stays (0.0047 against 0.0056; at unseen half idle speed 0.0049 against 0.0059). So 7n's reading stands with a smaller number: the one-number form works when the drag is read at the sizes the optimum picks for that pump near its threshold, and a third of 7n's margin was the threshold leaking in. The catalogue-efficiency read fails, and it was a poor registration in hindsight: the picks that decide E<sub>50</sub> are those at the threshold, where the fixed pump only half wins; at the pump's own efficiency (0.09 higher) the optimum picks larger sizes and pays more drag, a systematic bias (&minus;0.0100) no fit trained on threshold picks can absorb. Danfoss stays the one family that the one-number rule prices worse than the two-price rule (0.0066 against 0.0061).
- Limits: as 7n; the leak-free picks still need the full motion model (the oracle on that count remains); e<sub>m</sub> pools variants that share a motion and a model; the weight is fixed at the central setting.
- Next: (x2) a builder-side stand-in for picks at the threshold: picks at e<sub>m</sub> are what a publisher could tabulate per family once (a pick table by drag-curve class), registered against owdm and owh; (y) a second bending-curve family (7o's Next); (z) a two-feature tilt; (p) the geared AZPN's residual against a measured PTO gearbox map; (a) a multi-unit measured dataset; (e) the journal template and author block (owner).

## 2026-11-18 · Milestone 7o: a builder-side stand-in for the own picks. Tilt the published pick weight by the pump's own drag (7n's Next w; predictions written before any 7o number was computed)
- Why: 7n's Next (w). 7n showed the one-number rule beats the two-price rule by a quarter (0.0042 against 0.0056, (A)) once each pump's drag is averaged over the sizes the optimum picks for that pump (kW<sub>s</sub>), but those own picks need the full motion model, an oracle no builder has. A builder does have the published pooled weight w(q) and the pump's own idle curve. If the optimum sizes around drag that is high or steep, a pump's own picks should look like w tilted away from the flows where its drag is above the typical pump's. One published constant could then stand in for the oracle.
- Disclosed before the run: no 7o feature, constant, fit or score has been computed or looked at. 7n's results file must reproduce byte for byte first on the rebuilt cell cache.
- Method fixed now: `experiments/gear_pump_tilt.py`, on 7j to 7n's 22 conditions and 4312 cells unchanged. **Reference drag curve:** P&#772;(q), the mean of the six never-spun families' (7e's BASE) idle curves at the central condition. **Tilted weight:** w&#771;<sub>g</sub>(q; &lambda;) &prop; w(q) exp(&minus;&lambda; (P<sub>idle,g</sub>(q) &minus; P&#772;(q))), central condition, normalised; &lambda; = 0 gives w. **Feature:** kW<sub>t</sub>(g, c) = &Sigma;<sub>q</sub> w&#771;<sub>g</sub>(q) P<sub>idle,g</sub>(q; c) (weight fixed at the central setting, as kW<sub>w</sub> and kW<sub>s</sub>). **Calibration (builder-side):** &lambda; is the value on a grid 0 to 20 kW<sup>&minus;1</sup> in steps of 0.05 that minimises &Sigma; (kW<sub>t</sub> &minus; kW<sub>s</sub>)<sup>2</sup> at the central condition over the BASE families only, which whoever publishes w has already run; the AZPF and AZPN variants never enter it. In (A) and B the held-out maker family is also dropped from the calibration (for the AZPF and AZPN groups all six BASE families are used). **Rules** (one pooled fit each): owh, owdw, owds (7n's oracle) as references; owht = a + b H kW<sub>t</sub> + c k<sub>w</sub> + d H P<sub>LS</sub>; owdt = a + b H (kW<sub>t</sub> &minus; P<sub>LS</sub>) + c k<sub>w</sub>. **Scores** exactly as 7j to 7n: (A) leave-one-condition-and-maker-family-out over 308 diagonals; (B1) nine half-speed conditions held out; (B2) S = 0.20 and 0.50 held out. **Diagnostics:** &lambda; on all six and per left-out family; kW<sub>t</sub>/kW<sub>s</sub> per variant at the central condition; &kappa;<sub>st</sub>, the slope of kW<sub>s</sub> on kW<sub>t</sub> over the 14 variants with condition fixed effects; owdt's (A) RMS over a fixed &lambda; sweep (sensitivity, not scored).
- Disclosures: w itself pools the six BASE families' picks, so (A) for a BASE family still sees its picks through w, as in 7k to 7n; P&#772; includes the held-out family's catalogue curve, which a builder has.
- **Predictions:**
  - PL1: &lambda; fitted on all six BASE families is positive (the tilt leans away from high drag). Prior 80%.
  - PL2: Danfoss's kW<sub>t</sub>/kW<sub>w</sub> at the central condition, with Danfoss left out of the calibration, is below 0.90 (7n's oracle: 0.782). Prior 45%.
  - PL3: &kappa;<sub>st</sub> lies in [0.90, 1.10]. Prior 45%.
  - PL4: owdt's (A) RMS is below owh's 0.0056 (the builder-side one-number rule beats the two-price rule). Prior 50%.
  - PL5: owdt's (A) RMS is at most 0.0049 (at least half of the gap from owh to the oracle owds). Prior 35%.
  - PL6: owdt's B1 and B2 RMS are both at most 0.0056. Prior 35%.
  - PL7: in owht's fit to all 4312 cells, d &lt; 0 and &minus;d/b lies in [0.90, 1.10]. Prior 55%.
### Outcome of 7o (`experiments/gear_pump_tilt.py`, `experiments/gear_pump_tilt_results.txt`; numpy and h5py; 7n's results file reproduced byte for byte first (about 25 min on the rebuilt data and cell caches), then 7o in about 4 min on the cache; 5 new tests, `tests/test_gear_pump_tilt.py`; manuscript section 3.5, README and `paper/check_numbers.py` updated)
- **The constant:** &lambda; = 2.15 per kW on all six BASE families; 2.15 to 2.35 with any one of Casappa, Marzocchi, bench, Rexroth or AZPU left out, but **0.00 with Danfoss left out**. The five other families' own picks give the tilt nothing to fit: only Danfoss's drag bends enough relative to P&#772; for a tilt to move its read, so the calibration is in effect a one-family fit.
- **Per variant at the central condition** (&lambda; without the variant's family): kW<sub>t</sub>/kW<sub>s</sub> is 0.965 to 1.053 for the five plain BASE families other than Danfoss and for AZPF, 1.016 to 1.050 for the spun and geared AZPF, 1.072 for AZPU, 1.093 to 1.104 for the AZPN variants, and **1.278 for Danfoss** (&lambda; = 0, so kW<sub>t</sub> = kW<sub>w</sub>). The tilt moves the plain families' medians up slightly (72 to 74 L/min) where their own picks sit near 72, and pulls the geared AZPF at 98% down to 69 (own picks 68); it cannot lift the AZPN reads, whose own picks sit higher (75.5) and pay less drag than either read. &kappa;<sub>st</sub> = 1.161 (1.092 to 1.209 per condition), against &kappa;<sub>sw</sub> 0.743: the tilt overshoots the across-variant spread rather than closing it.
- **Fits to all 4312 cells:** owht = 0.8219 + 0.0384 H kW<sub>t</sub> &minus; 0.4350 k<sub>w</sub> &minus; 0.0377 H P<sub>LS</sub>, ratio 0.98; owdt = 0.8226 + 0.0378 H (kW<sub>t</sub> &minus; P<sub>LS</sub>) &minus; 0.4350 k<sub>w</sub> (in sample 0.0041 over diagonals; owdw and owh 0.0045, oracle owds 0.0039).
- **(A), 308 diagonals:** owh 0.0056, owdw 0.0056, **owdt 0.0053**, owht 0.0053, oracle owds 0.0042. **(B1):** owdt 0.0057 (owh 0.0059, owds 0.0047). **(B2):** owdt 0.0055 (owh 0.0057, owds 0.0043). A fifth of the gap from the two-price rule to the oracle, with a small negative bias (&minus;0.0008 in (A)).
- **Per variant (A), owdt against owh:** gains on bench 0.0015 (0.0042), Rexroth 0.0010 (0.0026), AZPU 0.0013 (0.0041), Marzocchi 0.0016 (0.0039), AZPF 0.0029 (0.0055), geared AZPN at 98% 0.0132 (0.0145); losses on Danfoss 0.0080 (0.0061), AZPN 0.0032 (0.0016), spun AZPN 0.0034 (0.0022), geared AZPN 0.0057 (0.0048), geared AZPF at 98% 0.0038 (0.0027).
- **Sensitivity (not scored):** one fixed &lambda; in every fold gives (A) 0.0056 at 0, 0.0051 at 0.5, **0.0049 at 1 and 2**, 0.0057 at 4, 0.0096 at 8. With Danfoss's fold given the same tilt as the rest, the builder-side rule reaches the halfway mark PL5 set; the registered calibration denies Danfoss its own lesson, and that costs 0.0004.
- **PL1 held** (2.15). **PL2 refuted** (1.000; &lambda; 0.00 without Danfoss). **PL3 refuted** (&kappa;<sub>st</sub> 1.161). **PL4 held** (0.0053 against 0.0056). **PL5 refuted** (0.0053). **PL6 refuted** (B1 0.0057; B2 0.0055). **PL7 held** (&minus;d/b 0.98). Three of seven.
- Reading: a weak positive. A builder can approach the oracle's one-number rule with the published weight and one constant, and beat the two-price rule slightly in (A) (0.0053 against 0.0056) while keeping the one-for-one trade of standby and drag (0.98), but not at unseen half idle speed (0.0057 against 0.0059 is inside noise of B1's 126 diagonals) and not by the margin the oracle shows. The diagnosis sharpens 7n's: the optimum's sizing-around-drag is real but expressed by one family in this set; the other five families' own picks differ from the pooled weight by things a drag tilt does not see (efficiency shape and, for AZPN, picks above the pooled median). A drag-only stand-in for the own picks therefore cannot be calibrated out of sample on six families of which one bends. The practical recommendation stays the two-price rule on kW<sub>o</sub> and P<sub>LS</sub>; the tilt is a candidate once a second bending family exists to calibrate it without leakage.
- Limits: as 7n; the tilt uses the central condition's curves only; the calibration objective matches kW<sub>s</sub> at the central condition rather than the picks' distribution; the &lambda; sweep is post hoc and not scored; one machine's motion.
- Next: (y) add a second bending-curve family (data-sheet search: a gear pump with a measured idle or low-pressure torque curve that bends, e.g. Marzocchi GHP or Casappa Polaris at several speeds) so the tilt can be calibrated out of sample; (z) a two-feature tilt, drag and the efficiency shape together, calibrated the same way; (x) the leak: own picks recomputed with the held-out condition excluded, or from a second motion record; 7i's (r); (p) the geared AZPN's residual against a measured PTO gearbox map; (a) a multi-unit measured dataset; (e) the journal template and author block (owner).


## 2026-11-18 · Milestone 7n: each pump's drag at its own picks. Is the one-number rule's failure a read problem or a form problem? (7m's Next v; predictions written before any 7n number was computed)
- Why: 7m's Next (v). 7l and 7m found that builder-side reads of the drag (one flow, two flows) land within 2% of the pick-weighted drag kW<sub>w</sub> for 13 of 14 variants, yet the one-number rule does not reach the two-price rule's 0.0056, because Danfoss, whose idle curve bends, sets the across-variant slope. But kW<sub>w</sub> itself uses the pooled pick weight w(q) of six families, not the sizes the optimum picks for a given pump. If Danfoss's steep drag makes the optimum pick smaller sizes for it, the drag it really pays is lower than kW<sub>w</sub> says. 7n reads each variant's drag at its own picks (an oracle no builder has) to bound the one-number form: if even that read does not beat the two-price rule, the failure lies in the form, not in any read.
- Disclosed before the run: the own-pick weight has not been computed for any variant; 7e's per-family pick medians (from `gear_pump_chosen_results.txt`) were not reread for this registration. 7m's results file must reproduce byte for byte first on the rebuilt cell cache. No 7n feature, fit, ratio or score has been computed or looked at.
- Method fixed now: `experiments/gear_pump_ownpick.py`, on 7j to 7m's 22 conditions and 4312 cells unchanged. **Own pick weight:** w<sub>g</sub>(q), the share of the 384 geometries whose chosen size (7e's `picks`, argmax of f<sub>t</sub>) is q on the diagonal cell (g, g) at the central setting, at the grid E nearest that cell's E<sub>50</sub>, for each of the 14 variants (as 7e did for the six never-spun families). **Feature:** kW<sub>s</sub>(g, c) = &Sigma;<sub>q</sub> w<sub>g</sub>(q) P<sub>idle,g</sub>(q; c), on each condition's own idle curve (the weight fixed at the central setting, as kW<sub>w</sub>'s). **Rules** (one pooled fit each): owh, owdw, owd2 as references; owhs = a + b H kW<sub>s</sub> + c k<sub>w</sub> + d H P<sub>LS</sub>; owds = a + b H (kW<sub>s</sub> &minus; P<sub>LS</sub>) + c k<sub>w</sub>. **Scores** exactly as 7j to 7m: (A) leave-one-condition-and-maker-family-out over 308 diagonals; (B1) nine half-speed conditions held out; (B2) S = 0.20 and 0.50 held out. **Diagnostics:** per variant the own picks' median and kW<sub>s</sub>/kW<sub>w</sub> at the central condition; &kappa;<sub>s2</sub>, the slope of kW<sub>s</sub> on kW<sub>2</sub> over the 14 variants pooled with condition fixed effects.
- **Predictions:**
  - PV1: Danfoss's own-pick median is below the pooled median q* = 72 L/min. Prior 65%.
  - PV2: Danfoss's kW<sub>s</sub>/kW<sub>w</sub> at the central condition is below 0.97. Prior 55%.
  - PV3: &kappa;<sub>s2</sub> is below 7m's &kappa;<sub>2</sub> = 0.859 (own picks widen the gap a read inside the pooled picks leaves). Prior 50%.
  - PV4: owds's (A) RMS is at most 0.0056 (owdw's and owh's). Prior 45%.
  - PV5: Danfoss's per-variant (A) RMS under owds is below its 0.0080 under owdw. Prior 50%.
  - PV6: in owhs's fit to all 4312 cells, d &lt; 0 and &minus;d/b lies in [0.90, 1.10]. Prior 55%.
  - PV7: owds's B1 and B2 RMS are both at most 0.0062. Prior 40%.
### Outcome of 7n (`experiments/gear_pump_ownpick.py`, `experiments/gear_pump_ownpick_results.txt`; numpy and h5py; 7m's results file reproduced byte for byte first (16 min on 4 cores with the data and cell caches rebuilt from Hugging Face), then 7n in 157 s on the cache; 4 new tests, `tests/test_gear_pump_ownpick.py`; manuscript section 3.5, README and `paper/check_numbers.py` updated)
- **Own picks:** for 13 of 14 variants the median own pick is 68 to 75.5 L/min, close to the pooled 72. Danfoss's own picks are spread and split: p25 57, median 63.5, p75 90.5 L/min (mean 73.5). The four AZPN variants' lower quartile drops to 55 to 56.5 L/min, and the geared AZPF at 98% to 58.5: the optimum avoids sizes above the family's largest where the drive extra is paid (share of own picks above Q<sub>L</sub>: AZPN 0.40, spun 0.34, geared 0.04, geared 98% 0.00).
- **The drag each pump pays at its own picks (central condition):** kW<sub>s</sub>/kW<sub>w</sub> is 0.95 to 1.01 for the eight plain and AZPF-spun/geared variants, 0.782 for Danfoss, 0.852 to 0.950 for the AZPN variants and 0.869 for the geared AZPF at 98%. The pooled weight overstates what the optimum pays exactly where the drag is high or steep, because the optimum then picks around it. Across variants kW<sub>s</sub> carries 0.743 kW per kW of kW<sub>w</sub> (&kappa;<sub>sw</sub>, condition fixed effects) and 0.604 kW per kW of the two-point read kW<sub>2</sub> (&kappa;<sub>s2</sub>; &kappa;<sub>w2</sub> 0.859 as in 7m).
- **Fits to all 4312 cells:** owhs = 0.8231 + 0.0363 H kW<sub>s</sub> &minus; 0.4350 k<sub>w</sub> &minus; 0.0355 H P<sub>LS</sub>, ratio 0.98; owds = 0.8240 + 0.0356 H (kW<sub>s</sub> &minus; P<sub>LS</sub>) &minus; 0.4350 k<sub>w</sub> (in sample 0.0039 over diagonals; owh and owdw 0.0045).
- **(A), 308 diagonals:** owh 0.0056, owdw 0.0056, owd2 0.0066, **owhs 0.0042, owds 0.0042** (max per condition 0.0052). **(B1):** owds 0.0047 (owh 0.0059). **(B2):** owds 0.0043 (owh 0.0057).
- **Per variant (A), owds against owh:** Casappa 0.0012 (0.0031), bench 0.0012 (0.0042), Rexroth 0.0010 (0.0026), AZPU 0.0013 (0.0041), AZPF 0.0025 (0.0055), geared AZPN at 98% 0.0110 (0.0145), Danfoss 0.0067 (0.0061). Worse than owh on the geared AZPF at 98% (0.0041 against 0.0027), AZPN (0.0022 against 0.0016) and Danfoss.
- **PV1 to PV7 all held** (Danfoss median 63.5; kW<sub>s</sub>/kW<sub>w</sub> 0.782; &kappa;<sub>s2</sub> 0.604; owds (A) 0.0042; Danfoss 0.0067 against 0.0080; &minus;d/b 0.98; B1 0.0047, B2 0.0043). Seven of seven.
- Reading: the one-number form was never the problem; the read was. With each pump's drag averaged over the sizes the optimum picks for that pump, the rule E<sub>50</sub> &asymp; 0.824 + 0.036 H (kW<sub>s</sub> &minus; P<sub>LS</sub>) &minus; 0.435 k<sub>w</sub> beats the two-price rule by a quarter out of sample (0.0042 against 0.0056 over 308 diagonals, 0.0047 against 0.0059 at unseen half speed), and standby and drag trade one for one (0.98). 7l and 7m failed because every read of a fixed set of flows, pooled picks included, overstates the drag of a pump whose drag is high or steep at those flows: the optimum sizes such a pump down, so the drag it pays is lower. That is why the slope of any fixed read on the drag paid is below one and why Danfoss set it.
- **What this does not show (an oracle bound, as registered):** kW<sub>s</sub> uses the diagonal cell's own picks at the central setting, computed with the same model and motion that give E<sub>50</sub>, including for the held-out family in (A). No builder has it, and part of the gain may be that leak. It bounds what a read can do; it is not a builder's rule. The practical recommendation stays the two-price rule on kW<sub>o</sub> and P<sub>LS</sub>.
- Limits: as 7m; own picks only at the central setting (the weight is not recomputed per condition); one machine's motion; the geared AZPN at 98% stays the worst variant (0.0110).
- Next: (w) a builder-side approximation of the own picks without running the model: pick each geometry's size by the drag's local slope (the size where the marginal drag of a larger pump equals a fixed fuel credit), or shift the pooled weight down by the drag's slope over the picks, registered against owds and owh; (x) the leak: recompute the own picks with the held-out condition excluded, or from a second motion record; 7i's (r), S varied jointly with idle speed; (p) the geared AZPN's residual against a measured PTO gearbox map; (g) a family with drag above 4 kW or below 2 kW, ideally with a bending idle curve; (a) a multi-unit measured dataset; (e) the journal template and author block (owner).

## 2026-11-18 · Milestone 7m: a two-point builder-side read of the drag for the one-number rule (7l's Next u; predictions written before any 7m number was computed)
- Why: 7l's Next (u). 7l found one catalogue read at the picks' median (72 L/min) misses two things the pick-weighted drag kW<sub>w</sub> carries: the bend of a curved idle curve (Danfoss) and a spun or geared drive's extra, which sits only above the family's largest size. Two reads can address each: the mean of the drag at the pick weight's lower and upper quartile flows (a trapezoid over the middle half of the picks, which sees curvature and, if the upper quartile is above the largest size, part of the drive extra), or the 72 L/min read plus the drive extra kW<sub>o</sub> already carries (the catalogue read plus the one term 7g added for drive losses). If either matches kW<sub>w</sub>, a builder can use the one-number rule from two data-sheet numbers.
- Disclosed before the run: q<sub>25</sub> and q<sub>75</sub> are fixed now as the smallest sizes whose cumulative central pick weight reaches 0.25 and 0.75 (computed by the script from w, an input; their values have not been looked at). 7l's results file must reproduce byte for byte first on the rebuilt cell cache. No 7m feature, fit, ratio or score has been computed or looked at.
- Method fixed now: `experiments/gear_pump_twopoint.py`, on 7j to 7l's 22 conditions and 4312 cells unchanged. **Features:** kW<sub>2</sub>(g, c) = &frac12; (P<sub>idle,g</sub>(q<sub>25</sub>; c) + P<sub>idle,g</sub>(q<sub>75</sub>; c)); kW<sub>72x</sub>(g, c) = P<sub>idle,g</sub>(q*; c) + &Sigma;<sub>q &gt; Q<sub>L</sub></sub> w(q) (P<sub>idle,g</sub>(q; c) &minus; P<sub>idle,h</sub>(q; c)), h the family's catalogue reading and Q<sub>L</sub> its largest size (kW<sub>o</sub>'s extra term, `held_plus_extra`, with the 85 L/min read replaced by q*). **Rules** (one pooled fit each): owh, owdw, owdq as references; owh2 = a + b H kW<sub>2</sub> + c k<sub>w</sub> + d H P<sub>LS</sub>; owd2 = a + b H (kW<sub>2</sub> &minus; P<sub>LS</sub>) + c k<sub>w</sub>; owdx = a + b H (kW<sub>72x</sub> &minus; P<sub>LS</sub>) + c k<sub>w</sub>. **Scores** exactly as 7j to 7l: (A) leave-one-condition-and-maker-family-out over 308 diagonals; (B1) nine half-speed conditions held out; (B2) S = 0.20 and 0.50 held out. **Diagnostic:** &kappa;<sub>2</sub> and &kappa;<sub>x</sub>, the slopes of kW<sub>w</sub> on kW<sub>2</sub> and on kW<sub>72x</sub> over the 14 variants, pooled with condition fixed effects.
- **Predictions:**
  - PT1: pooled &kappa;<sub>2</sub> lies in [0.95, 1.05]. Prior 50%.
  - PT2: in owh2's fit to all 4312 cells, d &lt; 0 and &minus;d/b lies in [0.90, 1.10]. Prior 60%.
  - PT3: owd2's (A) RMS is at most 0.0062 (within 10% of owdw's 0.0056). Prior 50%.
  - PT4: owdx's (A) RMS is at most 0.0062. Prior 45%.
  - PT5: the better of owd2 and owdx has (A) RMS below owdq's 0.0065. Prior 70%.
  - PT6: Danfoss's per-variant (A) RMS under owd2 is below its 0.0142 under owdq. Prior 65%.
  - PT7: owd2's B1 and B2 RMS are both at most 0.0070. Prior 45%.
### Outcome of 7m (`experiments/gear_pump_twopoint.py`, `experiments/gear_pump_twopoint_results.txt`; numpy and h5py; 7l's results file reproduced byte for byte first (about 25 min on 4 cores with the data and cell caches rebuilt from Hugging Face), then 7m in about 3 min on the cache; 4 new tests, `tests/test_gear_pump_twopoint.py`; manuscript section 3.5, README and `paper/check_numbers.py` updated)
- **Quartiles:** q<sub>25</sub> = 64, q* = 72, q<sub>75</sub> = 79 L/min: the middle half of the picks spans only 15 L/min.
- **The two-point mean against the pick-weighted drag (central setting):** kW<sub>w</sub>/kW<sub>2</sub> is 0.996 to 1.019 for 13 of 14 variants and 0.937 for Danfoss. Per variant the trapezoid is close, but the slope of kW<sub>w</sub> on kW<sub>2</sub> across variants is 0.859 pooled (0.838 to 0.872 per condition): Danfoss carries the highest read and a lower kW<sub>w</sub>, and it sets the spread. The four AZPN reads now differ (2.025 to 2.218 kW), since q<sub>75</sub> = 79 lies above the family's largest size.
- **kW<sub>72x</sub>, as registered, double-counts the AZPF drive extra.** For the AZPF family q* = 72 L/min lies above its largest size (58.9), so the variant's own read at q* already includes the spin or step-up drag and the extra term adds it again (kW<sub>w</sub>/kW<sub>72x</sub> 0.839 for the geared AZPF at 98%; &kappa;<sub>x</sub> 0.721). The registered definition is scored as written; the fix is reported below as post hoc.
- **Fits to all 4312 cells:** owh2 = 0.8218 + 0.0325 H kW<sub>2</sub> &minus; 0.4350 k<sub>w</sub> &minus; 0.0339 H P<sub>LS</sub>, ratio 1.04; owd2 = 0.8201 + 0.0335 H (kW<sub>2</sub> &minus; P<sub>LS</sub>) &minus; 0.4350 k<sub>w</sub> (in sample 0.0050 over diagonals; owdw 0.0045).
- **(A), 308 diagonals:** owh 0.0056, owdw 0.0056, owdq 0.0065, **owd2 0.0066**, owh2 0.0067, owdx 0.0070. **(B1):** owd2 0.0074 (owh 0.0059). **(B2):** owd2 0.0070 (owh 0.0057).
- **Per variant (A):** owd2 recovers Casappa (0.0072 under owdq to 0.0041; owdw 0.0036) and trims Danfoss (0.0142 to 0.0126; owdw 0.0080), but loses a little on the AZPF variants and the bench pump, so the total does not move. owdx's double count helps the spun and geared AZPF at 100% (0.0030, 0.0027) and costs the one at 98% (0.0100).
- **Post hoc, not registered:** kW<sub>72h</sub>, the catalogue curve read at q* plus the drive extra (kW<sub>o</sub>'s construction with 85 replaced by 72 L/min): &kappa;<sub>h</sub> 0.845, (A) 0.0071, B1 0.0080, B2 0.0077, Danfoss 0.0140. Removing the double count makes the read more faithful and the score worse, so the drive extra was not what kept owdq above owdw.
- **PT1 refuted** (&kappa;<sub>2</sub> 0.859). **PT2 held** (&minus;d/b 1.04). **PT3 refuted** (0.0066). **PT4 refuted** (0.0070). **PT5 refuted** (owd2 0.0066 against owdq 0.0065). **PT6 held** (Danfoss 0.0126 against 0.0142). **PT7 refuted** (B1 0.0074; B2 0.0070 at the bar). Two of seven.
- Reading: a second negative result for the builder-side one-number rule, and a sharper diagnosis than 7l's. With two reads every variant but one lands within 2% of its pick-weighted drag, yet the out-of-sample score does not improve on one read. What the one-number rule needs is not each pump's drag to 2% but the across-variant slope of the read on kW<sub>w</sub> to be one, and that slope is set by the one family whose curve bends (Danfoss), where any read inside the picks overstates what the optimum pays. The one-number form leaves no free coefficient to absorb that scale; the two-price rule does (its drag slope is fitted separately from the standby slope), which is why it stays at 0.0056 on the plain 85 L/min read. The practical recommendation does not change: price idle with two numbers, drag kW<sub>o</sub> and standby P<sub>LS</sub>, each per idle hour.
- Limits: as 7l; the quartiles come from the central setting's pick weight; one bending family drives the result, so a second bending family would test whether it is a Danfoss quirk; one machine's motion.
- Next: (v) why Danfoss's drag at the picks is lower than any read inside them: the pick weight per family (Danfoss's own picks, not the pooled w) against its curve; 7i's (r), S varied jointly with idle speed; (p) the geared AZPN's residual against a measured PTO gearbox map; (g) a family with drag above 4 kW or below 2 kW, ideally with a bending idle curve; (a) a multi-unit measured dataset; (e) the journal template and author block (owner).

## 2026-11-18 · Milestone 7l: a builder-side read of the drag for the one-number rule. The drag at one flow, the picks' median, in place of 7k's pick-weighted kW<sub>w</sub> (7k's Next t; predictions written before any 7l number was computed)

A builder can read a data sheet at one size. If the idle drag curves are close to linear over the picked range, the drag at the weight's median flow should carry the same information as kW<sub>w</sub> (slope near 1), and the one-number rule should survive with a single catalogue read.

  - PX1: pooled &kappa;<sub>q</sub> lies in [0.95, 1.05]. Prior 60%.
  - PX2: in owhq's fit to all 4312 cells, d &lt; 0 and &minus;d/b lies in [0.90, 1.10]. Prior 55%.
  - PX3: owdq's (A) RMS is at most 0.0062 (within 10% of owdw's 0.0056). Prior 55%.
