### Outcome of 7l (`experiments/gear_pump_onepoint.py`, `experiments/gear_pump_onepoint_results.txt`; numpy and h5py; 7k's results file reproduced byte for byte first (about 25 min on 4 cores with the data and cell caches rebuilt from Hugging Face), then 7l in 160 s on the cache; 3 new tests, `tests/test_gear_pump_onepoint.py`; manuscript section 3.5, README and `paper/check_numbers.py` updated)
- **q\*:** the w-weighted median of the central picks is 72 L/min (mean 72.3; 106 sizes from 51.5 to 108.5 L/min).
- **The drag at 72 L/min against the pick-weighted drag (central setting):** kW<sub>w</sub>/kW<sub>72</sub> runs 0.918 (Danfoss) to 1.073 (geared AZPN at 98%); ten of 14 variants lie within 0.02 of 1. Two things break the one-point read. Danfoss's idle curve bends, so its drag at 72 L/min stands for more than its average over the picks. And the four AZPN variants read the same 2.060 kW at 72 L/min: the spin and step-up drive extras sit only above the family's largest size (75.6 L/min, 7g; 34% of the picks lie above it), so a read at 72 L/min cannot see them, while kW<sub>w</sub> charges them on the picks above it. The AZPF variants, whose largest size is 58.9 L/min, do differ at 72 L/min. The pooled slope of kW<sub>w</sub> on kW<sub>72</sub> is 0.818 (0.803 to 0.831 per condition), not 1.
- **Fits to all 4312 cells:** owhq = 0.8225 + 0.0315 H kW<sub>72</sub> &minus; 0.4350 k<sub>w</sub> &minus; 0.0333 H P<sub>LS</sub>, ratio 1.06 (owh 1.22, owhw 1.02); owdq = 0.8203 + 0.0327 H (kW<sub>72</sub> &minus; P<sub>LS</sub>) &minus; 0.4350 k<sub>w</sub>; in sample 0.0048 over the diagonals against 0.0045 for owh and owdw.
- **(A) leave-one-condition-and-maker-family-out, 308 diagonals:** owh 0.0056, owdw 0.0056, **owdq 0.0065** (max 0.0085), owhq 0.0066, owd 0.0068. **(B1) half speed unseen:** owdq 0.0076 (owh 0.0059). **(B2) S = 0.20 and 0.50 unseen:** owdq 0.0070 (owh 0.0057).
- **Per variant (A):** owdq doubles Danfoss's error (0.0080 under owdw, 0.0142) and Casappa's (0.0036 to 0.0072), helps the geared AZPN at 98% a little (0.0151 to 0.0129) and is within 0.001 of owdw elsewhere.
- **Sensitivity (A RMS of owdq by read flow):** 60 L/min 0.0063, 65 0.0059, 72 0.0065, 80 0.0096, 85 0.0081. Not monotone, and the best read (65) was found by the sweep, so it is a post hoc choice and not a result: no single flow matches the pick weight's 0.0056.
- **PX1 refuted** (&kappa;<sub>q</sub> 0.818). **PX2 held** (&minus;d/b 1.06). **PX3 refuted** (0.0065 &gt; 0.0062). **PX4 held** (0.0065 &lt; 0.0068, barely). **PX5 refuted** (B1 0.0076; B2 0.0070 at the bar). **PX6 refuted** (Danfoss 0.0142). **PX7 held** (best 65, 7 L/min from q*). Three of seven.
- Reading: a negative result for the builder-side shortcut. Read at one flow, the drag still trades with standby near one for one (1.06), confirming 7k's explanation that the 1.22 was a size mismatch; but one point on the curve is not enough to make the one-number rule as good as 7j's two-price rule on kW<sub>o</sub>. It loses about 16% of out-of-sample accuracy overall and twice that on a family whose idle curve bends. The two-price rule on kW<sub>o</sub> (drag at 85 L/min on the catalogue curve plus the drive's extra above the largest size) stays the practical form for a builder; the one-number form needs the pick distribution or something that carries it.
- Limits: as 7k; q* comes from the central setting's pick weight; the sweep has five points; one machine's motion.
- Next: (u) a two-point read (one below and one above the family's largest size, or the drag at 72 L/min plus kW<sub>o</sub>'s drive extra) registered against owdw and owh; 7i's (r), S varied jointly with idle speed; (p) the geared AZPN's residual against a measured PTO gearbox map; (g) a family with drag above 4 kW or below 2 kW; (a) a multi-unit measured dataset; (e) the journal template and author block (owner).

## 2026-11-17 · Milestone 7k: where the 1.22 comes from. The idle-hour rule with the drag read at the optimum's picks (kW<sub>w</sub>) in place of kW<sub>o</sub>, and the one-price-difference rule retried on it (7j's Next s; predictions written before any 7k number was computed)
- Why: 7j's Next (s). 7j's one rule prices a kW of load-sensing standby at 1.22 kW of gear-pump drag (7i: 1.27), yet the break-even model charges idle as b (P<sub>LS</sub> &minus; P<sub>OC</sub>(q)) h, so a uniform kW added to either side moves every crossing by exactly the same amount with opposite sign (in `fcurve` the two enter only as the difference; checked numerically below, not a prediction). The ratio must then come from the feature: kW<sub>o</sub> reads the drag at 85 L/min (plus spin/drive extras), while the cells pay it at each geometry's picked size, centred near 72 L/min, where 7f found kW<sub>w</sub>/kW<sub>85</sub> = 0.74 to 0.87. If across the 14 drag variants a kW of kW<sub>o</sub> carries about 0.82 kW of drag at the picks, the fitted price ratio should be about 1/0.82 = 1.22, and with kW<sub>w</sub> in the idle term it should go to 1. If it does, the one-number rule 7j refuted (SI6, owd +22%) should come back: a builder could price idle by a single difference, drag at the picks minus standby, per idle hour.
- Disclosed before the run: the explanation was formed after seeing 7j's 1.22 and recalling 7f's kW<sub>w</sub>/kW<sub>85</sub> range at the central setting; kW<sub>w</sub> has not been computed at any other condition, and no 7k fit, ratio or score has been computed or looked at. 7f found kW<sub>w</sub> a worse read than kW<sub>85</sub> on the held catalogue panel, so a better ratio need not mean a better score. 7j's cells (4312) are rebuilt and its results file must reproduce byte for byte first.
- Method fixed now: `experiments/gear_pump_ratio.py`, on 7j's 22 conditions and cells unchanged. **Feature:** kW<sub>w</sub>(g, c) = &Sigma;<sub>q</sub> w(q) P<sub>idle,g</sub>(q; c), 7f's pick-weighted idle power, read from each condition's own idle curve with 7e's central pick weight w(q) (as 7j's kW<sub>o</sub>). **Rules** (one pooled fit each): owh and owd (7j's, references); owhw = a + b H kW<sub>w</sub> + c k<sub>w</sub> + d H P<sub>LS</sub>; owdw = a + b H (kW<sub>w</sub> &minus; P<sub>LS</sub>) + c k<sub>w</sub>; H = S/(1 &minus; S). **Scores** exactly as 7j: (A) leave-one-condition-and-maker-family-out over 308 diagonals; (B1) the nine half-speed conditions held out; (B2) S = 0.20 and 0.50 held out. **Diagnostic:** &kappa;, the least-squares slope of kW<sub>w</sub> on kW<sub>o</sub> over the 14 variants, per condition and pooled over all 308 (variant, condition) pairs with a condition fixed effect. **Check:** at the central condition, each of the 14 diagonals rerun with +0.5 kW added to P<sub>LS</sub> and, separately, to the whole P<sub>OC</sub>(q) curve: the two E<sub>50</sub> shifts must be equal and opposite to 1e-9.
- **Predictions:**
  - RW1: in owhw's fit to all 4312 cells, d &lt; 0 and &minus;d/b lies in [0.90, 1.10]. Prior 50%.
  - RW2: owhw's &minus;d/b is closer to 1 than owh's 1.22. Prior 80%.
  - RW3: the pooled &kappa; lies within 0.10 of 1/1.22 = 0.82 (the feature's scale explains the ratio). Prior 60%.
  - RW4: owdw's (A) RMS is within 10% of owhw's (the SI6 bar, which owd missed by +22%). Prior 50%.
  - RW5: owdw's (A) RMS is below owd's 0.0068. Prior 70%.
  - RW6: owhw's (A) RMS is at most 0.0062 (within 10% of owh's 0.0056). Prior 55%.
### Outcome of 7k (`experiments/gear_pump_ratio.py`, `experiments/gear_pump_ratio_results.txt`; numpy and h5py; 7j's results file reproduced byte for byte first (14 min on 4 cores with the cell cache rebuilt from Hugging Face), then 7k in about 160 s on its cache; 4 new tests, `tests/test_gear_pump_ratio.py`; manuscript section 3.5, README and `paper/check_numbers.py` updated)
- **Check as registered: not met, and mis-specified.** +0.5 kW on P<sub>LS</sub> moves the 14 central diagonals by &minus;0.0120 to &minus;0.0102; +0.5 kW on the whole P<sub>OC</sub>(q) curve by +0.0100 to +0.0128; the largest |sum| is 2.3e-3, not 1e-9. The registered bar was wrong: two opposite shifts of the idle charge are equal and opposite only to first order, because the crossing is a nonlinear function of the charge. What the model guarantees, and what the reasoning needed, is that standby and drag enter only as their difference: post hoc, +0.5 kW on both at once moves no diagonal (largest change 0.0e+00), and a unit test checks the same identity on `fcurve`.
- **The drag at the picks:** at the central setting kW<sub>w</sub>/kW<sub>o</sub> runs 0.786 (Casappa) to 0.867 (Danfoss). The slope of kW<sub>w</sub> on kW<sub>o</sub> across the 14 variants is 0.880 pooled (condition fixed effects), 0.868 to 0.890 per condition: a kW of kW<sub>o</sub> carries 0.88 kW of the drag the optimum pays.
- **Fits to all 4312 cells:** owh (7j) E<sub>50</sub> = 0.8214 + 0.0329 H kW<sub>o</sub> &minus; 0.4350 k<sub>w</sub> &minus; 0.0402 H P<sub>LS</sub>, ratio 1.22; **owhw = 0.8217 + 0.0353 H kW<sub>w</sub> &minus; 0.4350 k<sub>w</sub> &minus; 0.0360 H P<sub>LS</sub>, ratio 1.02**; **owdw (one number) = 0.8208 + 0.0359 H (kW<sub>w</sub> &minus; P<sub>LS</sub>) &minus; 0.4350 k<sub>w</sub>**. - Reading: the 1.22 was a units problem, not physics. The model can only trade standby and drag kW one for one; kW<sub>o</sub> measures the drag at the wrong size, so its slope absorbs the conversion. Read at the picks, the price ratio is 1.02 and 7j's refuted one-number form comes back at no cost: E<sub>50</sub> &asymp; 0.821 + 0.036 H (kW<sub>w</sub> &minus; P<sub>LS</sub>) &minus; 0.435 k<sub>w</sub>, the idle drag a builder's pump has over the load-sensing pump's standby, per idle hour, plus the shape. &kappa; alone (1/0.88 = 1.14) accounts for about two-thirds of the 0.22 excess; RW3's 0.10 band was wide enough to hold either way, so it is weak evidence. The rest presumably comes from kW<sub>w</sub>'s different spread across variants (its correlation with k<sub>w</sub>), not tested here. kW<sub>w</sub> needs the pick weight w(q), which a builder does not have; 7f's held-panel cost reappears per variant (Danfoss), so for a single catalogue pump the two-price rule on kW<sub>o</sub> stays the practical form and the one-number form is the explanation.
- Limits: as 7j; w(q) is the central setting's, pooled over six families; the check was mis-registered (disclosed); one machine's motion.
- Next: (t) a builder-side proxy for kW<sub>w</sub>: drag read at one flow (the picks' median, about 72 L/min) in the one-number rule, registered against owdw and owh; 7i's (r), S varied jointly with idle speed; (p) the geared AZPN's residual against a measured PTO gearbox map; (g) a family with drag above 4 kW or below 2 kW; (a) a multi-unit measured dataset; (e) the journal template and author block (owner).

## 2026-11-17 · Milestone 7j: an independent replication of 7i (registered as "7i" by a parallel run before #16997 landed), with the idle hours S/(1 &minus; S) in place of S, the idle shares held out, and a one-number price difference (predictions written before any number was computed)
- Why: 7h found the rule's two slopes transfer across idle settings but its intercept moves by up to 0.035, because the setting changes the load-sensing pump's standby power, which the rule does not carry, and the kW<sub>o</sub> slope scales with the idle share. A builder should not have to rerun the break-even model to get one number. The break-even model itself (`fcurve`) charges idle as b (P<sub>LS</sub> &minus; P<sub>OC</sub>) h, with h = S(1 &minus; s<sub>0</sub>)/(1 &minus; S) idle hours per working hour, so the competitor's standby kW and the fixed pump's drag should enter the rule multiplied by the idle hours, with opposite signs.
- Disclosed before the run: the form (idle power times H = S/(1 &minus; S)) was chosen after seeing 7h's per-condition slopes (kW<sub>o</sub> slope +0.0087 to +0.0275 over S 0.20 to 0.50, roughly proportional to H) and intercept shifts (+0.03 at half idle speed); H drops the record's own still share s<sub>0</sub>, which varies by geometry. No pooled fit, cross-condition score or coefficient below has been computed or looked at. 7h's conditions, cells and features are rebuilt unchanged.
- Method fixed now: `experiments/gear_pump_onerule.py`. The 22 conditions of 7h &times; 196 cells (4312 cells). Per cell (f shape, g drag) in condition c: kW<sub>o</sub>(g, c) and k<sub>w</sub>(f) as 7h, P<sub>LS</sub>(c) the load-sensing standby kW, H(c) = S/(1 &minus; S). **Rules**, each fitted once on pooled cells: ow = a + b kW<sub>o</sub> + c k<sub>w</sub> (7g's, setting-blind); owl = ow + d P<sub>LS</sub> (share-blind); owh = a + b H kW<sub>o</sub> + c k<sub>w</sub> + d H P<sub>LS</sub> (idle-hour prices); owd = a + b H (kW<sub>o</sub> &minus; P<sub>LS</sub>) + c k<sub>w</sub> (one price-difference number). **Scores:** (A) leave-one-condition-and-maker-family-out: for each held condition and each of the eight maker groups, fit on the other 21 conditions' cells with neither variant in the group, predict that group's diagonals in the held condition; RMS over the 22 &times; 14 = 308 diagonals. (B1) the nine half-idle-speed conditions held out together (fit on the 13 working-speed conditions), also leave-one-maker-family-out; (B2) S = 0.20 and 0.50 held out together (fit on the other 20). References: 7h's per-condition refitted ow (median 0.0056) and frozen slopes with own intercept (median 0.0051), both of which use the held condition's own cells. Check: per-condition refitted ow reproduces 7h's (i) in all 22 conditions.
- **Predictions:**
  - SI1: owh's score A RMS is at most 0.0070 (1.25 &times; 7h's per-condition median, with no cell of the held condition used). Prior 50%.
  - SI2: owh's score A RMS is at least 50% below setting-blind ow's. Prior 75%.
  - SI3: owh's score A RMS is below owl's. Prior 70%.
  - SI4: in owh's fit to all 4312 cells, d &lt; 0 and &minus;d/b lies in [0.5, 2] (standby kW and drag kW trade near one for one, as the model's idle charge says). Prior 60%.
  - SI5: owh's B1 RMS (nine half-speed conditions, never seen in training) is at most 0.010. Prior 35%.
  - SI6: owd's score A RMS is within 10% of owh's. Prior 45%.
  - SI7: owh's B2 RMS (S = 0.20 and 0.50, never seen) is at most 0.010. Prior 55%.

### Outcome of 7j (`experiments/gear_pump_onerule.py`, `experiments/gear_pump_onerule_results.txt`; numpy and h5py, about 25 min on 4 cores with the data cache rebuilt, cells cached so a rerun only refits; 3 new tests, `tests/test_gear_pump_onerule.py`; manuscript section 3.5 and README updated)
- Check: each condition's own refitted ow reproduces 7h's (i) in all 22 conditions (max difference 0.0000).
- **Fitted once on all 4312 cells:** E<sub>50</sub> = 0.8214 + 0.0329 H kW<sub>o</sub> &minus; 0.4350 k<sub>w</sub> &minus; 0.0402 H P<sub>LS</sub>, H = S/(1 &minus; S) (in-sample RMS 0.0043 over cells, 0.0045 over the 308 diagonals). At the central share (H = 0.613) the drag slope is +0.0202 per kW, near 7g's +0.0182. A kW of load-sensing standby is worth 1.22 kW of gear-pump drag, the opposite sign, as the model's idle charge b (P<sub>LS</sub> &minus; P<sub>OC</sub>) h says. The setting-blind ow fitted the same way is 0.0147 in sample; the k<sub>w</sub> coefficient is the same in every rule because the shape varies only by row of a balanced design.
- **(A) leave-one-condition-and-maker-family-out, 308 diagonals:** ow 0.0163, owl 0.0065, **owh 0.0056**, owd 0.0068. owh's worst condition is 0.0064 (S = 0.50) and its bias +0.0000. That equals the median of 7h's per-condition refits (0.0056), which used the held condition's own cells: one fit with two extra numbers (the idle share and the standby kW) costs nothing against refitting at every setting. owl (standby kW alone) matches owh at S = 0.38 but misses S = 0.20 and 0.50 by 0.0117 and 0.0111, the share's part.
- **(B1) the nine half-speed conditions never seen in training** (fit on 13 working-speed conditions, where standby kW runs only 2.89 to 3.06): owh 0.0059 (bias +0.0002), owl 0.0062, owd 0.0083, ow 0.0366 (bias +0.0361). The rule extrapolates the standby kW from 2.9 to 1.4 kW without loss. **(B2) S = 0.20 and 0.50 never seen:** owh 0.0057, owl 0.0109, owd 0.0096, ow 0.0137.
- **Against 7i's form (post hoc, added after #16997 landed; not registered):** 7i's owC (S in place of H) scored on the same folds: (A) 0.0057 (worst 0.0062) against owh's 0.0056 (0.0064); (B2) 0.0057 against 0.0057; (B1) 0.0066 (bias &minus;0.0030) against 0.0059 (+0.0002). The two forms agree in sample (0.0043 both) and on unseen idle shares; H = S/(1 &minus; S), the model's own idle hours per working hour, extrapolates the halved standby power slightly better. 7i's headline replicates under an independently written pipeline and a share-extrapolation test 7i did not run.
- **SI1 held** (0.0056). **SI2 held** (&minus;65%). **SI3 held** (0.0056 against 0.0065). **SI4 held** (d &minus;0.0402, &minus;d/b 1.22). **SI5 held** (0.0059). **SI6 refuted** (owd +22%: forcing drag and standby to trade one for one costs; the fitted ratio is 1.22, and on the Salami neutral readings, whose drag is lower, owd misses by 0.0059 to 0.0076). **SI7 held** (0.0057). Six of seven.
- Reading: the intercept 7h found to move is the competitor's idle cost, and it can be carried as a number rather than refitted. A builder needs the pump's drag kW<sub>o</sub> and shape k<sub>w</sub> from its data sheet, plus two numbers about their own machine: the idle share and the load-sensing pump's standby kW at their idle speed. Written per idle hour, E<sub>50</sub> = 0.8214 + H (0.0329 kW<sub>o</sub> &minus; 0.0402 P<sub>LS</sub>) &minus; 0.435 k<sub>w</sub>. Why a standby kW counts 1.22 times a drag kW is not settled here: the drag kW<sub>o</sub> is read at 85 L/min while the cells charge drag at each geometry's picked size (centred lower, 72 L/min), so a kW of kW<sub>o</sub> stands for less than a kW of the drag the optimum pays.
- Limits: the 22 conditions are 6j's bracket (idle speed ratios 1.0 and 0.5 are not sourced; neutral curves are other valves'), so B1 and B2 test interpolation along two axes and extrapolation in standby kW over 1.4 to 3.1 kW only; H drops the record's own still share; the pick weight w(q) is the central setting's; coefficients still rest on 14 readings from eight maker groups; all crossings are from one machine's motion (fuxi folder 75).
- Next: (s) the 1.22 ratio (7i finds 1.27): read the drag at the pick-weighted size in the idle term (kW<sub>w</sub>) and test whether the ratio goes to 1; 7i's (r), S varied jointly with idle speed; (p) the geared AZPN's residual against a measured PTO gearbox map; (g) a family with drag above 4 kW or below 2 kW; (a) a multi-unit measured dataset; (e) the journal template and author block (owner).
## 2026-11-17 · Milestone 7i: carry the competitor's idle cost in the rule, so one fit covers every idle setting (7h's Next q), scored leave-one-setting-out (predictions written before any 7i number was computed)
- Why: 7h's Next (q). 7h found the rule's two slopes (drag kW<sub>o</sub>, shape k<sub>w</sub>) transfer across idle settings but its intercept does not: at half idle speed the load-sensing pump's standby power halves (about 2.9 to 1.5 kW) and the crossing rises by 0.02 to 0.035, which the frozen rule misreads. A builder should not have to rebuild the break-even model to re-derive the intercept. Adding the one number that moves it, the competitor's idle cost, should let a single pooled fit predict a setting it never saw.
- Disclosed before the run: 7h's cells are rebuilt (they were not saved); 7h's per-condition (i) ow and (iii) RMS must reproduce its results file to four places before any 7i score is read. No 7i fit or error has been computed or looked at.
- Method fixed now: `experiments/gear_pump_idlecost.py`, on 7h's 22 conditions &times; 196 (shape, drag) cells (4312 cells), features read per condition as 7h reads them; P<sub>LS</sub> is the condition's load-sensing standby kW (2.89 to 3.06 at working speed, 1.44 to 1.61 at half), S its idle share. **Rules**, each one pooled least-squares fit over all training cells of all training conditions: ow = a + b kW<sub>o</sub> + c k<sub>w</sub> (no setting term, reference); owL = ow + d P<sub>LS</sub>; owC (idle-cost form) = a + b S&middot;kW<sub>o</sub> + c k<sub>w</sub> + d S&middot;P<sub>LS</sub>. **Scores:** (A) leave-one-condition-out and leave-one-maker-family-out at once (the held condition's cells and every cell touching the held family are out of training; 22 &times; 8 fits), RMS over each condition's 14 diagonals; (B) leave-one-idle-speed-out, the same with all conditions at the held idle speed ratio out of training (extrapolation in P<sub>LS</sub> by a factor of two). References from 7h, per condition: (i) refitted ow and (iii) frozen slopes, own intercept, both of which need the condition's own cells.
- **Predictions:**
  - IC1: in the pooled fit over all 4312 cells, owL's P<sub>LS</sub> coefficient is negative (a dearer competitor idle lowers the crossing). Prior 90%.
  - IC2: under (A), ow's worst condition RMS is above 0.015 and owL's at most 0.010. Prior 55%.
  - IC3: under (A), owL's median condition RMS is at most 1.25 &times; the median of 7h's refitted ow (i), 0.0056, that is at most 0.0070. Prior 50%.
  - IC4: under (A), owC's median condition RMS is below owL's. Prior 50%.
  - IC5: under (A), the better of owL and owC (by median, chosen before reading per-condition rows) is within 1.25 &times; 7h's (iii) in at least 18 of 22 conditions. Prior 40%.
  - IC6: under (B), owC's worst condition RMS is at most 0.010. Prior 25%.

### Outcome of 7i (`experiments/gear_pump_idlecost.py`, `experiments/gear_pump_idlecost_results.txt`; numpy and h5py, about 16 min on 4 cores; 4 new tests, `tests/test_gear_pump_idlecost.py`; manuscript section 3.5 and README updated)
- Check: 7h's per-condition (i) refitted ow and (iii) frozen-slope RMS reproduce its results file in all 22 conditions (largest difference 0.0000), and the central condition reproduces 7g's 0.0056.
- **Pooled fits over 4312 cells:** ow (no setting term) E<sub>50</sub> = 0.7961 + 0.0053 kW<sub>o</sub> &minus; 0.4350 k<sub>w</sub>, in-sample RMS 0.0147 (the drag slope collapses to a third of 7g's, because across settings drag and the competitor's standby fall together at half idle speed); owL adds &minus;0.0247 per kW of P<sub>LS</sub> (drag slope back to +0.0202; RMS 0.0052); **owC: E<sub>50</sub> = 0.8234 + 0.0524 S&middot;kW<sub>o</sub> &minus; 0.4350 k<sub>w</sub> &minus; 0.0664 S&middot;P<sub>LS</sub>**, RMS 0.0043. The shape coefficient is the same in all three (k<sub>w</sub> varies only with the shape family, and every condition carries every pair).
- **(A) leave-condition-and-family-out, per condition RMS over 14 diagonals:** ow median 0.0123, worst 0.0298 (half idle speed, bias +0.028); owL median 0.0057, worst 0.0117 (S = 0.20, bias +0.0098; and 0.0111 at S = 0.50): P<sub>LS</sub> fixes the idle speed but not the idle share; **owC median 0.0058, worst 0.0062**, with no condition worse than 7h's fully refitted ow (median 0.0056, worst 0.0064), which needed each condition's own 196 cells.
- **(B) leave-idle-speed-and-family-out** (trained on the other speed only, P<sub>LS</sub> extrapolated by a factor of two): ow worst 0.0435; owL worst 0.0129; owC worst 0.0098, median 0.0066.
- **IC1 held** (&minus;0.0247 per kW). **IC2 refuted** (ow's worst 0.0298 &gt; 0.015 as predicted, but owL's 0.0117, at S = 0.20, misses the 0.010 bar; owC's worst is 0.0062). **IC3 held** (0.0057). **IC4 refuted**, narrowly (owC median 0.0058 against owL's 0.0057, though owC's worst is half owL's). **IC5 held** (owL, chosen by median, within 1.25 &times; (iii) in 18 of 22; misses at S 0.20, 0.30, 0.50 and PVG 32 standby 5 L/min). **IC6 held** (0.0098). Four of six.
- Post hoc (not registered): owC is within 1.25 &times; 7h's (iii) in 21 of 22 conditions (the miss, S = 0.30, at 1.28 &times;).
- Reading: one four-number rule now covers every idle setting tried without a refit. Both idle powers enter scaled by the idle share, and the competitor's standby kW counts about 1.27 times the fixed pump's drag kW (0.0664 against 0.0524 per idle-share kW), so the rule reads as E<sub>50</sub> &asymp; 0.823 &minus; 0.435 k<sub>w</sub> + S (0.052 kW<sub>o</sub> &minus; 0.066 P<sub>LS</sub>): what matters at idle is the drag difference, weighted by how long the machine idles. Median prediction is no better than owL's; the gain is in the worst case, at the idle shares owL cannot see. A builder needs four numbers they can read off their own machine (shape, drag, the load-sensing pump's standby kW and the idle share) and no break-even model run.
- Limits: as 7h; the 22 conditions are one machine's motion and one load-sensing pump; S varies only at the central setting, so S &times; P<sub>LS</sub> is not tested jointly away from it (S at half idle speed is untried); the form owC was registered, but chosen as 7h's Next named it; (B) has two folds, one per idle speed.
- Next: (r) S varied jointly with idle speed and standby (a 5 &times; 18 grid), scored leave-one-condition-out, to test the S&middot;P<sub>LS</sub> product off the central setting; (p) the geared AZPN's residual against a measured PTO gearbox map; (g) a family with drag above 4 kW or below 2 kW; (a) a multi-unit measured dataset; (e) the journal template and author block (owner).

## 2026-11-17 · Milestone 7h: does the selection rule hold away from the one idle setting it was fitted at? The 196-cell factorial rerun at all 18 idle settings and four other idle shares, with 7g's rules refitted and frozen (predictions written before any 7h number was computed)
- Why: every milestone since 6u lists "one central setting" among its limits. The rules were fitted at S = 0.38 with the PVG 32 neutral curve, 2 L/min standby flow and idle at working speed; 6j already showed that the setting moves the share above the threshold from 71 to 91%, so it moves the crossing E<sub>50</sub> too. A builder's machine idles at its own setting. Whether ow (7g's headline) is a rule about gear pumps or a fit to one idle assumption is the question a reviewer will ask first.
- Disclosed before the run: 7g's results file is being reproduced byte for byte first; no cell, fit or error at any other setting has been computed or looked at. 7e's pooled pick weight w(q) is reused from the central setting (not re-derived per setting), so kW<sub>o</sub>'s weights are fixed; the drag features themselves are read at each condition's own idle setting, as a builder would read them.
- Method fixed now: `experiments/gear_pump_settings.py`. Conditions: the 18 idle settings of 6j (`SETTINGS`: PVG 32, Salami-text and Salami-curve neutral readings &times; standby flow 0, 2, 5 L/min &times; idle pump speed ratio 1.0, 0.5) at S = 0.38, plus S in {0.20, 0.30, 0.40, 0.50} at the central setting: 22 conditions, the central one included once. In each, 7g's 14 variants and 196 (shape, drag) cells are rebuilt with that condition's idle powers and share (6k's 384 geometries and E grid unchanged), and four rules are scored as 7g scored them: two (kW<sub>85</sub>), w (kW<sub>85</sub>, k<sub>w</sub>), o (kW<sub>o</sub>), ow (kW<sub>o</sub>, k<sub>w</sub>), with kW<sub>85</sub> and kW<sub>o</sub> read from the condition's own idle curve. Scores: (i) refitted per condition, pooled leave-one-maker-family-out (eight groups), RMS over the 14 diagonals; (ii) **frozen**, 7g's central pooled ow coefficients (0.7562, +0.0182, &minus;0.4269) applied unchanged to the condition's features; (iii) **frozen slopes, own intercept**: the central kW<sub>o</sub> and k<sub>w</sub> slopes with the intercept refitted, leave-one-maker-family-out, on the condition's training cells. Check: the central condition reproduces 7g (pooled two 0.0084, w 0.0069, o 0.0062, ow 0.0056).
- **Predictions:**
  - ST1: refitted per condition, ow's pooled diagonal RMS is below two-number's in all 22 conditions. Prior 60%.
  - ST2: ow is at least 20% below two-number in at least 18 of the 22 conditions. Prior 45%.
  - ST3: the refitted kW<sub>o</sub> slope is positive in all 22 conditions. Prior 85%.
  - ST4: the refitted k<sub>w</sub> coefficient is negative in all 22 conditions. Prior 85%.
  - ST5: the fully frozen central rule predicts the 14 diagonals with RMS at most 0.010 in at least 11 of the 22 conditions. Prior 40%.
  - ST6: frozen slopes with an own intercept score within 1.25 &times; the refitted ow's RMS in at least 18 of the 22 conditions (one number, the intercept, is all a builder must re-derive for their idle setting). Prior 55%.

### Outcome of 7h (`experiments/gear_pump_settings.py`, `experiments/gear_pump_settings_results.txt`; numpy and h5py, 14 min on 4 cores; 3 new tests, `tests/test_gear_pump_settings.py`; manuscript section 3.5 and limitation 5, README updated)
- Check: 7g's results file was reproduced byte for byte first (155 s, cache rebuilt from Hugging Face); the central condition reproduces 7g's pooled two, w, o and ow exactly (+0.0000 each). All 196 cells cross 50% inside the E grid in every condition.
- **The setting moves the crossing, as expected:** the 14 diagonals' mean E<sub>50</sub> runs 0.7914 (Salami-text neutral, 5 L/min standby, working speed) to 0.8374 (PVG 32, no standby, half idle speed), and 0.7989 to 0.8162 over idle shares 0.50 to 0.20. Half idle speed raises it by about 0.02 to 0.03 on every neutral reading: the load-sensing pump's standby power halves (2.89 to 3.06 kW at working speed, 1.44 to 1.61 at half), while the gear pump's drag at 85 L/min falls by only 0.35 kW (PVG 32) to 1.0 kW (Salami readings).
- **Refitted per condition (i):** ow beats two-number in all 22 conditions, by 17% to 43% (median 32%); its pooled leave-one-maker-family-out RMS is 0.0047 to 0.0064 (median 0.0056; two-number 0.0064 to 0.0103). The ranking two &gt; w &gt; o &gt; ow holds at working speed; at half idle speed o and ow tie or o wins narrowly (0.0056 to 0.0062 against 0.0058 to 0.0062), because the shape number's weight falls (k<sub>w</sub> coefficient &minus;0.39 to &minus;0.42 against &minus;0.41 to &minus;0.50 at working speed). The kW<sub>o</sub> slope is +0.0175 to +0.0195 over the 18 settings at S = 0.38, and moves with the idle share: +0.0087, +0.0136, +0.0182, +0.0195, +0.0275 at S = 0.20, 0.30, 0.38, 0.40, 0.50.
- **Frozen (ii):** 7g's central coefficients applied unchanged miss by RMS 0.0042 to 0.0086 at working speed and S 0.30 to 0.40, but by 0.029 to 0.035 at half idle speed (a bias of +0.028 to +0.035: the rule reads the lower drag as a lower crossing, while the crossing rises with the cheaper standby) and 0.0121 at S = 0.50.
- **Frozen slopes, own intercept (iii):** RMS 0.0044 to 0.0076 (median 0.0051), within 1.25 &times; the refitted ow's in all 22 conditions and below it in 20 (above only at S = 0.20 and 0.50, where the kW<sub>o</sub> slope itself moves): fixing two slopes and refitting one number beats refitting all three. So the two slopes transfer across idle settings; the intercept does not.
- **ST1 held** (22 of 22). **ST2 held** (20 of 22; both misses are Salami neutral readings at half idle speed, &minus;17% to &minus;20% to four places). **ST3 held** (22 of 22). **ST4 held** (22 of 22). **ST5 held**, narrowly (12 of 22: every working-speed setting and S 0.20 to 0.40; every half-speed setting and S = 0.50 fail). **ST6 held** (22 of 22). Six of six.
- Reading: the rule's form is not an artefact of the central idle setting. Drag in kW at 85 L/min (with the drive's extra drag where the picks fall) and the shape number k<sub>w</sub> carry the gear pump's part of the crossing at every setting tried, with about the same slopes; what the setting changes is the competitor's idle cost, which the rule does not carry and which moves the intercept by up to 0.035. A builder can keep the published slopes and re-derive one number, the intercept, from the break-even model at their own idle speed, standby flow and neutral curve; the slope on drag also scales with the idle share, so a fleet whose machines idle half the time should refit it too.
- Limits: as 7g; the pick weight w(q) is the central setting's; settings are 6j's bracket (idle speed 1.0 and 0.5 are not sourced; neutral curves are other valves'); the rule's coefficients still rest on 14 readings from eight maker groups.
- Next: (q) a fourth rule number for the competitor's idle cost (the load-sensing standby kW, or the idle-hour price difference S &times; h &times; (P<sub>LS</sub> &minus; P<sub>OC</sub>)) so one fit covers every setting, registered and scored leave-one-setting-out; (p) the geared AZPN's residual against a measured PTO gearbox map; (g) a family with drag above 4 kW or below 2 kW; (a) a multi-unit measured dataset; (e) the journal template and author block (owner).

## 2026-11-17 · Milestone 7g: read the drag at 85 L/min from the pump's own held curve, and add only the spin's and drive's extra drag, weighted by the picks above the largest size (7f's Next o), registered and scored as 7f scored kW<sub>w</sub> (predictions written before any 7g number was computed)
- Why: 7f's Next (o). 7f's pick-weighted drag kW<sub>w</sub> cleared the registered 20% bar pooled (dww &minus;29%) but cost the held panel (0.0034 against two-number's 0.0022), because among the eight held catalogue families kW<sub>w</sub>/kW<sub>85</sub> varies (0.79 to 0.87) by more than it carries about the crossing. The pooled gain came from not charging a spun or geared pump at 85 L/min for drag it carries only above its largest size. A rule that keeps kW<sub>85</sub> for the curve a catalogue pump actually has, and charges the extra drag of the spin or step-up drive only where the optimum's picks fall, should keep the held panel and the pooled gain at once.
- Disclosed before the run: 7f's results file is reproduced byte for byte first; no 7g feature, fit or error below has been computed or looked at. 7e's pooled pick weight w(q) and 7f's pipeline are reused unchanged.
- Method fixed now: `experiments/gear_pump_holddrag.py`, on 7f's 14 variants, 196 cells and picks unchanged. **Feature:** kW<sub>o</sub>(g) = kW<sub>85</sub>(h) + &Sigma;<sub>q &gt; Q<sub>L</sub></sub> w(q) [P<sub>idle,g</sub>(q) &minus; P<sub>idle,h</sub>(q)], with h the held (never-spun, ungeared) reading of g's family and Q<sub>L</sub> its largest size's 20 bar flow at 2100 r/min, both on GRID at the central setting. - Reading: 7f's trade was not needed. The pooled gain came from charging the spin and the drive only where the optimum picks; the held-panel cost came from also re-reading the drag of the catalogue curve itself at the picks. Splitting the two keeps the gain (and adds to it) at no cost on catalogue pumps. One rule now serves both cases: for a pump inside its size range it is 7e's w rule at 85 L/min; for a pump spun or geared past it, add the extra drag weighted by the share of picks above the largest size. The paper's headline moves to ow. The geared AZPN's residual (&minus;0.0065) is still the largest and is not a drag-reading problem: five drag and shape readings have moved it by at most 0.002.
- Limits: as 7f; kW<sub>o</sub> was the rule 7f's Next named, but its form was chosen after seeing 7f's held-panel cost (disclosed); for the AZPF, whose largest size (58.9 L/min) lies below 85 L/min, kW<sub>85</sub> of the held reading is itself Gear's extrapolation past the size range; the pooled gain still rests on two maker groups' spun and geared readings.

- Reading: the pick-weighted drag wins where the pooled test is weighted, on pumps run past their largest size, because it does not charge a spun or geared pump for drag it carries only at sizes the optimum seldom picks. On real catalogue families alone, without spinning, it is worse than reading at 85 L/min (held panel 0.0034 against 0.0022), since among those eight families kW<sub>w</sub>/kW<sub>85</sub> varies (0.79 to 0.87) by more than it carries about the crossing. So the registered bar is cleared, but by a rule that trades the held case, the one a builder buying a catalogue pump faces, for the spun and geared cases. The paper keeps the two-number rule at 85 L/min as its headline for held catalogue pumps and states dww as the registered rule for a pool that includes pumps spun or geared past their size range, with the held-panel cost stated beside it. The pick's own pressure adds nothing (DP5). The geared AZPN still sits 0.007 below every rule tried.

## 2026-11-16 · Milestone 7e: read the shape where the optimum actually picks its pumps (a chosen-size-weighted shape number, and the share of picks above a family's largest size), registered and scored as 7d scored k<sub>lo</sub> (predictions written before any 7e number was computed)
- Why: 7d's Next (m). k<sub>lo</sub> reads a family's shape at one flow (57 L/min) and cut the pooled out-of-sample error by 18%, short of the registered 20%; the geared AZPN still sits 0.0070 below it. The crossing is set by the fixed pump sizes the fuel optimum picks across the 384 geometries, not by one flow, so the feature that should carry the shape is the family's efficiency relative to its index, averaged over where those picks fall. And 7d's Next named a second reading: how much of the chosen-size distribution lies above the family's largest size at 2100 r/min, where the hold, spin or step-up drive decides the map.
- Disclosed before the run: 7d's results file was reproduced byte for byte this session; no pick, weight, feature, fit or error below has been computed or looked at. The 7d E<sub>50</sub> of each never-spun family's diagonal (committed) is used to fix where the picks are read.
- Method fixed now: `experiments/gear_pump_chosen.py`, on 7d's 14 variants, 196 cells and pipeline unchanged. **Picks:** for each of the six never-spun families' diagonal cells (own shape and drag), at the E on the grid nearest that cell's E<sub>50</sub>, each geometry's chosen fixed-pump size is the GRID flow that maximises its break-even rate f<sub>t</sub> (`fcurve`'s argmax); the 6 &times; 384 = 2304 picks are pooled into one weight w(q) on GRID. The AZPF and AZPN readings never enter the weights, so their features carry nothing of their own outcomes; a never-spun family's own picks are one sixth of the weight it is scored with (disclosed leakage). **Features** (per shape family f): k<sub>w</sub> = &Sigma;<sub>q</sub> w(q) &eta;<sub>f</sub>(q, 150 bar)/E<sub>f</sub> &minus; 1; s<sub>f</sub> = share of the pooled picks above the family's largest size's 20 bar flow at 2100 r/min (the same for all four readings of the AZPF, and of the AZPN). **Rules:** one, two, lo (7d's, as a check), w = a + b kW + c k<sub>w</sub>, above = a + b kW + c s. Scored exactly as 7d: pooled 196-cell fit; leave-one-maker-family-out over eight groups, RMS over 14 diagonals; and the three eight-family panels (held, spun, geared) alone, leave-one-family-out. Check: two and lo reproduce 7d (pooled 0.0084 and 0.0068; panels 0.0022, 0.0035, 0.0051).
- **Predictions:**
  - WS1: pooled leave-one-maker-family-out, the w rule's diagonal RMS is at least 20% below two-number (at most 0.00672). Prior 50%.
  - WS2: the w rule's pooled diagonal RMS is below k<sub>lo</sub>'s 0.0068. Prior 45%.
  - WS3: in the 196-cell pooled fit the k<sub>w</sub> coefficient is negative. Prior 75%.
  - WS4: under the w rule, pooled out of sample, the geared AZPN at the sourced point lies within &plusmn;0.005. Prior 35%.
  - WS5: on the held panel alone, the w rule's leave-one-family-out RMS is at most 0.0030. Prior 60%.

### Outcome of 7e (`experiments/gear_pump_chosen.py`, `experiments/gear_pump_chosen_results.txt`; numpy and h5py, 111 s; 4 new tests, `tests/test_gear_pump_chosen.py`; manuscript section 3.5 updated)
- Check: two-number and k<sub>lo</sub> reproduce 7d exactly, pooled (0.0084, 0.0068) and on all three panels (+0.0000 each); 7d's own results file was reproduced byte for byte first.
- **Where the optimum picks:** over the six never-spun diagonals at their crossings, the chosen fixed-pump flow runs p10 58.0, median 72.0, p90 90.5 L/min (mean 72.3); 41.5% of picks fall in 50 to 70 L/min, 47.4% in 70 to 90, 11.1% in 90 to 110, none below 50 or above 110. Every family's median lies between 64 and 72.5 L/min; every geometry had a positive break-even rate. So the 85 L/min index sits above most picks, and 7d's 57 L/min sits at about the 10th percentile.
- **The features:** k<sub>w</sub> runs &minus;0.018 (Casappa, Danfoss) to +0.003 (Marzocchi) over the never-spun families. It rises with the drive's loss but less than k<sub>lo</sub> does (AZPF held +0.0004, spun +0.0037, geared +0.0046, at 98% +0.0065; AZPN &minus;0.0095, &minus;0.0056, &minus;0.0008, +0.0091), because most picks lie above 57 L/min, nearer the index the drive lowers. s: 0.883 of the picks lie above the AZPF's largest size (58.9 L/min) and 0.340 above the AZPN's (75.6); none above any other family's.
- **Pooled, 196 cells:** E<sub>50</sub> = 0.8189 + 0.0172 (kW &minus; 3.57) &minus; 0.427 k<sub>w</sub> (in-sample RMS 0.0044; k<sub>lo</sub> 0.0042, two-number 0.0055). Leave-one-maker-family-out over 14 diagonals: one 0.0120, two 0.0084, lo 0.0068, **w 0.0069 (&minus;18%)**, above 0.0093 (+11%). On the eight held diagonals: two 0.0056, lo 0.0050, **w 0.0043**. k<sub>w</sub> fixes the Casappa and Danfoss misses that k<sub>lo</sub> leaves or overshoots (+0.0088/&minus;0.0078 to +0.0020; +0.0099/+0.0076 to &minus;0.0001) but leaves the geared AZPN at &minus;0.0082 (lo &minus;0.0070) and at 98% &minus;0.0204.
- **Panels alone:** held two 0.0022, lo 0.0022, **w 0.0017** (the lowest held error the programme has had); spun 0.0035, 0.0028, 0.0026; geared 0.0051, 0.0040, 0.0042. s is worse than two-number pooled and barely helps on panels (0.0027, 0.0031, 0.0049).
- **WS1 refuted** (&minus;18% against the registered &minus;20%). **WS2 refuted** (0.0069 against 0.0068). **WS3 held** (&minus;0.427). **WS4 refuted** (&minus;0.0082). **WS5 held** (0.0017). **WS6 held** (+11%). Three of six.
- Reading: two shape numbers chosen on different grounds, one fixed by the families' size ranges (7d) and one by where the optimum actually picks (7e), land on the same 18% out-of-sample gain, and both leave the geared AZPN 0.007 to 0.008 below the rule. That makes the 18% look like what a shape number at 150 bar can buy here, and the AZPN's residual look like something outside the shape at 150 bar. The weighted reading is the better one on real catalogue families (held panel 0.0017, Casappa and Danfoss fixed), the 57 L/min one on spun and geared pumps whose loss sits near the index; neither passes the registered bar, so the two-number rule stays the headline. The share above the largest size (s) carries nothing a family constant can: it cannot tell a family's held, spun and geared readings apart, as registered.
- Diagnostic for the next step (read after the registered numbers, not a prediction): the picks centre on 72 L/min, but the rule's drag is read at 85 L/min. A pump whose drag per L/min changes with size (spinning raises it; the drive adds a tare) is charged at the wrong size, which the AZPN, with 34% of picks above its largest size and the rest below, would show most.
- Limits: the weights come from six families' own diagonals, so each never-spun family is scored with a weight one sixth its own; one pressure (150 bar) although fixed pumps run at p + m* (6u); one central setting; typical catalogue curves.
- Next: (n) the drag read at the picks (idle kW averaged over w(q), in place of kW at 85 L/min), and the shape read at each pick's own outlet pressure, registered and scored the same way; unchanged (l) a measured PTO gearbox map; (g) a family with drag above 4 kW or below 2 kW; (a) a multi-unit measured dataset; (c) the XE80E's own pump make; (e) the journal template and author block (owner).

## 2026-11-16 · Milestone 7d: a third rule number for the shape below the index point (the family's efficiency at 57 L/min over its index), registered and scored leave-one-maker-family-out on held, spun and geared readings pooled (predictions written before any 7d number was computed)
- Why: 7c's Next (j). The step-up drive lowers the index point (85 L/min) without touching the smaller pumps the optimum also picks, so normalised to the index those pumps look better and the crossing falls, the opposite of what the drag rule reads (geared AZPN error &minus;0.0101). 6u's size slope at 150 L/min (k<sub>hi</sub> = &eta;(150 L/min, 150 bar)/E &minus; 1) was tried as a third number in 6u/FX5 and overfit with five folds; it looks above the index, where the drive's loss also sits. The feature the 7c diagnosis points to looks below: **k<sub>lo</sub> = &eta;<sub>f</sub>(57 L/min, 150 bar)/E<sub>f</sub> &minus; 1**. 57 L/min is fixed now because it is the only flow inside every family's size range at 2100 r/min (smallest sizes up to 55.8 L/min, Casappa and the bench KP30; largest down to 58.9 L/min, the AZPF; read this session from the sizes alone), so no family's k<sub>lo</sub> leans on the hold rule or a spun or geared pump.
- Disclosed before the run: 7c's results file was reproduced byte for byte this session (88 s); the families' 20 bar flow ranges were read; no k<sub>lo</sub>, fit, crossing or error below has been computed or looked at.
- Method fixed now: `experiments/gear_pump_third.py`. Pool 14 variants: the six families that are never spun (Casappa, Danfoss, Marzocchi, bench, AZPG, AZPU) and four readings each of the AZPF and AZPN: held (7a), spun lossless (7b), geared at the sourced point (&eta;<sub>f</sub> 0.9934, tare 0.65) and geared at the swept lossy corner (0.98, 0.65) (7c). Build the 14 &times; 14 (shape, drag) factorial (196 cells; 7a's pipeline: 6k's 384 geometries, S = 0.38, central setting, E 0.70 to 0.96 step 0.0025). Rules by least squares: one number; two-number a + b kW (drag family's idle kW at 85 L/min); three-number (lo) adding c k<sub>lo,f</sub> (shape family); three-number (hi) adding c k<sub>hi,f</sub> (6u's). Leave-one-maker-family-out: eight groups (each never-spun family alone; the AZPF's four readings together; the AZPN's four together); fit on the cells with neither shape nor drag in the group and predict each of the group's diagonals; score the RMS over all 14 diagonals. Also on the three eight-family panels of 7a (held), 7b (spun) and 7c (geared, sourced) separately, each 64 cells, ordinary leave-one-family-out as those milestones ran it. Check: each panel's two-number diagonal RMS reproduces its milestone (0.0022, 0.0035, 0.0051).
- **Predictions:**
  - TN1: pooled leave-one-maker-family-out, three-number (lo) diagonal RMS is at least 20% below two-number. Prior 50%.
  - TN2: in the 196-cell pooled fit the k<sub>lo</sub> coefficient is negative (smaller pumps that look better against the index pull the crossing down). Prior 70%.
  - TN3: three-number (hi) does not cut the pooled diagonal RMS by 20% against two-number (6u's overfit repeats). Prior 60%.

### Outcome of 7d (`experiments/gear_pump_third.py`, `experiments/gear_pump_third_results.txt`; numpy and h5py, 116 s; 4 new tests, `tests/test_gear_pump_third.py`, 127 in all; manuscript section 3.5 updated)

- **The feature:** k<sub>lo</sub> runs &minus;0.057 (Casappa, whose small sizes are much worse than its index) to +0.007 (AZPU) over the never-spun families, and rises with every step a pump takes away from its own size range: AZPF held +0.002, spun +0.009, geared +0.017, geared at 98% +0.033; AZPN &minus;0.002, +0.004, +0.011, +0.026. The bench shape is built on one size, so its k<sub>lo</sub> and k<sub>hi</sub> are exactly 0.
- **Pooled, 196 cells:** E<sub>50</sub> = 0.8200 + 0.0172 (kW &minus; 3.57) &minus; 0.182 k<sub>lo</sub> (in-sample RMS 0.0042, against 0.0055 two-number and 0.0054 with k<sub>hi</sub>). Leave-one-maker-family-out RMS over the 14 diagonals: one number 0.0120, **two-number 0.0084, k<sub>lo</sub> 0.0068 (&minus;18%)**, k<sub>hi</sub> 0.0096 (+14%). On the eight held diagonals alone 0.0056 against 0.0050. Per reading, k<sub>lo</sub> shrinks the geared misses (AZPF geared &minus;0.0035 to +0.0006, at 98% &minus;0.0091 to &minus;0.0014) but not the AZPN's (&minus;0.0085 to &minus;0.0070; at 98% &minus;0.0234 to &minus;0.0200), and it moves the Casappa from +0.0088 to &minus;0.0078.
- **Panels alone, leave-one-family-out:** held 0.0022 two-number against 0.0022 k<sub>lo</sub> (no cost); spun 0.0035 against 0.0028; geared 0.0051 against 0.0040. k<sub>hi</sub> is worse than two-number on all three (0.0039, 0.0051, 0.0067): 6u's overfit repeats on eight folds.
- **TN1 refuted, near the bar** (&minus;18% against the registered &minus;20%). **TN2 held** (&minus;0.182). **TN3 held** (k<sub>hi</sub> +14%). **TN4 refuted** (geared AZPN &minus;0.0070). **TN5 held** (0.0022). **TN6 held** (0.0040 against 0.0051).
- Reading: the 7c diagnosis was right in sign and in kind. A shape feature read *below* the index point, the efficiency of a 57 L/min pump of the same family relative to the index, carries real out-of-sample information that the drag does not: it never hurts a held family, it cuts the spun and geared panels' errors by a fifth, and its coefficient has the sign the mechanism predicts. The feature read above the index (6u's k<sub>hi</sub>) hurts every time. It does not fix the AZPN: the geared AZPN's crossing still sits 0.007 below the three-number rule, so most of that miss is something neither number reads. Because TN1 missed its registered bar, the paper keeps the two-number rule as its headline and states k<sub>lo</sub> as a registered, partial improvement for pumps run outside their size range; for a builder, a pump whose small sizes look as good as its index point (k<sub>lo</sub> above about +0.01) should be expected to cross about 0.002 lower per +0.01 than the drag alone says.
- Limits: the pool weights the six never-spun families' cells heavily (they appear in every row and column); the four AZPF and AZPN readings are not independent families, so eight groups, not fourteen, carry the out-of-sample claim; 57 L/min is fixed by the families' size ranges, not tuned, and no other flow was tried; one central setting; typical catalogue curves.
- Next: (m) what else the geared AZPN carries: a reading of how much of the optimum's chosen-size distribution lies above the largest size at 2100 r/min, scored the same way; (l) a measured PTO gearbox efficiency map if one is published openly; unchanged (g) a family with drag above 4 kW or below 2 kW; (a) a multi-unit measured dataset; (c) the XE80E's own pump make; (e) the journal template and author block (owner).

## 2026-11-16 · Milestone 7c: price the step-up drive that 7b's spun pump needs (spur-gear system loss from NASA TP-1622, swept), predictions written before any geared number was computed
- Why: 7b's Next (k) and its first limit. Spinning the largest size above 2100 r/min needs a step-up drive, and 7b priced that drive as lossless, so its spun crossings and the rule's &minus;0.0060 miss on the spun AZPF leave out a loss that adds drag at idle and lowers efficiency under load. ISO/TR 14179-1 (the method 7b named) is not open, so the loss is taken from an open, measured source instead: Anderson and Loewenthal's NASA Technical Paper 1622 (1980), "Spur-gear-system efficiency at part and full load" (NTRS 19800009206, read this session with its text layer; PDF sha256 33a11f71&hellip;cbb6). Its Table III design example, one steel spur mesh on ball bearings at 2000 r/min and 56.79 kW in, loses 0.3776 kW in all (sliding, rolling, windage and bearings): **system efficiency 99.34%** at full load. Its figure 9 text says the no-load (tare) loss "theoretically can reach 65 percent of the full-load loss" at 2000 r/min, because rolling, windage and bearing losses barely depend on load.
- Disclosed before the run: nothing geared has been computed; 7b's file is reproduced first. The NASA rig is an aerospace-grade test gearbox with jet lubrication; a mobile-machine PTO step-up drive is likely lossier, so the full-load efficiency is swept down to 98.0%, a figure chosen as a plausible lower end, not sourced. The tare share is swept 0 to 0.65 (0.65 sourced as the upper end at 2000 r/min; the PTO input runs at about 2100). The drive is rated for the spun pump's own drive power at its p<sub>2</sub> (AZPF size 28: 200 bar; AZPN size 36: 180 bar) at its spun speed; a builder might fit a larger one, which would raise the tare loss.
- Method fixed now: `experiments/gear_pump_stepup.py`. A `GearedSpun` family equals 7b's `SpunGear` up to the largest size's 2100 r/min flow; above it the input power is the pump's drive power P plus a drive loss L = (1 &minus; &eta;<sub>f</sub>) [t P<sub>rated</sub> + (1 &minus; t) P], with P<sub>rated</sub> the pump's drive power at p<sub>2</sub> at the same speed (both scaled with flow beyond the cap, as 7b does). Efficiency is hydraulic power over P + L; idle power is P + L at the standby pressure. Grid: &eta;<sub>f</sub> in {0.9934, 0.99, 0.985, 0.98} &times; t in {0, 0.325, 0.65}, both families; the sourced point is (0.9934, 0.65). Each cell: index 2, idle kW at 85 L/min, E<sub>50</sub> with its own shape and drag (7b's pipeline: 6k's 384 geometries, S = 0.38, central setting, E 0.70 to 0.96 step 0.0025), scored against rule (Z) 0.8239 + 0.0179 (kW &minus; 3.57). At the sourced point, also the eight-family factorial and leave-one-family-out with both families geared (7b's method).
- **Predictions:**
  - GB1: at the sourced point the geared AZPF's idle power at 85 L/min is 0.05 to 0.25 kW above the ungeared spun 2.99 kW. Prior 70%.
  - GB2: at the sourced point the geared AZPF's E<sub>50</sub> is 0.0005 to 0.0040 above the ungeared spun 0.8075. Prior 60%.
  - GB3: E<sub>50</sub> does not fall as the loss grows, along either sweep axis, in either family (to within the 0.0001 the crossing is reported to). Prior 80%.
  - GB4: at the sourced point the rule (Z) error on the geared AZPF lies in &minus;0.0075 to &minus;0.0045 (the drive adds drag the rule reads, so the miss barely moves). Prior 60%.
  - GB5: across the whole grid the rule (Z) error on the geared AZPF stays within &plusmn;0.008. Prior 65%.
  - GB6: with both families geared at the sourced point, the eight-family leave-one-family-out two-number diagonal RMS is at most 0.0040. Prior 75%.

### Outcome of 7c (`experiments/gear_pump_stepup.py`, `experiments/gear_pump_stepup_results.txt`; numpy and h5py, 111 s; 3 new tests, `tests/test_gear_pump_stepup.py`, 122 in all; manuscript section 3.5 updated)
- Check: the ungeared spun diagonals reproduce 7b (AZPF 0.8075, AZPN 0.8031, +0.0000 each), and `gear_pump_spin.py` still reproduces its committed file byte for byte.
- Reading of &eta;<sub>f</sub>, found while testing and disclosed: the loss is (1 &minus; &eta;<sub>f</sub>) times the pump's power, so at rated load the drive passes 1/(2 &minus; &eta;<sub>f</sub>) of its input, 0.99344 at the sourced point (NASA: 0.9934) and 0.98039 at the swept 0.98; the difference is below 0.0004 in drive efficiency.
- **The drive at the sourced point** (&eta;<sub>f</sub> 0.9934, tare 0.65), per pump at 85 L/min: AZPF at 3000 r/min, rated 31.06 kW at 200 bar, loses 187 W at 150 bar and 136 W at zero pressure (on 1.05 kW of pump drag); AZPN at 2359 r/min, rated 27.47 kW at 180 bar, loses 171 W and 119 W.
- **AZPF geared:** idle at 85 L/min 3.13 kW (+0.14), index 2 0.8971, **E<sub>50</sub> 0.8094 (+0.0019 on the lossless spin)**; rule (Z) predicts 0.8160, **error &minus;0.0066** (spun lossless: &minus;0.0060). Over the 12-cell grid E<sub>50</sub> runs 0.8056 to 0.8100 and the error &minus;0.0064 to &minus;0.0118.
- **AZPN geared:** idle 2.73 kW, index 0.9129, **E<sub>50</sub> 0.7988 (&minus;0.0044 on the lossless spin)**; rule (Z) error **&minus;0.0101**. Over the grid E<sub>50</sub> falls to 0.7879 and the error to &minus;0.0255 at (0.98, 0.65).
- **Why the AZPN's crossing falls as its drive gets worse** (diagnostic added after the registered numbers were read, at the sourced point): E<sub>50</sub> sweeps the index with each family's shape normalised to it. The drive loss sits only above the largest size's 20 bar flow at 2100 r/min, so it lowers the index point (85 L/min) but not the smaller pumps the optimum also picks; normalised, those look better. The swaps bear this out for the AZPN: geared shape with lossless drag 0.7983 (&minus;0.0048), lossless shape with geared drag 0.8037 (+0.0006). For the AZPF the shape half does nothing (0.8075, +0.0000) and the drag half moves it +0.0031 (both together +0.0019): its largest size stops at 59 L/min, so the pumps the optimum picks are mostly spun and geared too, while the AZPN's 75.6 L/min end sits just below the index point. The rule reads only the drag, so it predicts a later crossing in every cell and misses the AZPN in the wrong direction.
- **Eight families with both geared (64 cells):** 0.7982 to 0.8300; two-number refit E<sub>50</sub> = 0.8221 + 0.0181 (kW &minus; 3.57) (in-sample RMS 0.0040); leave-one-family-out diagonal RMS **0.0051** two-number against 0.0121 one-number (lossless spin: 0.0035 against 0.0115); worst diagonal the geared AZPN, &minus;0.0093.
- **GB1 held** (+0.140 kW). **GB2 held** (+0.0019). **GB3 refuted** (the AZPN's crossing falls along both axes; the AZPF's falls along &eta;<sub>f</sub> at zero tare). **GB4 held** (&minus;0.0066). **GB5 refuted** (&minus;0.0064 to &minus;0.0118). **GB6 refuted** (0.0051).
- Reading: a sourced, aerospace-grade step-up drive costs the spun pump little (0.14 kW of idle power, 0.006 of index efficiency), but it moves the crossing the opposite way from what the drag rule expects, because it worsens the index point without touching the smaller pumps the index is meant to stand for. The rule still beats a constant by more than two to one out of sample, but on a geared pump its error reaches 0.010 at the sourced loss and 0.026 at a lossy 98% drive. For a builder: a step-up drive is not a cheap way around a family's largest size if the decision is read off the rule; read the drag *and* the index of the pump without the drive, or price the drive's loss directly. This is the first result in the programme where the error comes from a loss that changes the map's shape over size, which the rule does not read.
- Limits: the loss form is one spur mesh on ball bearings (NASA TP-1622), scaled with the pump's rated power, not a measured PTO drive; the 98% lower end is not sourced; the tare share is not speed-dependent here (the PTO input runs near 2100 r/min for every spun size); one central setting; typical catalogue curves.
- Next: (j) still open, and now sharper: a rule feature for how the index point sits relative to the family's largest size (the share of the size grid above it, or the ratio of the index to the largest size's own efficiency), registered and scored leave-one-family-out on held, spun and geared readings; (l) a two-stage drive or a measured PTO gearbox efficiency map if one is published openly; unchanged (g) a family with drag above 4 kW or below 2 kW; (a) a multi-unit measured dataset; (c) the XE80E's own pump make; (e) the journal template and author block (owner).

## 2026-11-16 · Milestone 7b: spin the largest size instead of holding it (Bosch Rexroth AZPF size 28 driven up to its rated 3000 r/min, and the AZPN size 36 up to its drawn 2700 r/min), predictions written before any spun number was computed
- Why: 7a's Next (i). Above a family's largest size the model (`Gear._blend`) holds the largest size's efficiency map and drag per L/min, as if a larger pump of the same make existed. For the AZPF that covers 77% of the fixed-pump size grid and the index point itself. A builder who needs 85 L/min from this family would instead drive size 28 faster through a step-up drive: the sheet rates size 28 to n<sub>max</sub> = 3000 r/min at p<sub>2</sub> (RE 10089/2021-11-24, page 9, table of values, read this session with `pdftotext`) and draws its power curves to 3000 r/min, so 58.9 L/min at 2100 r/min becomes about 84 L/min at 3000 r/min. Speed changes both what the hold rule assumes away: leakage is roughly fixed per bar, so volumetric efficiency rises with speed, while churning and viscous drag grow faster than flow. Whether the rule written on 2100 r/min families still prices a pump run at 1.4 times that speed is the question.
- Disclosed before the run: the 7a results were reproduced first (`gear_pump_eight.py`, checked against its committed file); nothing below has been computed. Engine idle is at working speed in the central setting (CENTRAL idle speed ratio 1.0), so the spun pump idles at its spun speed too. The step-up drive's own loss is not modelled (it would add drag; disclosed as a limit). The AZPN's rated n<sub>max</sub> could not be re-read this session (the mirror in `digitise_rexroth_azpn.py` now serves HTML), so its cap is the 2700 r/min to which its size 36 power curves are drawn; 85 L/min needs about 2380 r/min, inside that.
- Method fixed now: `experiments/gear_pump_spin.py`. A `SpunGear` family equals `Gear` up to the largest size's 2100 r/min 20 bar flow Q<sub>L</sub>; above it, a pump of flow q is the largest size at speed n = 2100 q / Q<sub>L</sub>, capped at n<sub>cap</sub> (AZPF 3000, AZPN 2700 r/min), its efficiency and drag per L/min read from the sheet's own curves at n (`measured.gear_pump_point` at that speed), and held at n<sub>cap</sub>'s per-unit values above Q<sub>L</sub> n<sub>cap</sub>/2100. Index 2 is the spun family's efficiency at 85 L/min and 150 bar; idle kW at 85 L/min as 7a reads it; E<sub>50</sub> with the spun family's own shape and drag, 6k's 384 geometries, S = 0.38, central setting, E 0.70 to 0.96 step 0.0025, exactly 7a's pipeline. Scored against rule (Z) 0.8239 + 0.0179 (kW &minus; 3.57) and 7a's refit 0.8238 + 0.0177 (kW &minus; 3.57), and against the held readings (AZPF: index 0.9103, 2.61 kW, E<sub>50</sub> 0.8075; AZPN from 7a's table). Diagnostics: spun swaps (spun shape with held drag and the reverse) to say which one moves the crossing.
- **Predictions:**
  - SP1: spinning raises the AZPF's idle power at 85 L/min above the held 2.61 kW. Prior 70% (drag grows faster than flow with speed).
  - SP2: the spun AZPF index 2 lies within 0.010 of the held 0.9103. Prior 55%.
  - SP3: the spun AZPF E<sub>50</sub> lies within 0.006 of rule (Z) at its own spun idle kW. Prior 60%.
  - SP4: the spun AZPF E<sub>50</sub> lies within 0.005 of the held 0.8075. Prior 45%.
  - SP5: the spun AZPN E<sub>50</sub> lies within 0.006 of rule (Z) at its own spun idle kW. Prior 60%.
  - SP6: in both spun families the drag swap moves E<sub>50</sub> more than the shape swap. Prior 70% (the drag has set the crossing in every family so far).

### Outcome of 7b (`experiments/gear_pump_spin.py`, `experiments/gear_pump_spin_results.txt`; numpy and h5py, 69 s, rerun identical; 4 new tests, `tests/test_gear_pump_spin.py`; manuscript section 3.5 updated)
- Check: the held AZPF and AZPN diagonals reproduce 7a's table (0.8075 and 0.8050, +0.0000 each).
- **What spinning does to the pump** (AZPF size 28, read off the sheet's curves at the solved speed): from 58.87 L/min at 2100 r/min to 84.38 L/min at the 3000 r/min cap, efficiency at 150 bar falls only from 0.9103 to 0.9043, while zero-pressure drag rises from 7.7 to 12.3 W per L/min of flow (60% more per litre). The AZPN size 36 does the same on a smaller scale: 0.9250 to 0.9197 at 85 L/min (2359 r/min), drag 6.6 to 7.9 W per L/min. Spinning covers only 12.2% (AZPF) and 10.5% (AZPN) of the size grid above the largest size; the rest is held at the cap speed.
- **Held out:** spun AZPF index 2 **0.9043**, idle power at 85 L/min **2.99 kW** (held 2.61); E<sub>50</sub> **0.8075, the same as held to four places**. Rule (Z) at 2.99 kW predicts 0.8135, **error &minus;0.0060**; the 7a 64-cell refit gives the same. Swaps: spun shape with held drag 0.8047 (&minus;0.0028), held shape with spun drag 0.8113 (+0.0038): the higher drag and the lower, flatter map cancel. Spun AZPN: index 0.9197, idle 2.61 kW, E<sub>50</sub> 0.8031 (held 0.8050), rule (Z) 0.8067, error &minus;0.0036; swaps &minus;0.0022 (shape) and +0.0007 (drag).
- **Eight families with both spun (64 cells):** 0.7996 to 0.8300; two-number refit **E<sub>50</sub> = 0.8230 + 0.0181 (kW &minus; 3.57)** (in-sample RMS 0.0035); leave-one-family-out diagonal RMS **0.0035** two-number against 0.0115 one-number (held: 0.0022 against 0.0113). The spun AZPF is now the worst-predicted diagonal (&minus;0.0055).
- **SP1 held** (2.99 against 2.61). **SP2 held** (&minus;0.0060). **SP3 refuted, at the boundary** (&minus;0.00600; its magnitude passes 0.006 below the fifth decimal, so it is called refuted as the code computes it). **SP4 held** (+0.0000). **SP5 held** (&minus;0.0036). **SP6 refuted** (AZPF: drag +0.0038 against shape &minus;0.0028, close; AZPN: drag +0.0007 against shape &minus;0.0022, the shape moved the crossing more).
- Reading: how a builder gets 85 L/min out of a family that stops at 59 L/min does not change where that family crosses (0.8075 either way), but it does change the rule's error, from +0.0008 held to &minus;0.0060 spun. The rule reads the drag and assumes the rest of the map moves with the index; spinning breaks that coupling, since it adds drag per litre while lowering efficiency at the index point and leaving the smaller sizes the optimum also picks untouched. The two-number rule still beats a constant by three to one out of sample, so the paper's claim survives, but its worst case is now a spun pump, not a held one: "read the drag at the speed the pump will run" belongs in the rule's instructions.
- Limits: the step-up drive's own loss is not priced (it would add drag and push the spun crossing up); the AZPN cap is the drawn 2700 r/min, not a rated n<sub>max</sub> read this session; one central setting (idle at working speed); typical curves.
- Next: (j) a drag feature read at the chosen pump's own speed and size, or a third rule term for the index point's position relative to the largest size, registered and scored leave-one-family-out with both held and spun readings; (k) the step-up gearbox loss from a published spur-gear efficiency (ISO/TR 14179-1) as a sweep; unchanged (g) a family with drag above 4 kW or below 2 kW; (a) a multi-unit measured dataset; (c) the XE80E's own pump make; (e) the journal template and author block (owner).

## 2026-11-16 · Milestone 7a: an eighth gear-pump family whose largest size stops below the index point (Bosch Rexroth AZPF, 4 to 28 cm<sup>3</sup>/r), held out from every fit (predictions written before any number was computed from its curves)
