## 2026-11-16 · Milestone 6z: the index fixed to hold at a family's largest size, then a seventh family (Bosch Rexroth AZPU, the low-noise cast-iron platform) held out from every fit (predictions written before any number was computed from its curves)
- Why: 6y's registered test failed because `gear_pump_shape.index2` extrapolated efficiency past the AZPN's largest size, while the model (`Gear`) holds the largest size's values there; its Next (h) asks to fix the index and register the fix before a seventh family, and (g) asks for that family. The reading that rescued the AZPN was chosen after the failure, so it needs a fresh family to confirm or refute it. Bosch Rexroth's data sheet RE 10098/02.12 (AZPU, "SILENCE" external gear pump, 12 teeth with a low-pulsation profile, cast iron, sizes 22.5 to 63 cm<sup>3</sup>/r, p<sub>2</sub> 280 bar to size 45 then 250, 225, 200 bar, table on page 14) draws on page 9 flow at 20 bar and p<sub>2</sub> against speed for the ten sizes and, on pages 9 to 11, drive power at 50 bar up to p<sub>2</sub> against speed per size, at 35 mm<sup>2</sup>/s and 50 C, as vector art (checked: `pdftocairo -svg` gives 360, 384 and 369 paths and no images on pages 9 to 11). Its largest size gives about 130 L/min, so the hold rule does not touch it: it is a clean test of the rule and of the AZPN diagnosis together. 6y's Next (g) wanted a drag above 4 kW or below 2 kW; whether the AZPU's lies there is not known yet, and nothing below picked the family for it.
- Disclosed before the run: the curves were digitised (`experiments/digitise_rexroth_azpu.py`, below) before these predictions were written, to check the sheet could be read at all (all ten flow lines and every power curve found; axis residuals at most 0.20 L/min and 0.14 kW); no efficiency, flow ratio, idle power, index or crossing was computed or looked at before this entry was committed. The sheet is measured at 35 mm<sup>2</sup>/s, the other Rexroth sheets at 32; it is used as drawn.
- Method fixed now: (h) `gear_pump_shape.index2` holds the largest (and smallest) size's eta(150 bar) outside the sizes' 20 bar flow range instead of extrapolating, which is what `Gear` does; `gear_pump_six.py` keeps its registered reading with `hold=False` so 6y's file still reproduces. For the five families that reach 85 L/min the two agree exactly (6y: largest difference 0.0000). (g) `experiments/digitise_rexroth_azpu.py`: 6w's digitiser with this sheet's conventions (curves and frames dark grey, white grid lines on a grey ground at 1000 and 2000 r/min, curve strokes 1.176 to 1.2 wide, dashed flow lines broken under labels into light-grey pieces, which are joined by collinearity as 6w does; flow lines sampled as polylines and speed mapped through the grid, as 6y does) into `data/rexroth_azpu_curves.csv`. `experiments/gear_pump_seven.py`: the AZPU family alone as 6w and 6y built theirs (index 2 under the fixed rule, idle kW at 85 L/min, E<sub>50</sub> with its own shape and drag, 6k's 384 geometries, S = 0.38, central setting, E 0.70 to 0.96 step 0.0025), scored against (A) 6x's rule as committed, 0.8241 + 0.0179 (kW &minus; 3.57), (B) 6y's post-hoc six-family refit, 0.8245 + 0.0183 (kW &minus; 3.57), and (C) the one-number 0.8172; then the 7 &times; 7 factorial (49 cells) under the fixed index, the rules refitted, and leave-one-family-out over seven families (fit on the 36 cells with neither shape nor drag h). Check: the 36 six-family cells under the fixed index reproduce 6y's (iii) post-hoc table.
- **Predictions:**
  - AZ1: the AZPU crossing lies within 0.006 of rule (B) at its own idle kW. Prior 65%.
  - AZ2: rule (A)'s error on the AZPU is smaller than the one-number rule's. Prior 65%.
  - AZ3: the AZPU's idle power at 85 L/min lies outside the 2.49 to 3.64 kW the six families span. Prior 35%.
  - AZ4: the AZPU's index 2 lies within 0.020 of the AZPG's 0.9124 (same maker, cast iron, same sizes). Prior 50%.
  - AZ5: the 49-cell two-number slope lies in 0.016 to 0.021 per kW. Prior 75%.
  - AZ6: leave-one-family-out over seven families, the two-number diagonal RMS is below 0.005 and below the one-number RMS. Prior 75%.

### Outcome of 6z (`experiments/digitise_rexroth_azpu.py`, `data/rexroth_azpu_curves.csv`, `experiments/gear_pump_seven.py`, `experiments/gear_pump_seven_results.txt`; numpy and h5py, 42 s, rerun identical; `gear_pump_six.py` still reproduces its file byte for byte with `hold=False`; 5 new tests, 111 in all; manuscript abstract, section 3.5, the design rule and the conclusions updated, `check_numbers.py --paper manuscript.md` 81 claims, 0 missing, 0 orphans; `build_tex.py` 0 problems)
- Digitising as fixed (1,713 rows, ten sizes; the module rebuilds the CSV from the cached PDF, tested). Largest axis residuals 0.195 L/min on the flow chart and 0.143 kW on the power charts; the speed axis is fixed by the 1000 and 2000 r/min grid lines (frame edges up to 20.6 r/min off 0 and 3000, on size 28's chart). Sanity: the 20 bar flow at 2100 r/min is drawn **2.3 to 4.5% above geometric** on every size (the AZPG's was 0.6 to 0.8%, the AZPN's within 2%). A volumetric efficiency above one is not physical, so this is a drawing or nominal-displacement offset; it scales index 2 by the same factor and does not touch E<sub>50</sub>, which sweeps E with the shape normalised. It does matter for where the AZPU's own catalogue point sits: corrected to geometric flow, its index would be about 0.867 rather than 0.889.
- **(h) The fix:** index 2 with hold equals the model's efficiency at 85 L/min for every family (largest difference 0.0000, Danfoss excepted by 6n's rule of leaving out SNP3 090); it changes only the AZPN (0.9587 to 0.9250). The 36 six-family cells under the fixed index reproduce 6y's table with its post-hoc AZPN row exactly (0.0000).
- **The family:** efficiency at 150 bar by size 0.883 to 0.901, flat in size (k &minus;0.0016); **index 2 0.8890**; **idle power at 85 L/min 2.45 kW**, the lowest of seven (AZPN 2.49, AZPG 2.58).
- **Held out:** E<sub>50</sub> (own shape, own drag) **0.8030**. (A) 6x's rule as committed 0.8041, **error &minus;0.0011**; (B) 6y's post-hoc refit 0.8041, error &minus;0.0011; (C) one number 0.8172, error &minus;0.0142. Swaps: AZPU shape with Casappa drag 0.8197 (with AZPG drag 0.8042); AZPG shape with AZPU drag 0.8030, so the AZPU and AZPG shapes behave alike and the drag sets the crossing again.
- **Seven families crossed (49 cells):** 0.7996 to 0.8300; shape main effect 0.0097, drag 0.0203. Refit: one number 0.8135 (RMS 0.0092); **two-number E<sub>50</sub> = 0.8239 + 0.0179 (kW &minus; 3.57)** (RMS 0.0035), the 6x rule to within 0.0002 in intercept and the same slope. Leave-one-family-out (36 training cells each): diagonal errors two-number +0.0035, +0.0040, +0.0002, +0.0005, &minus;0.0027, +0.0004, &minus;0.0010, **RMS 0.0023** against 0.0120 for one number; over all 91 held-out cells 0.0037 against 0.0099; fold slopes 0.0168 to 0.0192 per kW.
- **AZ1 held** (&minus;0.0011). **AZ2 held** (0.0011 against 0.0142). **AZ3 held, narrowly** (2.45 against 2.49: outside the span by 0.04 kW, not the drag below 2 kW that 6y's Next g wanted). **AZ4 refuted** (0.8890, &minus;0.0234 from the AZPG: same maker, same sizes and housing material, yet 0.023 lower, part of it the flow-offset above). **AZ5 held** (0.0179). **AZ6 held** (0.0023 against 0.0120).
- Reading: the post-hoc reading 6y found is now the registered one, and the first family scored under it lands within 0.0011 of a rule written two families earlier. Two catalogues from one maker, one housing and one size range read 0.912 and 0.889 at the index point, which is the gap that moves the answer in the paper's terms (about 0.02 of efficiency at the 50% point), so "read the maker's own sheet for the pump you will fit" is no weaker after 6z. The rule's slope has now been tested from 2.45 to 3.64 kW; nothing yet tests it far outside that.
- Limits: still one more catalogue family, typical curves, 35 mm<sup>2</sup>/s rather than 32; the copy read is a distributor mirror of RE 10 098/02.12 (sha256 recorded), an older edition than the AZPG and AZPN sheets; the seven families' drags cluster in two groups (2.45 to 2.85 and 3.32 to 3.64 kW), so the slope is fitted mostly on the difference between them.
- Next: (g) still open, a family with drag above 4 kW or below 2 kW (candidates found this session: Rexroth AZPF, AZPJ and AZPS stop at 28 cm<sup>3</sup>/r, about 59 L/min, so they would also test the hold rule hard; Parker's PGP330 sheet prints power only at rated pressure, no drag); unchanged (a) a multi-unit measured dataset (SAE 2012-01-2041; Schänzle et al. 2018); (c) the XE80E's own pump make and auto-idle speed; (e) the journal template, nomenclature and author block, which belong to the owner.

## 2026-11-15 · Milestone 6y: a sixth gear-pump family (Bosch Rexroth AZPN) held out from every fit, then the six-family factorial (predictions written before the catalogue was digitised)
- Why: 6x refitted the two-number rule on 25 cells, E<sub>50</sub> = 0.8241 + 0.0179 (kW &minus; 3.57), and tested it leave-one-family-out, but every fold's family had already shaped the choice of rule form, index and drag feature. 6x's Next (g) asks for a sixth family as a fresh out-of-sample point. Bosch Rexroth's data sheet RE 10091/2024-11-18 (AZPN, High Performance platform N, cast aluminium, sizes 20 to 36 cm<sup>3</sup>/r) draws, on pages 14 and 15, flow at 20 bar and p<sub>2</sub> against speed for the six sizes and drive power at 50 bar up to p<sub>2</sub> against speed per size, as vector art (checked: `pdftocairo -svg` gives 374 and 382 paths and no images on those pages; the pages were looked at once at 60 dpi to confirm the chart form, and nothing was read off them). It is the AZPG's sister platform with a different housing and size range, so it also asks whether one maker's two platforms land in the same place. huggingface.co answers this session (HTTP 200, probed).
- Disclosed before the run: the largest AZPN (36 cm<sup>3</sup>/r) gives about 75 L/min at 2100 r/min, below the 85 L/min the index and the idle kW are read at, so 6k's `Gear` reads the family there (and at every larger flow) at size 36's per-unit values, as it does for every family above its largest size. The power charts carry the series 1x pressures (p<sub>2</sub> 250, 250, 250, 230, 200, 180 bar, page 9), so the dashed flow lines are taken at those too.
- Method fixed now: `experiments/digitise_rexroth_azpn.py` reuses 6w's digitiser (axes from the 1000 and 2000 r/min grid lines and a least-squares value line, residuals printed) on pages 14 and 15 into `data/rexroth_azpn_curves.csv`. `experiments/gear_pump_six.py`: (i) the AZPN family alone, as 6w did for the AZPG: index 2, idle kW at 85 L/min, E<sub>50</sub> with its own shape and own drag (E 0.70 to 0.96, step 0.0025, S = 0.38, central setting, 6k's 384 geometries), scored against the rules as committed in 6x (two-number 0.8241 + 0.0179 (kW &minus; 3.57); one-number 0.8172); (ii) the 6 &times; 6 factorial (36 cells), one- and two-number rules refitted on it, and leave-one-family-out over six families (fit on the 25 cells with neither shape nor drag h). Check: the 25 five-family cells reproduce 6x exactly.
- **Predictions:**
  - AY1: the AZPN crossing lies within 0.010 of 6x's two-number rule at its own idle kW. Prior 70%.
  - AY2: the two-number rule's error on AZPN is smaller than the one-number rule's (|E<sub>50</sub> &minus; 0.8172|). Prior 60%.
  - AY3: AZPN's idle power at 85 L/min lies outside the 2.58 to 3.64 kW the five families span. Prior 35%.
  - AY4: AZPN's index 2 lies within 0.020 of the AZPG's 0.9124. Prior 50%.
  - AY5: the 36-cell two-number slope lies in 0.016 to 0.021 per kW. Prior 75%.
  - AY6: leave-one-family-out over six families, the two-number diagonal RMS is below 0.005 and below the one-number RMS. Prior 70%.

### Outcome of 6y (`experiments/digitise_rexroth_azpn.py`, `data/rexroth_azpn_curves.csv`, `experiments/gear_pump_six.py`, `experiments/gear_pump_six_results.txt`; numpy and h5py, 39 s, rerun identical; 5 new tests, 106 in all; manuscript sections 3.5, 4, 5, 6 and the abstract updated, `check_numbers.py --paper manuscript.md` 74 claims, 0 missing, 0 orphans; `build_tex.py` 0 problems)
- Digitising, three changes from 6w's digitiser, all forced by the sheet and made before any break-even ran: (1) strokes are dark grey, not black, and grid lines 85% grey; (2) the speed grid has a line every 500 r/min, so the speed axis is a least-squares line through five lines, and the power diagrams have a value grid line every half label step (5 kW against labels every 10 kW), so the value grid spacing is chosen between the two (largest value residual 0.003 L/min and 0.001 kW; frame edges sit up to 11.9 r/min off 0 and 3000); (3) the flow lines are polylines that bend below about 1000 r/min (up to 1.22 pt off straight on sizes 25 and 36), so the polyline is sampled rather than a fitted line, and speed maps to page x through the grid axis. 6w's module gained switches for these with its defaults unchanged: it rebuilds `rexroth_azpg_curves.csv` byte for byte. 954 rows, six sizes.
- **The family:** efficiency at 150 bar by size 0.921, 0.919, 0.943, 0.912, 0.895, 0.925 (not monotone in size, as drawn; size 25's 20 bar flow is drawn 2.0% above geometric, the others within 1.6%); idle power at 85 L/min **2.49 kW**, the lowest of six. The largest size gives 75.58 L/min, short of 85.
- **As registered** (index 2 extrapolated linearly over the sizes' flows to 85 L/min, as `gear_pump_shape.index2` does): index **0.9587**, E<sub>50</sub> (own shape, own drag) **0.8342**; 6x's two-number rule says 0.8049, **error +0.0293**; one-number 0.8172, error +0.0170. The six-family factorial (36 cells, 0.8004 to 0.8595; the 25 old cells reproduce 6x exactly) has its shape main effect jump to 0.0353, all from the AZPN row; two-number refit 0.8295 + 0.0185 (kW &minus; 3.57), in-sample RMS 0.0122; leave-one-family-out diagonal RMS 0.0131 two-number against 0.0127 one-number, the AZPN fold +0.0294.
- **AY1 refuted** (+0.0293). **AY2 refuted** (0.0293 against 0.0170). **AY3 held, narrowly** (2.49 against 2.58). **AY4 refuted** (0.9587, +0.0463 from the AZPG). **AY5 held** (0.0185). **AY6 refuted** (0.0131 against 0.0127).
- **Diagnosis (post hoc, found after the run, labelled so in the results file):** `index2` extrapolates efficiency past a family's largest size, while the model (`Gear`) holds the largest size's per-unit values there. For the five earlier families, which all reach 85 L/min, the two readings agree exactly (largest difference 0.0000); the AZPN is the first family that stops short, and its last two sizes (0.895 at 67 L/min, 0.925 at 76 L/min) extrapolate to 0.959, above anything the sheet draws. Because the crossing is in units of the index, it moves by the ratio of the readings (indices 0.9648, crossings 0.9650). With the model's own efficiency at 85 L/min (0.9250) as the index: AZPN E<sub>50</sub> **0.8050**, 6x's rule as committed **0.8049 (error +0.0001)**, one-number error &minus;0.0122; the AZPN shape row falls to 0.8050 to 0.8289, in line with the others; the 36-cell refit is 0.8245 + 0.0183 (kW &minus; 3.57) (in-sample RMS 0.0037) and the six held-out diagonals' two-number errors are +0.0029, +0.0036, &minus;0.0005, +0.0003, &minus;0.0030, +0.0002 (RMS 0.0023, against 0.0120 for one number).
- Reading: the registered test failed, and the reason is the index, not the drag rule. A family whose sizes stop short of the index point has no measured efficiency there; extrapolating a non-monotone size trend invented one 0.034 higher than any size the maker draws, and the rule duly missed by that factor. Read on the size the model actually runs, the AZPN lands on 6x's line almost exactly (+0.0001), and its drag, the lowest yet, extends the range the slope is tested over to 2.49 kW. The agreement rests on a reading chosen after the failure, so it is a diagnosis to be confirmed on a seventh family, not a confirmation. For the manuscript's design rule this adds one clause: read the efficiency on a size the maker builds, at the flow the machine needs, and never extrapolate a catalogue past its largest size.
- Limits: the post-hoc reading was not registered; the AZPN's per-size efficiencies are not monotone as drawn, so which size the index is read at matters for this family more than for any other; drag 2.49 kW is only 0.09 kW below the AZPG's, so 6x's Next (g), a drag above 4 kW or below 2 kW, is still open; the sheet's dashed flow lines were taken at the series 1x p<sub>2</sub>, which the index does not use.
- Next: (h) fix `index2` to hold, not extrapolate, past a family's largest size (a one-line change that leaves the five earlier families unchanged), and register it before (g); (g) a seventh family with drag above 4 kW or below 2 kW, digitised under the fixed index with predictions written first; unchanged (a) a multi-unit measured dataset (SAE 2012-01-2041; Schänzle et al. 2018); (c) the XE80E's own pump make and auto-idle speed; (e) the journal template, nomenclature and author block, which belong to the owner.

## 2026-11-15 · Milestone 6x: the five-family factorial and a leave-one-family-out test of the drag rule (predictions written before the sweep ran)
- Why: 6w's AZPG point confirmed 6v's two-number rule, but that rule's intercept and slope both come from the Casappa shape (6v), and the 6u factorial it was scored on spans idle drags of only 2.85 to 3.64 kW. 6w's Next (f) asks for the rule refitted on all five families crossed (25 cells), and a reviewer will ask how well the rule predicts a family it has never seen, in shape *and* in drag, when every family takes a turn as the held-out one. huggingface.co answers this session (HTTP 200, probed).
- Method fixed now (`experiments/gear_pump_five.py`, 6u's and 6w's machinery unchanged): build the 5 &times; 5 factorial (moving shape f, idle drag g; Casappa, Danfoss, Marzocchi, bench KP30, Rexroth AZPG), E swept 0.70 to 0.96 in steps of 0.0025 at S = 0.38 and the central setting over 6k's 384 geometries, E<sub>50</sub> by linear interpolation. Check: the 16 old cells reproduce 6u (on its 0.74 grid start the crossings are the same interpolation, so within 0.0005) and AZPG's diagonal reproduces 6w's 0.8040. Fit three rules by least squares on all 25 cells: one-number (a constant), two-number E<sub>50</sub> = a + b kW (kW: drag family's idle power at 85 L/min), and an exploratory three-number rule adding the shape's size slope k<sub>f</sub> = &eta;<sub>f</sub>(150 L/min, 150 bar) / E<sub>f</sub> &minus; 1 (6u found size slope the shape feature that matters). Leave-one-family-out: for each family h, fit each rule on the 16 cells with neither shape h nor drag h, and predict the diagonal (h, h) and all 9 cells that involve h; report per-family errors and the RMS over the five diagonals.
- **Predictions:**
  - FX1: the 25-cell two-number slope b lies in 0.016 to 0.021 per kW. Prior 65%.
  - FX2: the 25-cell two-number fit, evaluated at 3.57 kW, lies within 0.003 of 6v's 0.8277. Prior 55%.
  - FX3: every held-out diagonal's two-number error is within 0.010. Prior 60%.
  - FX4: over the five held-out diagonals, the two-number RMS is below the one-number RMS. Prior 80%.
  - FX5: the three-number rule cuts the held-out diagonal RMS by at least 20% against the two-number rule. Prior 35%.
  - FX6: all 25 cells lie within 0.020 of 6r's pooled 0.8212. Prior 60%.

### Outcome of 6x (`experiments/gear_pump_five.py`, `experiments/gear_pump_five_results.txt`; numpy and h5py, 39 s, rerun identical; 4 new tests, 101 in all; manuscript sections 3.5, 4, 5, 6 and the abstract updated, `check_numbers.py --paper manuscript.md` 64 claims, 0 missing, 0 orphans; `build_tex.py` 0 problems for both sources)
- Method as fixed. Checks: the 16 four-family cells reproduce 6u exactly (largest difference 0.0000) and the AZPG diagonal reproduces 6w's 0.8040.
- **The 5 &times; 5 factorial:** all 25 cells 0.8010 to 0.8300; the AZPG drag column (2.58 kW) is the lowest on every shape (0.8010 to 0.8108). Shape main effect 0.0096, **drag main effect 0.0194** (6u's four families: 0.0095 and 0.0138), so with a wider drag range drag is twice shape.
- **Fits on 25 cells:** constant 0.8172 (RMS 0.0084); **two-number E<sub>50</sub> = 0.8241 + 0.0179 (kW &minus; 3.57)** (RMS 0.0040); three-number adds +0.0650 &times; size slope (RMS 0.0030). 6v's committed rule scores RMS 0.0052 on the 25 cells.
- **Leave-one-family-out** (fit on the 16 cells with neither shape nor drag of h): diagonal errors one-number +0.0130, +0.0141, +0.0026, &minus;0.0073, &minus;0.0166; **two-number +0.0037, +0.0038, &minus;0.0002, +0.0006, &minus;0.0042** (Casappa, Danfoss, Marzocchi, bench, AZPG); three-number +0.0015, +0.0049, +0.0069, &minus;0.0008, &minus;0.0056. RMS over the five diagonals 0.0119, **0.0030**, 0.0046; over all 45 held-out cells 0.0094, 0.0044, 0.0038. The two-number fold slopes run 0.0162 to 0.0198 per kW, intercepts at 3.57 kW 0.8237 to 0.8247.
- **FX1 held** (0.0179). **FX2 refuted** (&minus;0.0036 against a 0.003 bar: Casappa's own cell sits above the five-family line). **FX3 held** (worst 0.0042). **FX4 held** (0.0030 against 0.0119). **FX5 refuted** (the shape term makes held-out diagonals worse, 0.0046 against 0.0030, though it trims the 45-cell RMS from 0.0044 to 0.0038). **FX6 refuted, narrowly** (0.0202 against 0.020: Marzocchi shape with AZPG drag).
- Reading: with every family taking a turn as the unseen one, efficiency plus drag predicts the family's crossing within 0.0042, four times better than one number (RMS 0.0030 against 0.0119). The rule the paper now states is the refit, E<sub>50</sub> = 0.824 + 0.018 (kW &minus; 3.57); it differs from 6v's Casappa-only calibration by 0.0036 in intercept, which is the Casappa shape's own offset. Shape (as the size slope) earns no third number: it fits the cells better but predicts a left-out family worse, a classic overfit with five folds. For a builder: read the efficiency at 150 bar and the zero-pressure power; nothing else on the data sheet moves the answer by more than about 0.004.
- Limits: five families are five folds, so the held-out errors are five numbers and not a distribution; drag enters only as the 85 L/min idle kW (its size dependence is each family's own); everything else is 6k's method.
- Next: unchanged (a) a multi-unit measured dataset (SAE 2012-01-2041; Schänzle et al. 2018), read for efficiency at 150 bar and no-load drag, now to be placed on the refitted rule; (c) the XE80E's own pump make and auto-idle speed; (e) the journal template, nomenclature and author block, which belong to the owner. New: (g) a sixth catalogue family with a drag outside 2.58 to 3.64 kW (above 4 kW or below 2 kW) would test the slope where the folds have not been.

### Outcome of 6w (`experiments/digitise_rexroth_azpg.py`, `data/rexroth_azpg_curves.csv`, `experiments/gear_pump_rexroth.py`, `experiments/gear_pump_rexroth_results.txt`; numpy and h5py, 32 s, rerun identical; 4 new tests, 97 in all, all pass with the motion cache present; manuscript sections 3.5, 4, 5, 6 and the abstract updated, `check_numbers.py --paper manuscript.md` 54 claims, 0 missing, 0 orphans; `build_tex.py` 0 problems for both sources)

That is about 0.5 pt on the chart, and a volumetric efficiency above one at 20 bar is not physical, so it is a drawing or nominal-displacement offset; it scales index 2 by the same factor and does not touch E<sub>50</sub>, which sweeps E with the shape normalised.

- **E<sub>50</sub> (own shape, own drag) 0.8040.** Check: the Casappa diagonal reproduces 6u's 0.8277 exactly. Diagnostics: AZPG shape with Casappa drag 0.8207; Casappa shape with AZPG drag 0.8108.

- Reading: the first family that no fit, index or form choice ever saw lands where the two-number rule says, and outside the band the one-number rule claimed. So 6v's second number is not a refinement but needed: the manuscript's design rule no longer says the 50% point moves "by no more than 0.015" with shape or drag; it says so for the four families it was built on, and that a pump whose drag is far from the Casappa catalogue's needs the drag term. The swap shows the reason is drag: the AZPG's shape with Casappa's drag sits 0.0005 from the pooled number, while the AZPG drag on the Casappa shape moves the crossing down by 0.0169, close to the 0.0185 the rule's slope gives for 0.99 kW. Separately, a second maker's catalogue now reads as high as the bench test (0.912 against 0.913), so selection curves are not uniformly pessimistic: the gap to the bench was 0.15 for Casappa's own sheet and is about zero for this one, which is one more reason the paper asks for a test, not a catalogue.

## 2026-11-15 · Milestone 6v: how much is a kilowatt of drag worth? A continuous idle-drag sweep on all four measured shapes, and a two-number selection rule tested on 6u's 16 cells (predictions written before the sweep ran)
- Why: 6u found idle drag moves the 50% crossing more than curve shape (drag main effect 0.0138 against 0.0095) and estimated +0.016 E<sub>50</sub> per kW from four column means, four points with shape averaged out. The manuscript's design rule says "read the no-load drag as well", but with no number a builder can use. In the model the idle term enters the break-even only as B (p<sub>ls</sub> &minus; p<sub>oc</sub>) h (6g, `fcurve`), so the effect should be close to linear in the fixed pump's idle power; whether it is, and whether its slope is the same for every shape, has not been run. huggingface.co answers this session (HTTP 200, probed).
- Method fixed now (`experiments/gear_pump_drag.py`, 6u's machinery unchanged): for each of the four moving shapes f (Casappa catalogue, Danfoss SNP3, Marzocchi ALP3, bench KP30) with Casappa's idle-power curve over size multiplied by d &isin; {0, 0.25, 0.5, 0.75, 1, 1.25, 1.5, 2}, sweep E from 0.70 to 0.96 in steps of 0.0025 at S = 0.38 and the central setting over 6k's 384 geometries and read E<sub>50</sub>. Express the drag as idle kW at 85 L/min (d &times; 3.57 kW) and as an equivalent drag pressure (kW &times; 600 / 85 bar). Fit E<sub>50</sub> = a<sub>f</sub> + b<sub>f</sub> kW per shape by least squares. The two-number rule, calibrated on the Casappa shape alone: E<sub>50</sub>(kW) = E<sub>50</sub>(Casappa, d = 1) + b<sub>Casappa</sub> (kW &minus; 3.57); score it on 6u's 16 measured cells (each cell's idle kW at 85 L/min from 6u) by RMS error, against the one-number rule (every cell 0.8212, 6r's pooled crossing).
- **Predictions:**
  - DR1: on the Casappa shape, E<sub>50</sub> is linear in idle kW over d = 0 to 2: the linear fit's largest residual is at most 0.002. Prior 60%.
  - DR2: b<sub>Casappa</sub> lies in 0.012 to 0.022 per kW. Prior 65%.
  - DR3: the four shapes' slopes b<sub>f</sub> span at most 0.004 per kW. Prior 55%.
  - DR4: on 6u's 16 cells the two-number rule's RMS error is at most 0.006 and below the one-number rule's. Prior 55%.
  - DR5: a pump with no idle drag at all (d = 0, Casappa shape) still needs E<sub>50</sub> of at least 0.75. Prior 70%.

### Outcome of 6v (`experiments/gear_pump_drag.py`, `experiments/gear_pump_drag_results.txt`; numpy and h5py, 36 s, rerun identical; 3 new tests, 93 in all, all pass with the motion cache present; manuscript sections 3.5 and 4 updated, `check_numbers.py --paper manuscript.md` 46 claims, 0 missing, 0 orphans; `build_tex.py` 0 problems for both sources)
- Method as fixed, plus one line printing the Casappa slope rounded to four places (so the manuscript's numbers trace to a results file). Check: the d = 1 column reproduces 6u's Casappa-idle column exactly (0.8277, 0.8217, 0.8175, 0.8216; tested).
- **E<sub>50</sub> by drag (d = 0 to 2, idle 0 to 7.14 kW at 85 L/min):** Casappa shape 0.7647 to 0.8983, Danfoss 0.7607 to 0.8933, Marzocchi 0.7580 to 0.8833, bench 0.7615 to 0.8900; every row rises at every step. Linear slopes **0.0187, 0.0185, 0.0175, 0.0180 per kW** (span 0.0012), i.e. about 0.0026 per bar of equivalent drag pressure at 85 L/min. The line is slightly convex on every shape (residuals positive at both ends, negative in the middle), largest 0.0022 (Casappa) to 0.0035 (Danfoss).
- Two-number rule E<sub>50</sub> = 0.8277 + 0.0187 (kW &minus; 3.57), calibrated on the Casappa shape only, on 6u's 16 cells: **RMS 0.0053**, worst +0.0102 (Marzocchi shape with Casappa idle); the one-number 0.8212: RMS 0.0066, worst +0.0143 (Marzocchi shape, bench idle). Diagnostic, not predicted: with each shape's own d = 1 crossing and slope, RMS 0.0037, so what the two-number rule leaves is the shape intercept (Marzocchi's shape sits about 0.008 low).
- **DR1 refuted, narrowly** (largest residual 0.0022 against 0.002; the curvature is real and the same sign on every shape). **DR2 held** (0.0187). **DR3 held** (0.0012). **DR4 held** (0.0053, below 0.0066). **DR5 held** (0.7647).
- Reading: a kilowatt of no-load drag at 85 L/min costs about 0.019 of efficiency at 150 bar, whatever the shape of the pump's map, and the dependence is nearly a straight line from no drag to twice the catalogue's. That turns 6u's "read the drag as well" into a number a builder can use: a pump that drags 1 kW less than the Casappa catalogue needs about 0.81 rather than 0.83 to win in half the geometries. Drag cannot do it alone: a pump with no idle drag at all still needs about 0.76 (0.758 to 0.765 over the four shapes), so moving efficiency stays the first number. The gain over the one-number rule on the measured cells is modest (RMS 0.0053 against 0.0066) because the four measured drags span only 2.85 to 3.64 kW; the slope matters for pumps outside that range. The convexity means the rule slightly under-predicts the crossing at both ends of the drag range (by up to 0.0035).
- Limits: the drag is varied by scaling one family's idle-power curve over size, so the size dependence of drag is Casappa's; the rule is calibrated on one shape's intercept; everything else is 6k's method.
- Next: unchanged (a) a multi-unit measured dataset (SAE 2012-01-2041; Schänzle et al. 2018), read for efficiency at 150 bar and no-load drag, now to be placed on the two-number rule; (c) the XE80E's own pump make and auto-idle speed; (e) the journal template, nomenclature and author block, which belong to the owner.

- Why: 6r's limits say "the sweeps scale each family's moving efficiency uniformly, so the curve maps efficiency level, not curve shape, to the share". The manuscript's one curve uses a single number per pump (index 2: overall efficiency at 150 bar, 2100 r/min, 85 L/min of 20 bar flow), but a real pump differs from another in more than that number: how its efficiency moves with pressure (relative change per 100 bar, 100 to 200 bar, at 85 L/min: Casappa catalogue +0.010, bench KP30 +0.016, Marzocchi +0.039, Danfoss +0.044), how it moves with size (150 bar, 85 to 150 L/min: Casappa +3.3%, bench 0, Danfoss &minus;2.8%, Marzocchi &minus;8.7%), and its idle drag. A reviewer will ask whether the three makers fall on one curve because index 2 is sufficient or by luck of compensating shapes. huggingface.co answers this session (HTTP 200, probed), so the break-even can be rerun (6k reproduced byte for byte, 19 s).
- Method fixed now (`experiments/gear_pump_shape.py`): for each family f (Casappa catalogue 6k, Danfoss SNP3 6m with all ten sizes as 6n swept it, Marzocchi ALP3 6o, the bench-shaped KP30 6q) its shape &phi;<sub>f</sub>(q, P) = &eta;<sub>f</sub>(q, P) / E<sub>f</sub>, E<sub>f</sub> its index 2. A pump with index E, shape f and idle drag g has moving efficiency E &phi;<sub>f</sub> and family g's idle power (so (f, f) is exactly the committed c sweep of family f). For all 16 (shape, idle) cells sweep E from 0.74 to 0.96 in steps of 0.0025 at S = 0.38 and the central setting over 6k's 384 geometries, and read the 50% crossing E<sub>50</sub> by linear interpolation. Synthetic shapes on the Casappa cell: a pressure tilt &eta; = E &phi; (1 + t (P &minus; 150)/100) with t = &minus;0.05, +0.05, +0.10 (the four families span +0.010 to +0.044), and a size slope &eta; = E &phi; (1 + k (q &minus; 85)/85), k = &minus;0.10, +0.10 (they span about &minus;0.10 to +0.04 over 85 to 150 L/min); efficiency capped at 0.99. Report the shape main effect (range over shapes of the cell E<sub>50</sub> averaged over idle) and the drag main effect (the reverse), and each family's idle power at 85 L/min.
- Check (not a prediction): the diagonal cells reproduce the committed crossings, Casappa 0.8284 (6q grid), Danfoss 0.8309 (6n), Marzocchi 0.8188 (6o), within 0.002.

  - SH1: with Casappa's idle held, the four moving shapes' E<sub>50</sub> span at most 0.010. Prior 60%.
  - SH2: with Casappa's shape held, the four idle drags' E<sub>50</sub> span at least 0.010. Prior 50%.
  - SH3: all 16 cells' E<sub>50</sub> lie within 0.015 of 6r's pooled 0.8212. Prior 55%.
  - SH4: the pressure tilts t = &plusmn;0.05 each move the Casappa E<sub>50</sub> by at most 0.005. Prior 60%.
  - SH5: the size slopes k = &plusmn;0.10 each move the Casappa E<sub>50</sub> by at most 0.010. Prior 50%.

Swapping any of four measured shapes and any of four measured idle drags moves the crossing by at most 0.015, the same size as the maker-bootstrap interval of 6t, so the paper's 0.82 survives curve shape too. What moves it most is not shape but idle drag: a pump that drags less when the valves are in neutral wins sooner, about 0.016 of efficiency per kW at 85 L/min. Of shape, the size slope matters (an efficiency that falls with size moves the crossing down) and the pressure tilt hardly at all, because at the sizes the optimum picks the pump works near 136 bar, close to the index point. ## 2026-11-15 · Milestone 6t: is the one curve an artefact of the logistic? Three other link functions, a model-free crossing, and an exact maker-level bootstrap interval on E<sub>50</sub> (predictions written before any alternative form was fitted)
- Why: 6r's limits say "the logistic is a chosen form", and the manuscript's headline (one curve crossing 50% at 0.821, nine geometries in ten above about 0.88, load-sensing below about 0.76) rests on that form and carries no interval. A reviewer will ask both. This session's proxy refuses huggingface.co (CONNECT 403, probed), so no break-even can be rerun; this step needs none, since it reads only the 29 committed sweep points 6r fitted.
- Method fixed now (`experiments/gear_pump_form.py`, stdlib, reuses 6r's parsers): fit four two-parameter forms, each written so its location parameter is the 50% crossing E<sub>50</sub>: logistic (6r), probit 100 &Phi;((E &minus; E<sub>50</sub>)/s), complementary log-log 100 (1 &minus; exp(&minus;ln2 &middot; exp((E &minus; E<sub>50</sub>)/b))) (slow start, steep top) and log-log 100 exp(&minus;ln2 &middot; exp(&minus;(E &minus; E<sub>50</sub>)/b)) (steep start, slow top), by grid least squares (E<sub>50</sub> 0.75 to 0.90, scale 0.002 to 0.10, coarse step 1e-3, then 1e-4 around the best). For each: pooled RMS, leave-one-maker-out crossing errors and RMS as in 6r, and the E at 10% and 90% (the design rule's two thresholds). Model-free: the pooled points' isotonic (pool-adjacent-violators) fit, crossing read by linear interpolation. Interval: every one of the 3<sup>3</sup> = 27 ways of drawing three makers with replacement (each maker's points kept whole), the logistic refitted on each, E<sub>50</sub>'s range and its 27-draw median reported (an exact cluster bootstrap; with three clusters it is coarse and says so).
- **Predictions:**
  - FF1: every form's pooled E<sub>50</sub> lies within 0.005 of the logistic's 0.8212. Prior 75%.
  - FF2: an asymmetric form (cloglog or log-log) beats the logistic's pooled RMS of 5.62 by at least 0.5 points. Prior 40%.
  - FF3: for every form, every left-out maker's crossing error is within 0.015 (6r's GE2 bar). Prior 65%.
  - FF4: across the four forms, the 10% and the 90% thresholds each span at most 0.010. Prior 50%.
  - FF5: all 27 bootstrap E<sub>50</sub> lie in 0.810 to 0.835. Prior 70%.
  - FF6: the model-free isotonic crossing lies within 0.005 of 0.8212. Prior 60%.

- Why: 6q's Next (e). The draft's abstract had grown to about 900 words, one sentence per milestone, and the draft had no conclusions section; a venue (IJFP) wants about 250 words and a stated answer. The answer the excavator half now gives is one curve: the share of geometries in which a smaller fixed gear pump beats load-sensing, as a function of that pump's overall efficiency at 150 bar and the optimum size. 6n and 6o showed three makers' 50% crossings lie within 0.012 of each other, but each crossing was interpolated on its own maker's sweep. Whether one function of efficiency alone predicts a maker it was not fitted on, across the whole sweep and not only at 50%, has not been tested. The network for a second measured unit was tried first: Zardin et al. 2019 (Energies 12:2468, fetched) report only model-versus-measurement errors for unnamed pumps; Michael et al. 2012 (SAE 2012-01-2041, 16 pumps, seven makers, modified ISO 4409) is paywalled; Schänzle et al. 2018 (seven gear pumps, TUprints) is behind a bot wall, so it was not fetched.
- Method fixed now (`experiments/gear_pump_evidence.py`, stdlib, reads only committed results files, no simulation): every scaled-sweep point on index 2 (efficiency at 150 bar, 2100 r/min, 85 L/min of 20 bar flow) from Casappa (the finer 6q grid), Danfoss (6m/6n) and Marzocchi (6o), with each maker's own idle drag, at S = 0.38 and the central setting. Fit share = 100 / (1 + exp(-(E - E50) / w)) by least squares on a grid (E50 0.75 to 0.90 step 0.0001, w 0.002 to 0.05 step 0.0001), pooled and leaving each maker out; the left-out maker's crossing is read from the fit on the other two. Then place the unscaled catalogue points, the piston pump (6j: 0.840, 74.0%; its efficiency is at full stroke, not on index 2, so a check, not a fit point) and the bench pump (6q: 0.913, 100.0%).
- **Predictions:**
  - GE1: the pooled E50 lies in 0.820 to 0.835. Prior 80%.
  - GE2: leaving each maker out, the other two's fit puts the left-out maker's 50% crossing within 0.015 of its own interpolated crossing (6n/6o/6q: Casappa 0.8283, Danfoss 0.8309, Marzocchi 0.8188), for all three. Prior 65%.
  - GE3: the pooled fit's RMS residual over all sweep points is under 6 percentage points. Prior 60%.
  - GE4: leaving each maker out, the left-out maker's RMS residual is under 10 points for all three. Prior 55%.
  - GE5: the pooled curve predicts the piston point's 74.0% to within 15 points at 0.840. Prior 55%.

### Outcome of 6r (`experiments/gear_pump_evidence.py`, `experiments/gear_pump_evidence_results.txt`, `experiments/gear_pump_evidence_figure.py`, `experiments/gear_pump_evidence.svg`; stdlib, 0.1 s, rerun identical; 4 new tests, 79 in all (78 pass, 1 skipped as before); paper: new abstract, section 3.7r, Figure 7, section 6, Appendix A; `check_numbers.py` 339 claims, 0 missing; `build_tex.py` 7 figures, 0 problems)
- Method as fixed, no changes. Points read: Casappa 11 (6q grid, E 0.7590 to 0.9487), Danfoss 7, Marzocchi 11. Casappa's own interpolated crossing reads 0.8284 here against 6q's printed 0.8283, because this script interpolates on the 4-decimal index values the results file prints.
- **Pooled (29 points): E50 0.8212, w 0.0270, RMS residual 5.62 points** (Casappa 4.19, Danfoss 5.60, Marzocchi 6.75; largest 13.43, on Marzocchi, whose rise is steeper than the symmetric curve). 10% to 90% of geometries between E 0.7619 and 0.8805.
- **Leave one maker out:** crossing errors &minus;0.0091 (Casappa), &minus;0.0118 (Danfoss), +0.0062 (Marzocchi); left-out RMS 5.05, 6.83, 8.91 points.
- Unscaled points against the pooled curve: Casappa catalogue 9.9% (curve 9.1), Danfoss 70.1% (73.3), Marzocchi 99.5% (94.4), the bench family 100.0% (96.8), the piston pump 74.0% (66.7, +7.3; not on index 2).
- **All five predictions held** (GE1 0.8212, GE2 worst 0.0118, GE3 5.62, GE4 8.91, GE5 +7.3). GE1 and GE2 were close to what 6n and 6o already implied; GE3 and GE4 are the new information: one curve fits the whole sweep, not only the crossing, out of sample to about one geometry in ten.
- Paper: the abstract is now 272 words around this curve (the previous 870-word abstract moved to Appendix A, unchanged), section 3.7r and Figure 7 present the fit, and section 6 states five conclusions, including the design rule (above about 0.88 at 150 bar a smaller fixed pump wins in nine geometries of ten, below about 0.76 load-sensing does) and what would overturn it.
- Search for a second measured unit, done before the fit: Zardin, Natali and Borghi 2019 (Energies 12:2468, fetched from iris.unimore.it) give only model-error plots, citing Michael, Khalid and Wanke 2012 (SAE 2012-01-2041; 16 external gear pumps, seven makers, modified ISO 4409) for the measurements; that paper is paywalled and its ResearchGate copy refuses (403). Schänzle, Störmer and Pelz 2018 (Bath/ASME FPMC; seven gear pumps of different displacement) is on TUprints behind a bot wall, which was respected. Chiavola et al. (FPMC 2024) and Milani et al. (IJFP 2024) were fetched and test small pumps at very low speed or 45 bar, outside the class.
- Limits: the sweeps scale each family's moving efficiency uniformly, so the curve maps efficiency level, not curve shape, to the share; the points are three families, not independent draws; the logistic is a chosen form.
- Next: (a) a multi-unit measured dataset (the two papers above, through a library or the authors) read at 150 bar and 30 to 50 cm3/r, to place typical units on Figure 7; (c) the XE80E's own pump make and auto-idle speed; (e) the paper: trim sections 3.1 to 3.7q toward the venue's 8 to 12 pages around the new abstract, and choose the venue.

## 2026-11-14 · Milestone 6q: the excavator break-even rerun with the bench-measured KP30 as the fixed pump, so 6p's share is computed rather than bounded (predictions written before the sweep ran)
- Why: 6p's Next (b). 6p put the one bench test found (Frosina et al. 2017, Casappa KP30, 44 cm3/r) at 0.913 overall at 150 bar and 2100 r/min, and bounded the excavator result from below with the committed Casappa sweep (94.8% of 384 geometries at c = 1.15, the bench needing c = 1.195). The bound says nothing about the bench pump's idle drag, which 6m showed moves the crossing by about 0.02, nor about how the bench shape (low friction at high pressure, more at 50 bar) differs from scaling the catalogue curves. huggingface.co answers (HTTP 200, probed), so the sweep can be rerun.
- Method fixed now (`experiments/excavator_gear_pump_bench.py`, numpy and h5py, 6o's machinery unchanged): a bench-shaped family written in the catalogue schema, one row set per Casappa KAPPA size (6k's displacements), so `Gear` and `measured.gear_pump_point` read it unchanged. Torque at 50, 150 and 250 bar = the measured Figure 11 torque times V/44 (loss torque proportional to displacement, so every size has the bench's efficiency map), linear in speed between 1000, 1500 and 2500 r/min, drawn from 1000 to 2500 r/min; power = torque times speed; the curves are continued below 50 bar along the 50 to 150 bar line, as for the catalogues. Flow lines at 20 and 250 bar from Figure 13's volumetric efficiency (6p's base, 2500 r/min times V), linear in pressure through 50 and 150 bar for the 20 bar line, linear in speed between 1500 and 2500 and held outside. Variants: bench (moving and idle), bench moving with Casappa catalogue idle, Casappa moving with bench idle, bench with Figure 12's larger friction (ideal torque plus Figure 12's delta), and the Casappa catalogue scaled by c on a finer grid (1.06 to 1.10, 1.12, 1.15, 1.195, 1.25) to place 6n's Casappa crossing and to compute the c = 1.195 share directly. S = 0.38, central setting, then all 18 idle settings for the bench family. I have seen 6p's torque and flow tables (so the bench drag in GQ3 is not blind) but have not run any break-even with them.
- **Predictions:**
  - GQ1: the Casappa catalogue scaled by c = 1.195 gives a share at S = 0.38 of at least 97%. Prior 75%.
  - GQ2: the bench family (moving and idle) gives a share at S = 0.38 of at least 95%. Prior 70%.
  - GQ3: the bench family's zero-pressure drag at 2100 r/min is below the KP 30.43 catalogue's 8.21 bar of 20 bar flow. Prior 75% (not blind).
  - GQ4: the bench family's central f_t,max at S = 0.38 is at least 6.0 L/h (Marzocchi 6.161). Prior 55%.
  - GQ5: bench moving with the catalogue's idle drag still gives at least 90%. Prior 70%.
  - GQ6: the bench family's share at S = 0.38 is at least 95% in every one of the 18 idle settings. Prior 50%.
  - GQ7: on the finer grid, Casappa's index-2 crossing lies within 0.005 of 6n's 0.8281. Prior 70%.

With Figure 12's friction: 0.922 at 150 bar, drag 4.60 bar.

Figure 12's friction: 100.0% (central 9.74). The optimum fixed pump is a median 85 L/min.
- Moving efficiency, not idle drag, carries it: bench moving with the catalogue's idle drag 100.0% (central 8.17 L/h); catalogue moving with the bench's idle drag 13.3% (catalogue alone 9.9%).

- **All seven predictions held** (GQ1 100.0%, GQ2 100.0%, GQ3 6.67 bar, GQ4 8.82 L/h, GQ5 100.0%, GQ6 lowest 100.0%, GQ7 +0.0002). Every bar was set below where 6p's bound already pointed, so these were weak tests; the information is in the size of the margins, not in the hits.
- Reading: on the one measured pump of the class, the excavator answer is no longer close. A smaller fixed gear pump on an open-centre valve beats load-sensing on fuel in every geometry and every idle setting this programme has tried, by a wide margin (break-even rest-of-machine rate a median 8.1 L/h against no-load rates of 3.99 to 5.06). The ranking would only return to a coin toss if typical units of the class run about 0.08 below this one (the 0.83 crossing), which is about the gap between this unit and its own catalogue. So the open question is now entirely unit-to-unit spread and catalogue conservatism, not the model.
- Limits: one unit; the family assumes loss torque and leakage proportional to displacement (every size carries the 44 cm3/r map), which matters little because the optimum picks 85 to 108 L/min, near the tested size (44 cm3/r is 92 L/min at 2100 r/min); the bench's 1000 to 2500 r/min span covers idle at 0.5 of working speed (1050 r/min) only just; flow below 1500 r/min held at the 1500 r/min volumetric efficiency.
- Next: (a) an independent ISO 4409 test, or any second unit's measured map, of a 30 to 50 cm3/r gear pump; (c) the XE80E's own pump make and auto-idle speed; (e) the paper: with the excavator answer computed on a measured pump, the draft's abstract and conclusion can be rewritten around the 0.83 threshold and the catalogue-versus-bench gap, ready for a venue choice.

## 2026-11-14 · Milestone 6p: a bench-measured Casappa KP30 (44 cm3/r) at 150 bar against the 0.83 crux and against Casappa's own catalogue curves (predictions written after the figures were seen, before any pixel was read)
- Why: 6o's Next (a): every efficiency the crux has been tested against is a maker's selection curve; none is a test. Frosina, Senatore and Rigosi (Energies 10(8):1113, 2017, CC BY; one author at Casappa S.p.A.) tested a Casappa KP30 of 44 cm3/r nominal displacement on Casappa's bench at 1000, 1500 and 2500 r/min and 50, 150 and 250 bar, oil at 50 and 80 C, with a torque meter on the shaft (their Figure 11, absorbed torque, measured against CFD) and a flowmeter (their Figure 13, delivery flow normalised, measured against CFD). That is the same maker and nearly the same size (KP 30.43, 43.98 cm3/r) as 6k's catalogue curves, which put it at 0.764 overall at 150 bar and 2100 r/min (hydro-mechanical 0.801, volumetric 0.989). The PDF (pdfs.semanticscholar.org, HTTP 200; mdpi.com refuses at the proxy, 403) draws both figures as raster images, so they are read by pixel, not from vector paths.
- Disclosure: I viewed both figures at screen resolution before writing these predictions, and by eye the measured torque at 150 bar is about 109 Nm against an ideal V p / 2 pi of 105 Nm. GB1 and GB2 are therefore not blind; GB3 to GB6 test the reading and its consistency.
- Method fixed now (`experiments/kp30_bench.py`, stdlib plus Pillow): extract the figure images (pdfimages), locate each panel's axes from its gridlines and tick positions, read the marker centroids of the measured (solid, square) and model (dotted, circle) series at 50, 150 and 250 bar, and convert to Nm; read Figure 13's measured markers in normalised flow. Hydro-mechanical efficiency = V p / (2 pi T) with V = 44 cm3/r and p the delivery gauge pressure (inlet 0.7 to 3 bar absolute, Table 1, ignored); volumetric efficiency from Figure 13, normalised as the figure states; overall = product; 2100 r/min by linear interpolation in speed between 1500 and 2500 r/min. Compare with 6k's KP 30.43 row and place on 6n/6o's 0.82 to 0.83 crux and Casappa's committed c sweep.
- **Predictions:**
  - GB1: the bench overall efficiency at 150 bar and 2100 r/min (50 C) is above 0.83. Prior 85% (not blind).
  - GB2: the bench hydro-mechanical efficiency at 150 bar exceeds the catalogue's 0.801 by at least 0.10. Prior 75% (not blind).
  - GB3: the CFD model torque at 150 bar (frictionless, pressure only) is within 2% of V p / 2 pi = 105.0 Nm at all three speeds (a check on the reading and on V). Prior 75%.
  - GB4: measured minus model torque at 150 bar, as I read it from Figure 11, agrees with the paper's own Figure 12 delta to within 1 Nm at each speed. Prior 65%.
  - GB5: measured volumetric efficiency at 150 bar, 2500 r/min, 50 C is between 0.95 and 0.99. Prior 55%.
  - GB6: friction torque (measured minus model) rises with speed at 150 bar (1000 < 1500 < 2500 r/min). Prior 70%.

### Outcome of 6p (`experiments/digitise_frosina_kp30.py`, `experiments/data/frosina2017_kp30_bench.csv`, `experiments/kp30_bench.py`, `experiments/kp30_bench_results.txt`; stdlib, about 2 s, rerun from a fresh fetch identical; 3 new tests, 72 in all; paper section 3.7p, abstract, threat 7, settle (h); `check_numbers.py` 314 claims, 0 missing)
- Changes to the stated method: the image's Pillow install is broken (`_imaging` missing), so the figures are read from pdfimages' PPM output with a stdlib parser instead; the digitiser is its own script (`digitise_frosina_kp30.py`) and `kp30_bench.py` only analyses its CSV. Reading fixes made before any efficiency was printed: the first Figure 11 pass read the frame's bottom edge (30 Nm) as the model line, so the plot band now stops at 31.5 Nm; Figure 13's x axis is fitted through the frame edge (0.5) and the four gridlines, after a first fit had paired them one step off; Figure 13 is read only at 50 C, because on the 80 C panel the grey model squares hide the black measured markers at 50 bar.
- Torque at 150 bar: measured 109.08, 110.72, 113.25 Nm at 1000, 1500, 2500 r/min; ideal V p / 2 pi 105.04 Nm; hydro-mechanical 0.963, 0.949, 0.928. Volumetric (50 C): 0.9673 at 1500 and 0.9811 at 2500 r/min; the unstated normalising base comes out as 2461 to 2530 r/min times V from the two speeds, so "normalised" means divided by the ideal flow at 2500 r/min.
- At 2100 r/min, 150 bar: hydro-mechanical 0.9359, volumetric 0.9756, **overall 0.9130**. Casappa's own catalogue for KP 30.43: 0.764 from the power curves (6k), 0.7926 from the torque curves; the catalogue's torque is 1.155 to 1.182 times the bench's at the bench's speeds. Lowest of six variants 0.8811 (eta_hm at 2500 r/min and eta_v 0.95).
- **GB1 held** (0.9130 > 0.83; not blind). **GB2 held** (0.9359 against 0.801, +0.135; not blind). **GB3 refuted** (model torque at 150 bar &minus;0.9%, +1.9%, +3.6% from ideal). **GB4 refuted** (Figure 11's measured minus model sits 1.9 and 2.2 Nm under Figure 12 at 1500 and 2500 r/min; &minus;0.04 at 1000). **GB5 held** (0.9811). **GB6 refuted on Figure 11** (5.03, 3.64, 4.42 Nm), held on Figure 12 (5.07, 5.55, 6.56).
- Reading: the one test of the class found puts a 44 cm3/r gear pump at 0.91 at 150 bar, 0.08 above the highest crossing and 0.05 above it at worst, so on this evidence a smaller fixed gear pump beats load-sensing on the small excavator's fuel in at least 94.8% of geometries (the committed Casappa sweep's last point, c = 1.15; the bench needs c = 1.195, and the share only rises with c). The bigger finding is the gap between a maker's bench and its own selection curves: 0.15 in overall efficiency, about 16% in torque, for the same size. If that generalises, the three catalogue readings (0.76, 0.85, 0.90) are conservative to different degrees, and catalogue curves are a poor input for fixed-pump sizing. GB3, GB4 and GB6 say the paper's model lines are not consistent between its Figures 11 and 12 at 1500 and 2500 r/min; the measured lines carry the result and agree with Figure 12 at 1000 r/min to 0.04 Nm.
- Limits: one unit, on its maker's bench, reported in a CFD validation paper (one author at Casappa), not an ISO 4409 report from an independent lab; Figure 11's oil temperature is not stated; the flowmeter's stated range (0.05 to 80 L/min) is below the 2500 r/min flow (about 108 L/min), unexplained in the paper; read by pixel from raster figures (gridline residuals up to 0.43 px, about 0.14 Nm); the excavator sweep was not rerun at this pump, only bounded.
- Next: (a) an independent ISO 4409 test of a 30 to 50 cm3/r gear pump, or a second maker's bench data, to tell a typical unit from this one; (b) rerun the Casappa sweep with the bench efficiency (c = 1.2) and a bench-shaped drag, so the share is computed rather than bounded; (c) the XE80E's own pump make and auto-idle speed.

## 2026-11-14 · Milestone 6o: a third gear-pump maker (Marzocchi ALP3, vector-digitised) as an out-of-sample test of 6n's 0.83 crux (predictions written before any curve was digitised)
- Why: 6n reduced the excavator ranking to one number fitted on two makers: a fixed gear pump of about 85 L/min (20 bar flow) at 150 bar and 2100 r/min, above or below about 0.83 overall efficiency. The charts are vector art (Illustrator), like Casappa's. huggingface.co answers (HTTP 200) this session, so the break-even can be rerun.   - GM1 (the crux, out of sample): the ALP3 share at S = 0.38 (central setting) is on the side 6n's rule gives from its index 2: at least 50% if index 2 is at least 0.83, under 50% if below. Prior 70%.
  - GM2: the ALP3's own c-sweep 50% crossing on index 2 lies within 0.02 of 0.8295 (the mean of Casappa's 0.8281 and Danfoss's 0.8309). Prior 55%.
  - GM3: ALP3 index 2 lies between Casappa's 0.759 and Danfoss's 0.849. Prior 60%.
  - GM4: the 25 bar flow over V n at 2000 r/min is within 0.97 to 1.03 for every size. Prior 60%.
  - GM5: power and torque curves agree, median P over M n / 9549 within 0.97 to 1.03 over all curves at 2000 r/min. Prior 65%.
  - GM6: ALP3-D-60 (39 cm3/r, about 80 L/min at 2100 r/min) runs between 0.78 and 0.86 at 150 bar and 2100 r/min. Prior 55%.

The catalogues themselves put that pump at 0.759, 0.849 and 0.898, a spread twelve times the crossings', so "a gear pump" is not one answer: the ranking turns on which pump and whether its catalogue curves are what it delivers. Marzocchi's own sizing text (PDF page 12: volumetric 0.95, hydro-mechanical 0.85 "under working conditions", 0.81 overall) sits under the threshold while its curves sit above it, the mirror of Casappa's stated 0.88 against curves of 0.71 to 0.80.

## 2026-11-14 · Milestone 6n: do the two gear-pump makers' break-even sweeps collapse onto one efficiency threshold? (predictions written before the index was computed)
- Why: 6m's reading is that the excavator ranking turns on "which gear pump", with a flip near 0.82 overall efficiency at 150 bar. That number came from scaling one maker's curves at a time (Casappa flips near c = 1.09, Danfoss near c = 0.98). If the two sweeps, put on one absolute efficiency axis, cross 50% at nearly the same efficiency, the crux is a single bench-testable number whatever the maker's curve shape; if they do not, the curve shape (efficiency against size, idle drag) matters too and one number would mislead. This session's network refuses huggingface.co and the maker sites (HTTP 403 at the proxy, probed), so no break-even can be rerun; this step uses only the committed results files.
- Method fixed now (`experiments/gear_pump_crux.py`, stdlib): for each maker, an efficiency index E = c times the median overall efficiency at 150 bar and 2100 r/min over that maker's sizes whose 20 bar flow lies in the range both cover (55.8 to 157.3 L/min, so Danfoss 033 to 075, Casappa all eight), read from the per-size tables in `excavator_gear_pump_results.txt` and `excavator_gear_pump_danfoss_results.txt`. Each c-sweep point (share of 384 geometries where a smaller fixed pump beats 3.99 L/h at S = 0.38) is placed at its E; the 50% crossing is interpolated linearly between adjacent points. Second index as a check: the efficiency interpolated in 20 bar flow at 85 L/min (near both makers' central optimum). A third series, Danfoss moving efficiency with Casappa idle drag (6m), separates moving efficiency from idle drag. The piston pump (0.840 at 150 bar, 74.0%) is one more point.
- I had read the committed results (the c sweeps and per-size tables) before writing these, but had not computed either index or any crossing.
- **Predictions:**
  - GX1: on index 1 the two makers' 50% crossings differ by less than 0.02. Prior 60%.
  - GX2: the mean of the two makers' crossings on index 1 lies in 0.81 to 0.84. Prior 65%.
  - GX3: the piston point (0.840, 74.0%) lies within 10 percentage points of each maker's sweep, interpolated at 0.840 on index 1. Prior 55%.
  - GX4: on index 2 (efficiency at 85 L/min) the two crossings still differ by less than 0.02. Prior 45%.
  - GX5: with Casappa's idle drag on both, the Danfoss crossing moves toward Casappa's on index 1 (the gap shrinks). Prior 55%.

- One correction before reading any crossing: the first run's shared-range filter used the rounded 157.3 L/min and so dropped Casappa's largest size (157.35), against the method above ("Casappa all eight"). The range is now taken from the two tables (55.81 to 157.35); the figures below are from that run only.
- Index 1 (median efficiency at 150 bar over the shared sizes): Casappa 0.7605 (eight sizes), Danfoss 0.8320 (seven, 033 to 075). 50% crossings: **Casappa 0.8295, Danfoss 0.8147**, gap 0.0147, mean 0.8221.
- Index 2 (efficiency at 85 L/min of 20 bar flow): Casappa 0.7593, Danfoss 0.8485. Crossings **0.8281 and 0.8309**, gap **0.0028**.

So the crux is one number, stated where it is measured: a fixed gear pump of about 85 L/min (about 40 cm3/r at 2100 r/min) at 150 bar, above or below about 0.83 overall. Above the flip the sweeps part (58.5% against 75.8% at 0.840), so how far a smaller fixed pump wins, once it wins, still depends on the curve shape.
- Limits: no simulation was rerun (huggingface.co and the maker sites answered 403 at this session's proxy); crossings are linear interpolations between sweep points about 0.04 apart on Casappa's grid (0.008 to 0.017 on Danfoss's), and scaling by c moves every size alike, which a real better pump need not do; two makers' typical curves at 50 C, the piston pump at 60 C.

## 2026-11-13 · Milestone 6k: a measured gear pump as the fixed pump (Casappa Kappa 30 catalogue curves, vector-digitised), in place of the piston pump at full stroke (predictions written before any curve was digitised)
- Why: 6g, 6i and 6j all named the same decisive unknown: the fixed pump's own full-stroke efficiency, and since 6j its drag at idle. Every break-even so far priced the open-centre pump as Kauranne's axial piston unit locked at full stroke (0.84 at 150 bar, 60 C), but the fixed pump on a small open-centre machine is usually an external gear pump. This session casappa.com answers (HTTP 200, probed), and its KAPPA catalogue (K 05 T A, 05/03.2012, pages 9 to 11) gives, for eight KP 30 sizes from 27 to 73 cm3/r, flow against speed and absorbed power and torque against speed at four to five pressures each, "obtained at 50 C, using oil with viscosity 36 cSt at 40 C". The curves are vector paths in the PDF, so they can be read exactly rather than by eye. Only the page images were looked at before these predictions (no coordinates read, no number computed).
- Method fixed now: extract the curve paths from the PDF pages' vector drawing (pdftocairo SVG, stdlib parse), map them to axes by the tick labels' positions, and tabulate per size: Q(n) at the two pressures the flow chart draws, P(n, p) and M(n, p) at each labelled pressure. At the working speed 2100 r/min (6f): overall efficiency eta_t = Q p / (600 P), with Q at each pressure interpolated linearly in p between the flow chart's lines (leakage roughly proportional to p); hydro-mechanical efficiency from torque, eta_hm = p V / (20 pi M); the power and torque curves are checked against each other (P = M n / 9549). Drag at zero pressure: M(p) at 2100 r/min extrapolated linearly to p = 0. Then the 6j break-even (384 geometries, central PVG neutral, standby 2 L/min, working speed, S = record and 0.38) rerun with the fixed pump of each size q_p priced by the gear pump interpolated in displacement at 2100 r/min (sizes outside 27 to 73 cm3/r held at the nearest end, disclosed), both moving (full stroke at p + m*) and idle (drag plus neutral circulation). Load-sensing is unchanged (6i/6j piston pump).
- **Predictions:**
  - GK1: at 2100 r/min and 150 bar the KP 30.43 and 30.51 overall efficiency (power and flow curves) lies between 0.80 and 0.90. Prior 60%.
  - GK2: the power and torque curves agree, P within 5% of M n / 9549, at 150 bar and 2000 r/min for KP 30.43. Prior 50%.
  - GK3: that gear-pump efficiency is within 0.04 of the piston pump's full-stroke 0.84 at 150 bar (60 C). Prior 50%.
  - GK4: the gear pump's zero-pressure drag, in bar of full-stroke flow, is below the piston fit's k0 of 7.86 bar. Prior 50%.
  - GK5: with the gear pump as the fixed pump, at S = 0.38 (central setting) a smaller fixed pump still beats 3.99 L/h in more than 50% of geometries (6j: 74.0%). Prior 55%.
  - GK6: the central geometry's f_t,max at S = 0.38 lies within 1 L/h of 6j's 5.07. Prior 50%.

## 2026-11-13 · Milestone 6j: idle and still time charged to both pumps (open-centre neutral circulation against load-sensing standby at zero stroke), with a sourced idle share (predictions written before any 6j case was computed)
- Why: every excavator break-even since 6b charges pump energy only on the moving samples. The record's still time (phi = 0.026 of moving time) and, far larger, the idle hours a real machine logs with the engine on and no function moving, are charged to neither pump. They are not equal: at idle the fixed pump circulates its whole flow q_p through the open-centre valve's neutral passage and still pays its own drag, while the load-sensing pump destrokes to standby pressure and pays its zero-stroke drag (6h: about 78% of its loss at 150 bar does not shrink with stroke) on the full 219 L/min displacement. 6i's Next (b) asked for this. The motion record cannot give the idle share (it is a dig record, 97.5% moving); telematics can.
- Idle share, opened today: Moore, "How to Manage Engine Idling for Efficiency", Construction Equipment, 24 March 2020 (constructionequipment.com). Komatsu telematics over about 75,000 North American machines for 12 months: 38% of engine hours idle; one manufacturer's 36 t excavator: about 40%; Volvo CE, a fleet of about 450 machines: 28 to 30%; Komatsu's target for customers who manage idling: about 20%. Trade-press reporting of manufacturer data, not a peer-reviewed study; used as the sweep's anchors, central 0.38.
- Method fixed now: S = no-function hours over all load-sensing machine hours (the record's own still time included); no-function hours per hour of load-sensing record = S (1 - s0) / (1 - S), s0 = t_still / t_ls from the trace (S = s0 is the trace alone). Idle pump power (kW): open-centre q_p n_r (p_n(q_p n_r) + L(1, p_n)) / 600; load-sensing (Q_max n_r L(0, p_sb) + q_sb p_sb) / 600, with L 6h's 4-term loss fit at 60 C (the efficiency table starts at 25 bar, idle pressures are below it), p_sb = the margin m (the compensator's standby setting), q_sb the standby flow, n_r the idle pump speed over working speed. Per hour of load-sensing record the fixed pump saves (b / 600) Q [(p + m) / eta_LS - (p + m*) / eta_OC] + b (P_LS,idle - P_OC,idle) S (1 - s0) / (1 - S), and f_t* is that over (tau - 1) (6i's moving part unchanged, anchored continuation). Neutral pressure p_n(Q), three sourced readings: PVG 32 bypass line 8.5 + 7.5 Q/140 bar (2n, central); Salami VD8A text, n (Q/75)^2 with n = 7 sections (2i); the Salami 8-spool curve scaled to 7, 12.7 (7/8) (Q/90)^2 (2i). Central m 20 bar, q_sb 2 L/min (2o, unsourced; swept 0, 2, 5), n_r 1 (engine at working speed; 0.5 as an auto-idle sensitivity with loss in bar of full-stroke flow held, so drag power scales with speed, an assumption), b 0.2203 for idle fuel as for work. Sweep S = s0, 0.20, 0.30, 0.38, 0.40, 0.50.
- What was seen before writing these: a back-of-envelope at the central size (101 L/min, PVG reading): load-sensing standby about 2.9 kW, the fixed pump about 3.7 kW at idle, so about 0.8 L/h off the central f_t,max at S = 0.38. No script has run.
- **Predictions:**
  - J1: the record's own still time (S = s0) moves the central f_t,max by less than 0.1 L/h either way. Prior 85%.
  - J2: at working speed (central reading) the fixed-pump size at which its idle power equals load-sensing's standby power lies between 50 and 120 L/min. Prior 60%.
  - J3: at the stock 219 L/min pump, open-centre idles at at least 3 times load-sensing's standby power (central reading). Prior 75%.
  - J4: at S = 0.38 (central reading, working speed) the share of the 384 geometries where a smaller fixed pump beats 3.99 L/h falls below 50% (6i: 66.7%). Prior 45%.
  - J5: at S = 0.38 the central geometry's maximising fixed-pump size falls below 6i's 101 L/min. Prior 65%.
  - J6: under the Salami text reading (no zero-flow intercept) S = 0.38 raises the median f_t,max over 6i's. Prior 40%.
