## 2026-11-12 · Milestone 5z: the volume ratio on a measured excavator, from joint motion and published cylinder sizes (predictions registered before any folder-75 data was read)
- Why: 5u showed the break-even load-sensing margin is about beta (r - 1) p_s: a pump-to-demand volume ratio r times a pressure. 5y measured the pressure on an excavator (174 bar, 0.39 to 0.43 of its top pressure) but its files carry no flow, so r on an excavator is untested; the synthetic cycles give 1.43 and the measured loader 1.79. 5y's Next: turn the joint motion of folder `75` of `fuxi-robot/excavator-motion` (CC BY-NC-SA 4.0) into cylinder flow with published cylinder geometry.
- What was seen before this entry: the dataset card (qpos = [boom, arm, bucket, swing] joint angles in rad, 10 Hz) and the folder listing: 10 files named `xcmg_data_2025-04-11-*.hdf5`. No folder-75 array has been read. That the machine is an XCMG of the 7.5 t class is our reading of the folder and file names, not a stated fact.
- Geometry (sourced): XCMG XE80E brochure (Europe, 2024), "Hydraulic Cylinders" table: boom 110 x 75 x 922, arm 95 x 60 x 885, bucket 85 x 55 x 742 mm (bore x rod x stroke); main pump: load-sensing, 104.3 cm3/rev, 219 L/min at 2100 rpm, relief 326.4 kgf/cm2; maximum system pressure boom/arm/bucket 316; max swing speed 12 rpm. A dealer listing of the XE75U gives 4.3 x 2.9 x 36.3, 3.7 x 2.4 x 34.8 and 3.4 x 2.2 x 29.2 in, which is the same cylinders within 1 mm, so the XE75 and XE80E share them; the pump is the XE80E's and is swept.
- Method fixed before the run: joint speed by `central_speed` over the timestamps (as 5y). Each cylinder's rate is a constant lever k_i = stroke_i / range_i times |joint speed|, range_i being the joint's full travel; that travel is not published, so the central range is the observed p0.1 to p99.9 span over all 10 files (a lower bound on the travel, so an upper bound on lever and flow), swept to 1.5 times it. Flow = area x rate, with the bore area for one direction and the annulus for the other; which sign of joint speed extends each cylinder is not in the card, so both assignments per cylinder (8 in all) are computed and the extremes reported. Swing is a motor: q_sw = s x 219 L/min x |swing speed| / (12 rpm), s swept 0, 0.5, 1 (central 0.5). Demand q(t) is the sum. Moving: any joint (swing included) above 0.05 rad/s, as 5y. r = pump volume over demand volume while moving, for a fixed pump of (a) the stock 219 L/min, (b) the p99 and (c) the p95 of moving demand (b and c are free of a common lever scale).
- **Predictions:**
  - Z1: the p99 of moving demand (central geometry, s 0.5, either sign assignment) lies between 60 and 219 L/min: the derived flows respect the pump's published maximum without being implausibly small. Prior 65%.
  - Z2: r at the stock 219 L/min pump exceeds the loader's 1.79 under every sign assignment and swing share. Prior 70%.
  - Z3: r for a pump sized at the p99 of moving demand lies between 1.43 and 2.5 (central). Prior 55%.
  - Z4: the sign assignment moves r(p99 pump) by less than 15% (max over min). Prior 60%.
  - Z5: 5u's rule m* ~ 0.78 (r - 1) p with r at the p99 pump and p = 0.41 x 316 = 130 bar (5y's share of top pressure on this machine's maximum) exceeds 25 bar, the top of the margins sold. Prior 70%.
- Data handling as 5y: only the small arrays, by HTTP range requests; nothing from the dataset is committed.

### Outcome of 5z (`experiments/measured_excavator_flow.py`, `experiments/measured_excavator_flow_results.txt`, 6 s from cache, under a minute of range reads on first run; `measured.cylinder_flow`, 1 new test, 49 in all)
- Data: 10 files, 9,679 samples, 16.0 min. Deviation from the plan: folder 75's `relative_time` runs from 0 to 1 in every file, so it is not a clock. The files also carry `observations/qvel`, and d qpos / d relative_time against qvel gives each file's length by least squares (sample interval 99.3 to 99.8 ms, the card's 10 Hz; correlation 0.88 to 0.999 per joint). qvel (rad/s) is used as the joint speed instead of `central_speed`.
- Observed spans (p0.1 to p99.9): boom 0.76, arm 1.20, bucket 2.65, swing 1.95 rad. The machine moves in 97.5% of samples (the loader: far less), and swing reaches 0.63 of the 12 rpm maximum at p99.
- Not registered, found while writing the script: the dataset's own `scripts/visualize.py` holds forward kinematics for excavator `75` (boom 3.70 m, arm 1.62 m) in which a larger boom angle lowers the boom (boom cylinder retracts) and a larger arm angle swings the arm in (arm cylinder extends). That fixes two of the three signs; the bucket is not in the script. Under those signs the bucket sign moves r by under 1%.
- **Z1 refuted, narrowly under the kinematic signs.** With the swing share 0.5 the p99 of moving demand is 240 L/min under the kinematic signs (240 to 413 over all 8 assignments), above the 219 L/min pump. With the swing left out, the cylinders' p99 is **218.8 L/min**, the pump's published maximum within 0.1%: on the observed spans the cylinder flows sit where a flow-saturated machine would put them. That agreement is a check the method passed, not one it was tuned to (no lever was fitted).
- **Z2 holds.** r at the stock 219 L/min pump is 1.88 to 3.84 over all 72 cases (2.16 to 2.17 central), above the loader's 1.79 in every one.
- **Z3 refuted.** r for a fixed pump sized at the p99 of moving demand is 2.38 to 4.08 over the 8 sign assignments, above the registered 2.5 ceiling for most; under the kinematic signs it is **2.38** (per file 2.31 to 2.51, 95% bootstrap over files 2.34 to 2.42). With independent lever factors of 1 to 2.5 per joint, both bucket signs and swing shares 0 to 1 (384 cases) it is 1.95 to 3.08, median 2.44; a pump at the p95 gives 1.69 to 2.63.
- **Z4 refuted.** Over all 8 sign assignments r(p99 pump) moves by 72%; the kinematic signs give the lowest r of the eight. Fixing two signs from the dataset's kinematics is what makes the result usable.
- **Z5 holds.** 5u's rule, m* ~ 0.78 (r - 1) p with p = 0.41 x 316 = 130 bar, gives **139 bar** at the kinematic r 2.38, and 96 bar at the lowest r p99 of the 384-case sweep (70 bar at the lowest r p95), against 14 to 25 bar margins sold and 44 bar at the synthetic r.
- Reading: on measured excavator motion the fixed pump that covers the machine's p99 demand delivers about 2.4 times the volume the work needs (1.95 to 3.08 over the geometry the files cannot fix), more than the loader's 1.79 and well above the synthetic cycles' 1.43. An excavator moves almost all the time, but its demand is peaky: a median of about 90 L/min against a p99 of 240. Put with 5y's pressure, the break-even margin for load-sensing comes out at 70 to 140 bar, several times any margin sold, so on the measured excavator data load-sensing wins with room to spare. The synthetic cycles understated both of the rule's numbers.
- Limits: the flow comes from a 7.5 t class machine and the pressure from a 49 t class machine (5y), so the two halves of the rule are not from one machine; the 5v covariance factor 0.78 is the loader's; levers are constant (stroke over observed span), not the real linkage (the bucket four-bar is the least linear); the cylinder sizes are the XE80E's (shared with the XE75U by dealer listing), and the machine is identified only by its file names; swing flow is a share of pump flow, not a motor displacement; 10 Hz misses short peaks, which would raise p99 and r. H4's fuel comparison is still untested: no measured fuel or pump flow on an excavator.
- Next: H4 with a fuel or flow record of an excavator; meanwhile, run the 5u/5w pump-size trade on this demand trace (a smaller fixed pump priced in cycle time, as 5w did for the loader), and add 5y and 5z to the paper draft as section 3.7.

## 2026-11-12 · Milestone 5y: a measured excavator pressure trace at last (predictions registered before the analysis script was written)
- Why: every excavator number in this programme rests on the force model's working pressure (33 bar demand-weighted, 5u), while the one measured machine, a 6 t wheel loader, works at 134 bar (5v), and 5v showed the break-even margin runs through (r - 1) p. "What would settle it" and 5v/5x's Next ask for a measured excavator pressure trace (H4). Zenodo, Figshare and Mendeley searches this run found none. The Hugging Face dataset `fuxi-robot/excavator-motion` (CC BY-NC-SA 4.0; three excavator models loading trucks, 10 Hz) does: its folder `490` files carry `observations/pressure`, 8 channels in Pa, beside joint angles (boom, arm, bucket, swing) and nanosecond timestamps. The channels are not labelled in the files, the dataset card or its two scripts. Folder `490` presumably names a 49 t class machine; the card does not say, so that is our reading, not a fact. The other two folders (`75`, `306`) carry joint angles only.
- What was seen before this entry (disclosed, so the predictions below are not all blind): one file (`robot_data_20250420_123326`, 393 s) only. Per channel, the 1st/50th/99th percentiles in bar were: ch0 23/144/223, ch1 1/7/50, ch2 4/51/181, ch3 21/140/347, ch4 constant at -60.5, ch5 12/99/320, ch6 3/30/293, ch7 67/121/424 (minimum 65 bar). Correlations with joint speed were all weak (|r| < 0.42) at the raw 10 Hz.
- Data handling: only the small arrays are read, by HTTP range requests against the HDF5 files (about 26 MB of each 4 to 5 GB file; the images are skipped). No data are committed (the licence is NC-SA; the repository is MIT); the scripts download what they need and print derived statistics with attribution.
- Setup fixed before the run: all 10 files of folder `490`. Moving = any of boom, arm, bucket joint speed (central difference of the angle over the timestamps) above 0.05 rad/s, swept 0.02 to 0.10; still = all four joints below 0.01 rad/s. Live channel = range above 5 bar. The per-sample maximum over live channels is the label-free reading of the highest pressure in the circuit (it can include a pump or a supply pressure, which is at or above the highest load pressure, so it is an upper reading of p_q). qpos has a few fewer rows than pressure in some files; samples are paired by index from the start, and the pairing is checked by the lag that maximises the pressure-to-motion correlation (reported, not tuned on).
- **Predictions:**
  - Y1: across the 10 files, the median over moving samples of the highest live channel exceeds 100 bar (three times the force model's 33 bar), and the file-level 95% bootstrap interval stays above 100 bar. Prior 85% (partly seen in one file).
  - Y2: channel 4 is dead (range under 5 bar) in every file. Prior 80%.
  - Y3: channel 7 behaves as a supply (pump) pressure: its median while moving exceeds its median while still by over 30%, and it is the highest live channel in more than half of the moving samples. Prior 45%.
  - Y4: putting the measured reading in place of the force model's 33 bar in 5u's rule R2 = (r - 1) p at the synthetic r 1.433 (d 0), the break-even margin is above 40 bar even after scaling by the loader's covariance correction (m*/R2 = 0.78, 5v). Prior 65%.
  - Y5: the median over moving samples of the highest live channel is within 30% of the loader's demand-weighted 134 bar (that is, 94 to 174 bar). Prior 55%.

### Outcome of 5y (`experiments/measured_excavator.py`, `experiments/measured_excavator_results.txt`, under 1 s from cache, about 10 min of range reads on first run; `measured.central_speed` and `measured.highest_live`, 2 new tests, 48 in all; reader `experiments/hf_range.py`)
- Data: 10 files, 76.7 min, sample interval median 99.5 ms. Each file has 4 to 70 more pressure rows than angle rows; 3 repeated timestamps were dropped. The stdlib `central_speed` equals a numpy central difference on file 1.
- Deviation from the plan: the pairing check (the lag that maximises the correlation of pressure with joint speed) did not work as an alignment test. The best lag sat at the edge of a ±10 window, and on a wider window it sat at +27 to +80 rows with correlations of only 0.2 to 0.4, because on an excavator pressure peaks in slow, high-force digging, not in fast motion. It was replaced by a sensitivity block: the headline under four pairings (as stored 174.1, aligned at the end 173.6, pressure 30 rows later 175.1 and earlier 178.0 bar) and with no motion mask at all (176.6 bar). The headline does not depend on the pairing.
- **Y1 holds.** Over moving samples (86.9% of the record) the highest live channel has a median of **174.1 bar** (p10 131.1, p90 324.5); the median of file medians is 172.9 with a 95% bootstrap of 167.1 to 180.4 bar. Moving thresholds of 0.02 to 0.10 rad/s move it from 175.5 to 172.5 bar.
- **Y2 holds.** Channel 4 is flat at -60.6 bar (range at most 0.75 bar) in every file; the other seven are live in every file.
- **Y3 refuted.** Channel 7 rises 67% from still to moving (67.0 to 112.0 bar median) but is the highest live channel in only 32.8% of moving samples (channel 0 in 29.0%, channel 3 in 20.2%). It is also the only channel above 400 bar (p99.9 444 bar, max 477). Which channel is the pump, if any, cannot be told from the files.
- **Y4 holds.** Rule R2 = (r - 1) p at the synthetic r 1.433 goes from 14.3 bar (force model) to 75.4 bar (measured), or 58.8 bar after the loader's 0.78 covariance correction.
- **Y5 refuted, narrowly.** 174.1 bar is 1.30 times the loader's 133.9 bar, just outside the registered 94 to 174 bar band.
- Not registered: a 49 t machine runs a higher relief than the force model's 180 bar machine, so pressure as a share of the machine's top pressure (p99.9 of the highest channel) is the fairer comparison: 0.392 (top 444.1 bar), or 0.434 without channel 7 (top 357.7 bar, moving median 155.4), against the force model's 33/180 = 0.183, a factor of 2.14 to 2.37. At 180 bar that is 70.6 to 78.2 bar, and R2 x 0.78 at r 1.433 is 23.8 bar (against 11.1 on the force model). The still samples are not idle: the highest channel's median while all joints are still is 132 to 323 bar per file, consistent with holding or pushing against the ground at stall.
- Reading: the force model's 33 bar working pressure is too low by a factor of about 2 (as a share of relief) to 5 (absolute) against the one measured excavator. Every synthetic break-even margin in sections 3.1 to 3.6c (20 to 30 bar) is therefore an underestimate. On measured excavator pressure the break-even at the synthetic volume ratio is 24 to 59 bar, at or above the 14 to 25 bar margins sold, so load-sensing wins unless the excavator's flow is much smoother than the synthetic cycle (r near 1). That is the remaining unknown: these files carry no flow, and without labelled chambers the joint angles cannot be turned into flows without the machine's cylinder geometry.
- Limits: unlabelled channels (the highest live channel is an upper reading of p_q and may include a pump or return-side pressure); a large machine, not the 1.5 to 3 t class of the question; one machine and operator, ten recordings on three days; 10 Hz sampling misses fast transients; the machine's make, circuit (open-centre, negative-flow or load-sensing) and relief setting are not stated.
- Next: the volume ratio on a real excavator. The same dataset's folder `75` (a 7.5 t class machine by its name, joint angles and speeds only) gives measured joint motion; with published cylinder geometry for a machine of that class, its flow demand and r could be estimated and set against the loader's 1.79 and the synthetic 1.43.

- Reproduction: `Short_Y_cycle_data.mat` downloaded fresh from Zenodo (96,108,872 bytes), numpy and scipy in a new environment (Python 3.13). `measured_breakeven.py`, `measured_volume_ratio.py`, `measured_pump_size.py` and `measured_fuel_time.py` print output identical to their committed results files, and `fuel_time_figure.py` regenerates the committed SVG unchanged (`git status` clean after the four runs).

The rerun (5v) is now its own section, 3.6d: the demand-weighted working pressure is about four times the force model's (133.9 against 33 bar), but the exact split runs through the surplus-flow pressure (99.5 bar), and using the working pressure overstates the break-even by 27% (110.3 against 87.0 bar). Swapping one input at a time, the load pressure carries most of the gap in (r - 1) p_q (14.3 to 58.0 bar), the volume ratio less (14.3 to 26.2).

New title and abstract: the measured-machine result leads, the synthetic small-excavator results follow as what the force model can and cannot say, and the abstract states that the open-centre loader is simulated on measured motion. Threats gain item 7 (slowing model, constant fuel rate, one efficiency); "What would settle it" gains (e) a measured open-centre machine of the class and (f) a payload signal.
- `paper/check_numbers.py`: 36 new rows; 155 quoted numbers checked, 0 missing.
- Still short of the venue: no measured excavator trace (H4), the citation list was confirmed by web search only (Crossref was unreachable then), and the draft is Markdown, not the venue's template. Next: run `cite_check.py` on the draft's DOIs and open the rest; then a measured excavator trace if one can be found, or a second machine.

## 2026-11-12 · Milestone 5x: the smaller open-centre pump's lost time priced in fuel per unit of work (predictions registered before any code was written)
- Why: 5w found that a fixed open-centre pump must shrink to 28.5 cc to match load-sensing's hydraulic energy at the measured 24.9 bar margin, at 39% more record time, and named the next step: price that time with the record's own fuel rate, so the trade is one number a builder can act on. Equal motion is equal work (the same 18 Y-cycles), so fuel for the whole record is fuel per unit of work. The trace carries the ECU fuel rate (0.935 L over 440.2 s, 5n).
- Setup fixed before the run: fuel(X) = F_meas + b (E_hyd,X - E_hyd,meas) / eta + f_t (T_X - T_rec) / 3600, with b = 0.2332 L/kWh the marginal rate of 5n's Willans line on this record, eta the work pump's hydraulic-to-shaft efficiency (central: the record's measured hydraulic over the authors' shaft-power estimate; swept 0.75 to 0.95), E_hyd from 5w's `parts` (case (b), q_sb 0) with load-sensing at the measured 24.9 bar margin, and times from 5w's `times` (each circuit charged its own pump limit). Three readings of f_t, the fuel rate over the extra time (5n's Willans intercept is negative, -0.94 L/h, so it cannot serve as a floor): (i) "still": the measured mean fuel rate over samples where neither section moves for open-centre (5o's masks); (ii) "rest of machine", central: the record's mean fuel rate less b times the work pump's mean shaft power; (iii) "low": the 5th percentile of the record's 1 s mean fuel rates. Same sweep of Vg as 5w plus V_eq.
- **Predictions:**
  - X1: at V_eq, open-centre burns more than 15% more fuel than load-sensing for the same work under the central f_t. Prior 75%.
  - X2: no Vg on the sweep gives open-centre fuel at or below load-sensing's, under any of the three f_t readings and eta from 0.75 to 0.95. Prior 80%.
  - X3: the fuel-minimising open-centre Vg under the central readings is between 45 and 56 cc (time loss outweighs hydraulic savings well before V_eq). Prior 55%.
  - X4: that minimum open-centre fuel penalty is between 7% and 13% of load-sensing's fuel. Prior 50%.
  - X5: the still rate (i) is below the rest-of-machine rate (ii). Prior 60%.

### Outcome of 5x (`experiments/measured_fuel_time.py`, `experiments/measured_fuel_time_results.txt`, 20 s; `measured.extra_fuel`, 1 new test, 46 in all; figure `experiments/fuel_time_pump_size.svg`)
- Checks: the refitted Willans slope equals 5n's 0.2332 L/kWh; at 5w's V_eq (28.51 cc) m* is 24.90 bar and the hydraulic part of the fuel difference is 0.00 mL, as it must be; load-sensing at the measured margin models 3957 kJ against 3867 kJ measured (fuel 0.942 L against 0.935 L measured).
- Readings: eta 0.818 (measured hydraulic 3867 kJ over the authors' shaft estimate 4725 kJ); f_t (i) still **5.99 L/h**, (ii) rest of machine **5.15 L/h**, (iii) low 1.48 L/h.
- **X1 holds.** At V_eq open-centre burns **26.2%** more fuel than load-sensing for the same work (central); the hydraulic part is zero, so all of it is the 39% longer record at the rest-of-machine rate.
- **X2 holds.** No pump size from 25 to 63 cc (0.5 cc grid) beats load-sensing under any f_t reading and any eta from 0.75 to 0.95, and none does in any of the 18 Y-cycles.
- **X3 holds.** The fuel-minimising fixed pump is **53.5 cc** (central; 52.5 to 57.5 cc across eta and the still/rest readings; 35.5 to 38.5 cc only at the low rate). Per cycle the best size is a median 54.5 cc (45 to 60).
- **X4 holds.** At that size open-centre still burns **11.5%** more fuel than load-sensing (central; 10.2 to 12.7% across eta and the still/rest readings, 6.2 to 6.8% at the low rate), against 13.0% at the loader's own 63 cc. Shrinking the pump saves 1.5 points of fuel at most, at about 4.5% more cycle time.
- **X5 refuted.** The fuel rate while the work hydraulics are still is **higher** (5.99 L/h, engine at 28.6 kW) than the rest-of-machine average (5.15 L/h): on a Y-cycle the bucket is still while the loader drives, so "still" is not idle. Waiting for the hydraulics at the still rate would make every open-centre size worse (V_eq 30.5%), not better.
- Reading: on this loader the open-centre fuel penalty is about 11 to 13% whatever fixed pump a builder picks; the pump size only moves the penalty between the pump (big pump, surplus flow) and the clock (small pump, the rest of the machine running longer). The fuel curve is flat from about 50 to 63 cc and climbs steeply below 45 cc. 5w's energy parity at 28.5 cc is the worst choice for fuel per unit of work among the pumps at or above it: 26% more fuel, double the 63 cc penalty.
- Limits: the extra time is charged at a constant rate (the rest of the machine keeps running as it did on average), not simulated (a real operator might idle or drive less while waiting, which the low rate brackets); eta is one record-wide number for both circuits (a fixed pump at full stroke is likely more efficient than the variable pump at part stroke, which favours open-centre slightly: at eta 0.95 the minimum is 10.2%); time is priced only in fuel, not in operator or machine hours; one machine, one 440 s record.
- Next: the excavator side, the force model's 33 bar working pressure against a published excavator pressure trace (5v's Next); a fuel-per-tonne reading needs the payload, which this record does not carry.

## 2026-11-12 · Milestone 5w: a smaller fixed pump on the measured loader, priced in cycle time (predictions registered before any code was written)
- Why: 5t to 5v kept the loader's own 63 cc pump in the open-centre counterfactual, so r (pump volume over demand volume while moving) was 1.79 and the break-even margin 87 bar. A designer building open-centre would pick a smaller fixed pump and accept slower peaks (3i/3k priced that trade on synthetic cycles). 5t, 5v "Next": how far does a smaller pump close the gap on real motion, and what does it cost in time?
- Setup fixed before the run: fixed pump Vg swept 63, 56, 50, 45, 40, 36, 32, 28, 25 cc at the measured engine speed (2.5 L/min leak as before), 5t's case (b) (PVG 32 orifice floor sized per section at p99.9 flow, p_neutral 5 bar), q_sb 0, 5o's noise-floor masks. Where the open-centre demand exceeds the pump flow, the actuators slow so that the pump delivers the demanded volume: that sample lasts dt x q_dem / q_pump, the pump flow all goes to the work, and the hydraulic energy equals what `parts` already charges with q_oc = max(q_pump, q_dem) (the energy of a slowed sample is q_dem (p + d) dt either way). Time cost: open-centre record time over load-sensing record time, with load-sensing charged its own pump limit (the measured machine's 63 cc variable pump, max(1, q_dem / q_63) per sample), so the net ratio is 1 at 63 cc by construction only if the two demands agree. V_eq: the Vg at which the whole-record m* equals the measured 24.9 bar margin (linear interpolation on the sweep).
- **Predictions:**
  - W1: whole-record m* under (b) stays above 24.9 bar for every Vg of 40 cc or more. Prior 70%.
  - W2: V_eq lies between 25 and 40 cc. Prior 50%.
  - W3: at V_eq the net open-centre/load-sensing record time ratio exceeds 1.25. Prior 60%.
  - W4: the surplus-flow pressure p_s at V_eq is below the 99.5 bar of 63 cc (the surplus that survives a small pump comes when demand, and pressure, are low). Prior 60%.
  - W5: across the 18 Y-cycles, Spearman(r at 63 cc, net time ratio at V_eq) is below -0.5 (cycles with more spare flow lose less time). Prior 55%.

### Outcome of 5w (`experiments/measured_pump_size.py`, `experiments/measured_pump_size_results.txt`, 3 s; `vg` parameter on `measured_breakeven.parts`; `measured.slowed_time`, 1 new test, 45 in all; figure `experiments/trade_pump_size.svg`)
- Checks: vg = 63 cc reproduces 5t/5v (m* 87.0, p_s 99.5 bar); in every slowed sample pump flow times stretched time equals the demanded volume (the energy identity that lets `parts` charge a slowed sample at q_dem (p + d) dt); the numpy time equals the tested stdlib `slowed_time` on the first 50,000 samples; `measured_breakeven.py` and `measured_volume_ratio.py` reproduce their results files byte for byte after the `vg` edit.
- Data check (not predicted): the velocity-derived demand exceeds the 63 cc pump's geometric flow in 16.3% of samples with demand over 2 L/min (median excess 3.0 L/min, p99 29.0). The authors' pump flow estimate is the summed valve flows (identical to 1e-15), and their angle estimate there has median 1.055, beyond full stroke, so the excess sits in the derived flows. Charging load-sensing its own 63 cc limit cancels it: the net time ratio at 63 cc is 0.9997.
- **W1 holds.** m* stays above 24.9 bar for every pump of 40 cc or more (41.6 bar at 40 cc; 70.7 at 56, 58.9 at 50, 49.9 at 45).
- **W2 holds.** V_eq = **28.5 cc** (interpolated 28.49, bisection 28.51), 45% of the loader's pump. Mean pump flow there is 40.7 L/min against a median moving demand of 55.9 L/min.
- **W3 holds.** At V_eq open-centre needs **1.39 times** load-sensing's record time (per Y-cycle 1.27 to 1.54, median 1.42).
- **W4 holds.** p_s at V_eq is **80.0 bar** (99.5 at 63 cc); the surplus that survives comes at lower pressure. p_q is unchanged at 133.9 bar (it is a property of the demand).
- **W5 holds.** Spearman(r at 63 cc, time ratio at V_eq) = **-0.686**.
- The trade gets worse at every step. The first 7 cc off the pump (63 to 56) buy 16.2 bar of break-even for 2.9% time (5.6 bar per %); 32 to 28 cc buys 5.4 bar for 10.2% (0.5 bar per %). The break-even never reaches the measured margin before the machine is a third slower; per cycle, 12 of 18 cycles fall below 24.9 bar at 28 cc.
- Reading: on real loader motion a smaller fixed pump cannot buy open-centre parity cheaply. To match load-sensing at its own 24.9 bar margin on hydraulic energy, the open-centre pump must shrink to about 28.5 cc and the machine must accept about 39% more cycle time. Any open-centre pump of 40 cc or more loses on energy at every cycle with the time cost already above 16%. For a builder choosing between a 63 cc fixed pump plus open-centre valve and a load-sensing pump on a loader-like duty, the open-centre route costs either energy (m* far above any margin sold) or productivity, never neither.
- Limits: hydraulic energy only; the engine's own losses over the extra time are not charged (that favours open-centre, so the 39% understates its full cost); slowing is the simplest rule (actuators share the shortfall by their demand, no operator change, no engine speed-up, which a real machine could trade for fuel); one machine and one 440 s record; open-centre still the 5o/5r counterfactual with 5t's case (b) drops.
- Next: price the extra time with the engine's measured idle or part-load fuel rate from the same record (the trace has ECU fuel rate) so the trade is in litres per tonne; the force model's 33 bar against a published excavator pressure trace (5v's Next).

## 2026-11-12 · Milestone 5v: the volume-ratio split on the measured loader (V6 run; V7 to V10 registered before any code was written)
- Why: 5u split the exact break-even margin into m* = beta [(r - 1) p_q + r (p_Q - p_q) + r d_bar] + I / B and explained the fourfold gap between the synthetic cycles (20.5 bar) and the measured loader (82.9 bar at no spool drop) by r (1.78 against 1.43) and an inferred loader working pressure near 100 bar. That pressure was inferred, not computed: zenodo.org refused the proxy in 5u, so V6 stayed registered and unrun. It answered (HTTP 200) at 03:45 UTC this session.
- Setup fixed before the run: 5t's cases (a) floor, no drop, p_neutral 5; (b) PVG 32 orifice floor, p99.9 sizing, p_neutral 5; (c) 10 bar drop, p_neutral 10; q_sb 0; 5t's noise-floor motion masks and 18 Y-cycles. The numpy twin of `trace_breakeven` in `measured_breakeven.py` gains the 5u fields (r, p_Q, p_q, d_bar, beta, terms), checked against the stdlib function on the first 50,000 samples as in 5t.
- **Predictions:** V6 as registered in 5u (p_q between 90 and 115 bar; covariance term positive). Added now, before any code of this step existed:
  - V7: under (b), the moving rule R1 (all but the covariance term) is within 10% of the exact whole-record m*. Prior 50%.
  - V8: across the 18 Y-cycles under (b), Spearman(r, m*_c) exceeds Spearman(p_q, m*_c), as on the synthetic cycles (0.77 against 0.12). Prior 55%.
  - V9: beta, the share of load-sensing margin volume delivered while open-centre moves, exceeds 0.9 on the whole record (load-sensing is almost never still on this trace, 5t). Prior 70%.
  - V10: under (b), the per-cycle two-number rule R2 = (r - 1) p_q + r d_bar is within 25% of m*_c in the median cycle. Prior 50%.

### Outcome of 5v (`experiments/measured_volume_ratio.py`, `experiments/measured_volume_ratio_results.txt`, 3 s; `p_s` on `trace_breakeven`, 1 new test, 44 in all)
- Checks: the numpy twin's 5u fields equal the stdlib `trace_breakeven` on the first 50,000 samples (1e-9); the four terms sum to m* in every case; `measured_breakeven.py`, `breakeven_volume_ratio.py` and `breakeven_closed_form.py` reproduce their results files byte for byte after the edits.
- **V6 refuted on both counts.** The loader's demand-weighted load pressure p_q is **133.9 bar** (per cycle 121 to 150), above the 90 to 115 bar I registered, and the covariance term is **negative**, -27.1 bar (per cycle -49 to +2.4): the pump-flow-weighted pressure p_Q is 118.7 bar, lower than p_q. r is 1.793 on this split (5t's 1.78 rounds the same ratio of means).
- **V7 refuted.** Leaving out the covariance puts the moving rule R1 at 114.1 against the exact 87.0 bar under (b), 31% high. On the synthetic cycles the same rule was within 6%, because there the covariance averaged out.
- **V8 holds, strongly.** Spearman(r, m*_c) **0.920** against Spearman(p_q, m*_c) 0.243 over the 18 cycles. As on the synthetic cycles, the flow profile ranks cycles and the load pressure hardly does.
- **V9 holds.** beta = **0.995**: load-sensing delivers almost all its margin volume while open-centre moves, so the idle term is small (4.4 bar of 87).
- **V10 refuted.** The two-number rule R2 overstates m*_c by a median 30.5% per cycle under (b) (32% at no drop, 22% at a 10 bar drop). On the synthetic cycles it read 31% low (idle left out); here it reads about 30% high (covariance left out). The error is the same size with the opposite sign, for a different reason.
- Not predicted: define the **surplus-flow pressure** p_s = (r p_Q - p_q) / (r - 1). Then m* = beta [(r - 1) p_s + r d_bar] + I / B exactly (the covariance folds in), and where every LS section is also moving for open-centre p_s is the plain average load pressure weighted by surplus flow (pump flow minus demand), tested on random traces. On the loader **p_s = 99.5 bar** (per cycle 79 to 126), 34 bar under p_q: the fixed pump's surplus is largest when demand is low, and on this record low demand comes at lower load pressure (demand-weighted 133.9 bar against pump-flow-weighted 118.7; which motions carry the high-pressure flow is not broken out here). 5u's back-inferred "working pressure near 100 bar" was p_s, not the working pressure. On the synthetic cycles the covariance averages out, so p_s and p_q agree there.
- Reading: the break-even margin is (r - 1) times the pressure at which the surplus flow is pushed, plus spool drop times r, plus idle bleed. A builder who uses the working pressure (p_q) where the surplus pressure belongs overstates the break-even by about 30% on a loader-like cycle, where big flows come at high pressure; the ranking of cycles barely needs a pressure at all (r alone, Spearman 0.92). The fourfold synthetic-to-loader gap (20.5 to 82.9 bar) factors as (r - 1) 0.43 to 0.79 and p_s about 33 to 100 bar, with the idle term shrinking from 8 to 4 bar.
- Limits: one machine and one 440 s record; open-centre is the 5o/5r counterfactual on this motion with the measured 63 cc pump; p_s depends on the noise-floor motion masks of 5o (LS counts 99.99% of samples as moving, open-centre fewer); hydraulic energy only.
- Next: p_s against a published excavator pressure trace (the force model's 33 bar sets every synthetic break-even); a smaller fixed pump on the loader trace, which lowers r but raises the cycle time (5t's open question).

## 2026-11-11 · Milestone 5u: the break-even margin is a volume ratio times a load pressure (predictions registered before any code was written)
- Why: 5s wrote the break-even margin as m* = (S + D + I) / B, and 5t found on the measured loader that dm*/dd equals the ratio of fixed-pump flow to demand flow while moving (1.78), and that my back-of-envelope for m* (about 64 bar) missed the measured 87 bar for a reason I did not trace. The surplus term has an exact split that names the two numbers a builder actually knows. With V the volume load-sensing delivers while open-centre moves, r = (pump volume while moving) / V, p_Q the pump-flow-weighted highest load pressure while moving (time-weighted for a fixed pump) and p_q the demand-weighted one: S = V (r p_Q - p_q), so
  m* = beta [ (r - 1) p_q + r (p_Q - p_q) + r d_bar ] + I / B,   beta = V / B,
  exact, where d_bar is the pump-flow-weighted spool drop. The first term is a "two-number rule": a machine whose fixed pump moves r times the volume the work needs, at a typical working pressure p, breaks even with load-sensing at a margin of about (r - 1) p. The second is a pressure-flow covariance: positive when the machine works at high pressure while it moves slowly. zenodo.org refused the proxy again this session (403 at 20:10 UTC), so the loader half is registered here for the next run that reaches it; the synthetic half needs no download.
- Setup fixed before the run: the same 3,000 force-model cycles, idle model, PVG 32 neutral drop (10.91 bar) and 2 L/min standby as 5s; constant open-centre spool drop d = 0 and 6 bar. "Two-number rule" R2 = (r - 1) p_q + r d (moving only, no idle, no beta); "moving rule" R1 = beta [(r - 1) p_q + r d] + I / B (everything except the covariance term).
- **Predictions (written before any code of this step existed):**
  - V1: median r over the cycles is 1.35 to 1.55 (speed fractions are drawn uniformly from 0.4 to 1.0 per phase). Prior 60%.
  - V2: the covariance term r (p_Q - p_q) is under 10% of S / V in absolute value in the median cycle (speed is drawn independently of load in the force model). Prior 70%.
  - V3: the two-number rule R2's median over cycles is within 25% of the exact median m* at both d = 0 and d = 6 (beta < 1 and the idle term pull in opposite directions). Prior 45%.
  - V4: Spearman rank correlation between R2 and the exact m*_c over cycles exceeds 0.7 at d = 0. Prior 60%.
  - V5: R2 and the exact m*_c agree on which circuit wins at a 14 bar and at a 25 bar margin in at least 85% of cycles (d = 6). Prior 55%.
  - V6 (loader, for the next run that reaches Zenodo 10639199): with r = 1.78 known from 5t, p_q lies between 90 and 115 bar and the covariance term is positive. Prior 50%.

### Outcome of 5u (`experiments/breakeven_volume_ratio.py`, `experiments/breakeven_volume_ratio_results.txt`, 2 s, seeded; `breakeven.decompose` and 5u fields on `trace_breakeven`, 3 new tests, 43 in all)
- Checks: the four terms sum to the exact m* on every cycle (largest relative gap 5.6e-15) and on 20 random traces; beta r equals 5s's dm*/dd; a single actuator with no idle gives m* = (Q/q - 1) p + (Q/q) d exactly.
- **V1 holds.** Median r **1.433** (5 to 95%: 1.22 to 1.75).
- **V2 refuted, narrowly.** The covariance term is centred on zero (median 0.09 bar, 5 to 95% -4.2 to 4.5) but its magnitude is a median **11.1%** of the surplus part, above the 10% I registered.
- **V3 refuted.** The two-number rule's median is 0.690 of the exact median at d = 0 (14.2 against 20.5 bar) and 0.816 at d = 6 (22.8 against 28.0). The idle term (median 8.1 bar) is what it leaves out; with idle and beta put back (R1, everything but the covariance) the per-cycle error is a median 6.4% (d = 0) and 4.7% (d = 6).
- **V4 holds.** Spearman(R2, m*) 0.743 at d = 0 (0.805 at d = 6).
- **V5 refuted.** At d = 6 R2 and the exact m* agree on the winner in 95.8% of cycles at a 14 bar margin but only 67.9% at 25 bar: LS wins in 66.4% of cycles by the exact margin and 38.3% by R2. The 25 bar margin sits near the median m*, where the 8 bar the rule omits decides.
- **V6 not run** (zenodo.org refused the proxy, 403).
- Not predicted: the demand-weighted load pressure of the force-model cycles is only **33 bar** median (24 to 46 bar, 5 to 95%), against a 180 bar relief; and p_q barely ranks cycles (Spearman 0.12) while r does (0.77). On these cycles the flow profile, not the pressure, decides the break-even.
- Reading: the break-even margin is the surplus term, (r - 1) times a working pressure, plus spool drop times r, plus idle bleed, all scaled by the share of LS volume delivered while moving. That explains the fourfold gap 5t found between the synthetic cycles (20.5 bar at no drop) and the measured loader (82.9): (r - 1) is 1.8 times larger on the loader, and 5t's 78 bar surplus term then needs a working pressure near 100 bar, about three times the force model's. It also traces my 5t back-of-envelope miss to the denominator, as 5t said: B is a demand volume, not (measured - useful energy) / margin, which would also count the throttling of lower-pressure actuators (not computed here: the data were unreachable). For a reader placing their own machine, r and a working pressure get within about a third, adding the idle term gets within about 6%.
- Limits: exact only inside the 5s model; the synthetic cycles' low working pressure is a property of the force model (assumed soil, lever and breakout ranges, 2c), not a measurement, and the loader pressure is inferred, not computed.
- Next: V6 on the loader when Zenodo answers (one call: `trace_breakeven` already returns r, p_Q, p_q and the terms); check the force model's 33 bar working pressure against a published excavator pressure trace, since it sets every synthetic break-even.

## 2026-11-11 · Milestone 5t: the measured loader's own break-even margin, per Y-cycle (predictions registered before any code was written)
- Why: 5s turned the programme's break-evens into one formula, m* = (S + D + I) / B, and named the measured loader (5n to 5r) as the next place to apply it. Every break-even so far (20.6 to 30.5 bar) comes from the synthetic force-model excavator cycles. The loader is a real load-sensing machine running at a measured 24.9 bar median margin; the question is what margin it could run at before the best open-centre counterfactual of 5o/5r (same motion, fixed 63 cc pump, full-travel spool floor) would use less hydraulic energy, and how much headroom its actual margin leaves. zenodo.org answered (HTTP 200) at the start of this session.
- Setup fixed before the run: LS(m) = sum over moving samples of q_demand (p_load + m) dt + sum over still samples of q_sb m dt, the 5n "LS model" (constant margin on the measured P-port demand); OC from 5o/5r's noise-floor motion masks, (a) the floor dp_spool 0, p_neutral 5 bar, (b) 5r's PVG 32 orifice floor, p99.9 sizing, p_neutral 5, (c) 5n's central case, dp_spool 10, p_neutral 10. Standby flow q_sb = 0 by default (5n's convention), swept to 2 and 5 L/min.
- **Predictions (written before any code of this step existed):**
  - T1: over the whole record, the break-even margin against the 5r floor (b) is 50 to 80 bar. Prior 60%.
  - T2: in every one of the 18 Y-cycles m*_c exceeds the measured 24.9 bar, so load-sensing at its own margin beats the best open-centre counterfactual in every cycle. Prior 90%.
  - T3: surplus flow at load pressure (S) carries over 70% of S + D + I under (b). Prior 70%.
  - T4: a 2 L/min standby flow lowers the whole-record m* under (b) by less than 15%. Prior 60%.
  - T5: the median per-cycle m*_c under (a) is more than twice the synthetic zero-drop break-even of 5q/5s (20.5 bar). Prior 65%.

### Outcome of 5t (`experiments/measured_breakeven.py`, `experiments/measured_breakeven_results.txt`, 2.5 s; `breakeven.trace_breakeven`, 2 new tests)
- Checks: A + 24.9 B equals 5n's LS model energy; the open-centre energies equal `measured_motion.oc` (a, c) and `spool_floor.oc_floor` (b) to 1e-9; the numpy twin equals the tested stdlib `trace_breakeven` on the first 50,000 samples. `spool_floor.py` still reproduces its results file byte for byte. The measured pump energy corresponds to a constant margin of 21.3 bar (m_eff), a little under the 24.9 bar median because the margin is lower at high flow.
- **T1 refuted.** Whole-record m* under (b) is **87.0 bar**, above the 50 to 80 bar I registered: (a) 82.9, (b) 87.0, (c) 105.5. My back-of-envelope (about 64 bar) estimated B as (measured minus useful energy) / 24.9 bar instead of from the demand volume; I did not trace why the two differ.
- **T2 holds.** Per cycle under (b): median **81.2 bar**, mean 87.1 (95% bootstrap 76.9 to 99.2), minimum **59.0**, maximum 143.0. No cycle comes near 24.9 bar under any of (a) to (c).
- **T3 holds.** Surplus flow at load pressure carries **89.7%** of S + D + I under (b) (78.0 of 87.0 bar); spool drop 4.1 bar, neutral bleed 4.9 bar.
- **T4 holds, but vacuously.** The standby flow changes nothing (0.00%) because LS never counts a sample as still: in every sample some section shows a positive P-port flow with a known pressure (the flow channels carry the 0.8 to 1.8 L/min noise floor of 5o), so the constant-margin LS model already pays its margin on that small flow. I did not check this before registering T4.
- **T5 holds.** Median m*_c under (a) is **76.9 bar**, 3.7 times the synthetic 20.5 bar.
- Not predicted: dm*/dd is **1.78** bar of margin per bar of spool drop (synthetic 1.235), and it equals the ratio of mean fixed-pump flow to mean demand while open-centre moves (93.8 against 52.7 L/min, 1.78). The 63 cc pump at the loader's engine speed delivers 1.78 times the mean demand, yet demand still exceeds it in 23.4% of moving samples: the loader's flow is peaky, so a fixed pump big enough for the peaks wastes most of its flow the rest of the time.
- Reading: the synthetic excavator cycles put the break-even LS margin at 20 to 30 bar, so there the margin decided the ranking. On the measured loader it is 59 to 143 bar per cycle; the machine's 24.9 bar margin uses under a third of that headroom, and nearly all the headroom (about 90%) is surplus flow a fixed pump must push at load pressure. The difference is pump volume against demand volume while moving: 1.78 on the loader against 1.235 on the synthetic cycles (5s), and on the loader that excess flow is pushed at load pressures far above the margin. For a builder: on a peaky duty cycle (a loader's Y-cycle), load-sensing wins regardless of margin choice within any realistic range (12 to 40 bar); on a smoother cycle the margin matters and the formula says by how much.
- Limits: the open-centre counterfactual keeps the measured machine's 63 cc pump; a designer building open-centre could choose a smaller pump and accept slower peaks (3i priced that trade on the synthetic cycles), which would lower S and m*. Hydraulic energy only (no pump efficiency map; 5n's shaft ratios track the hydraulic ones within about 0.08). One machine, one 440 s record.
- Next: m* against a smaller fixed pump on the measured trace, with the cycle-time cost of the flow it cannot supply (the 3i trade on real motion); a valve family rated above 130 L/min for the lift section (5r).

## 2026-11-11 · Milestone 5s: the break-even LS margin in closed form (predictions registered before any code was written)
- Why: every break-even in this programme (2l, 2n, 5p, 5q) was found by sweeping dp_ls on a grid and interpolating where the median LS/OC crosses 1, and the slopes it reported (0.92 bar of margin per bar of neutral pressure in 2l, 1.24 per bar of spool drop in 5q) were read off those sweeps. In the model the load-sensing energy is affine in the margin, LS_c(m) = A_c + B_c m, and open-centre does not depend on it, so each cycle has an exact break-even m*_c = (OC_c - A_c) / B_c. Because LS_c/OC_c is increasing in m for every cycle, the median ratio crosses 1 exactly at the median of m*_c, and the share of cycles in which LS loses at margin m is the share with m*_c < m. That turns the programme's grid answers into a formula a reader can evaluate on their own machine's cycle: m* = (surplus-flow throttling + Q x spool drop x moving time + Q x p_n x idle time - LS idle useful work) / (LS delivered volume + LS idle volume). zenodo.org and assets.danfoss.com refused the proxy this session (403 at 17:58 UTC), so the measured loader and the PVG 100 / partial-travel steps 5r named could not run; this step needs no download.
- **Predictions (written before any code of this step existed):**
  - B1: the median of the closed-form m*_c reproduces the grid break-evens of 2n (28.0 bar), 5p orifice (26.5), 5q envelope orifice (23.0) and every row of 5q's constant-drop sweep (0 to 8 bar: 20.6 to 30.5) within 0.15 bar. Prior 85%.
  - B2: the share of cycles with m*_c < m equals the printed "LS worse" share at 14 and 25 bar in 5p and 5q to 0.1 points. Prior 95%.
  - B3: at a zero open-centre spool drop (5q's 20.6 bar row) the idle term (Q p_n t_idle net of LS idle useful work) carries over half of m*_c in the median cycle (median per-cycle share > 0.5). Prior 50%.
  - B4: the median of the per-cycle slope Q T_work / B_c is within 0.05 of 5q's 1.24 bar of margin per bar of drop. Prior 70%.
  - B5: at zero spool drop the 5 to 95% range of m*_c is wider than 12 bar. Prior 65%.

### Outcome of 5s (`experiments/breakeven_closed_form.py`, `experiments/breakeven_closed_form_results.txt`, 3 s, seeded; `src/heavy_hydraulics/breakeven.py`, 5 new tests)
- Checks: on 200 random cycles with random idle settings LS equals OC at the closed-form margin to 1e-9 (relative) and LS is below OC one bar under it; the two slopes are exact finite differences; for each spool model the per-phase open-centre pressures fed to the formula give the same moving energy as the script the grid answer came from (asserted on every cycle).
- **B1 holds.** Closed-form medians against the grid: 2n 27.97 (28.0), 5p orifice 26.55 (26.5), 5p power 27.18 (27.2), 5p one size up 22.57 (22.6), 5q envelope 23.00 (23.0), 5q per cycle 25.59 (25.6); constant drop 0 to 8 bar 20.53 to 30.49 (20.6 to 30.5). Largest gap **0.075 bar**, the grid's linear interpolation.
- **B2 holds.** LS-worse shares at 14 and 25 bar match every printed value; largest gap 0.03 points.
- **B3 refuted.** At zero spool drop the idle term carries a median **39%** of m*_c (5 to 95%: 13 to 61%); surplus flow at load pressure carries the rest. In bar: surplus 12.3 (5.3 to 23.9), idle 8.1 (2.1 to 14.0). I expected idle to dominate at a load factor of 0.346; it does not, because LS also pays its margin on 2 L/min standby plus 2 to 8 L/min idle flow, which grows B.
- **B4 holds.** Median per-cycle dm*/dd = Q T_move / B = **1.235**, against 1.24 read off 5q's sweep (the slope of the median itself is 1.245). Its idle counterpart dm*/dp_n = Q t_idle / B has median **0.916**, the 0.92 bar of margin per bar of neutral pressure that 2l read off its grid (2l used other idle settings, so the agreement is a consistency check, not a test).
- **B5 holds.** At zero drop m*_c spans **10.7 to 34.1 bar** (5 to 95%), 23.4 bar wide. The cycle, not the valve, decides most of the answer: the spread across cycles is ten times the 2.3 bar the best-case spool floor adds.
- Reading: the programme's break-evens are one formula, m* = (S + D + I) / B, evaluated per cycle, and its medians and LS-worse shares follow exactly. The two slopes the programme measured are the ratios of pump volume (moving and idle) to load-sensing volume, so a builder can get them from cycle times and flows alone. The 20.6 bar zero-drop break-even is about three fifths surplus flow and two fifths idle bleed. The formula replaces grid sweeps in every later step (3 s for all settings here against about 30 s per setting), and it runs on any trace that has phase flows, the highest load pressure and idle time: the measured loader of 5n to 5r is the next place to apply it.
- Limits: exact only inside this model (a fixed open-centre pump; a load-sensing pump whose energy is affine in the margin, with no margin-dependent leakage or efficiency; the same pump efficiency for both); the cycles are the synthetic force-model cycles (2c).
- Next: m*_c on the measured loader's 18 Y-cycles when zenodo.org is reachable (the break-even margin of a real machine, against its measured 24.9 bar); the open questions of 5r (a valve rated above 130 L/min; partial-travel drop) stand.

## 2026-11-11 · Milestone 5r: 5p's registered R1 to R5 on the measured loader (Zenodo reachable again)
- Why: 5p wrote `experiments/spool_floor.py` and registered R1 to R5 before any run, but zenodo.org refused the proxy that session. It answered at the start of this one (HTTP 200 at about 13:05 UTC; the 96 MB `Short_Y_cycle_data.mat` downloaded in under a minute), so the measured half of 5p ran with the predictions unchanged. Data check: `experiments/measured_motion.py` on this download reproduces `measured_motion_results.txt` byte for byte.
- Code changes before the first run: none to the computation. After it, two edits that leave every number unchanged: the weighted-drop mean now subtracts only moving samples (the old line computed `-inf - -inf` on still samples and then masked them, a NumPy warning), and each sizing rule now prints how much of each section's moving flow exceeds its spool's rated flow.

### Outcome of 5r (`experiments/spool_floor.py`, `experiments/spool_floor_results.txt`, 2.4 s)
- **R1 holds.** Orifice floor, p99.9 sizing, p_neutral 5 bar: open-centre needs **1.424 times** the measured load-sensing pump hydraulic energy (shaft 1.346, **11.3% more fuel**); 1.455 at p_neutral 10 bar. 5o's zero-drop floor was 1.40, so the PVG 32 full-travel drop adds 0.024.
- **R2 holds.** Hydraulic-energy-weighted extra drop while moving 2.59 bar (orifice), 2.11 (power law).
- **R3 holds.** Power law minus orifice: 0.005 in the ratio.
- **R4 holds, but vacuously.** Every sizing rule picks spool E, the largest PVG 32 spool (100 L/min): lift needs 121.6 to 133.2 L/min and tilt 81.6 to 132.8 L/min, so the span is 0.000 because there is nothing to choose. I did not foresee this when registering R4; I had not checked the trace's flows against the spool range.
- **R5 holds.** Per cycle (18), mean 1.419 (95% bootstrap 1.350 to 1.506), minimum 1.224.
- Not predicted: on the lift section, flow exceeds spool E's 100 L/min rating in **11.2% of moving samples carrying 30.2% of its moving hydraulic energy** (tilt 0.1%). There the floor extrapolates the orifice law past the rated point, so it describes a hypothetical larger spool of the same shape, not a PVG 32 that exists. A 6 t loader's lift circuit at 130 L/min is outside the PVG 32 range; the real machine's valve is not named in the record.
- Reading: on a real machine's measured motion and pressures, the full-travel spool floor barely moves the open-centre penalty (1.40 to 1.42, p_neutral 5 bar). Almost all of it is structural: a fixed pump delivering its whole flow at the highest moving load pressure. So the open-centre counterfactual on this trace is bracketed at **1.42 to 1.54** times load-sensing pump energy (floor to 5o's 10 bar spool drop), **11 to 15% more fuel**, and the spool question that decided 5q's synthetic break-even does not decide this one. The bracket rests on the floor being a floor; the over-rating extrapolation makes the upper end of the floor drop (and so 1.424) less certain, not the lower bound 1.40.
- Limits: unchanged from 5o and 5p (one wheel-loader record, counterfactual open-centre on the same motion with a 63 cc fixed pump, Willans slope from the ECU's power estimate, band-middle reading of the manual curves); PVG 32 is a stand-in valve family, too small for the lift flow.
- Next: a valve family rated above 130 L/min (e.g. the Danfoss PVG 100 or a monoblock loader valve) to give the lift section a sourced floor; the partial-travel drop from the PVG 32 spool-travel curves for the synthetic excavator cycles (5q's open question).

## 2026-11-11 · Milestone 5q: size each open-centre section per function, not per phase (predictions registered before the first run)
- Why: 5p's largest unpredicted lever was spool sizing. Its "3g sizing" picks a spool per actuator-phase from that phase's demand, which no machine does: a valve section is chosen once per function (boom, arm, bucket, swing) for the largest flow that function is ever asked to pass, so light phases run on an oversized spool whose full-travel floor is near zero. This step sizes per function and asks where the break-even margin goes, and how small an open-centre drop must be before the 25 bar thesis margin loses.
- Method (`experiments/spool_function_sizing.py`): same 3,000 force-model cycles, idle model and PVG 32 neutral drop as 2n/5p. Functions are my reading of the phase names (not sourced): dig = arm + bucket, lift and lower = boom, swing and swing back = swing, dump = bucket. Two rules: (a) design envelope, each function's spool covers the largest flow the model can give it (single function at the full 45 L/min pump, arm at 22.5 L/min, half of dig), so arm B and boom, bucket, swing D; (b) per cycle, each function sized to its largest demand in that cycle (a machine tuned to this one cycle). Plus a constant-drop sweep (0 to 8 bar) for the drop at which the break-even margin is 25 bar.
- **Predictions (written before the script existed):**
  - F1: design-envelope sizing (orifice) moves the break-even dp_ls to 21.5 to 24.0 bar. Prior 65%.
  - F2: its energy-weighted extra open-centre drop while moving is 0.5 to 2.5 bar. Prior 65%.
  - F3: per-cycle function sizing puts the break-even strictly between design-envelope sizing and 5p's per-phase 26.5 bar. Prior 75%.
  - F4: under design-envelope sizing LS/OC median is above 1 at a 25 bar margin and below 0.9 at 14 bar. Prior 75%.
  - F5: the constant open-centre drop at which the break-even margin equals 25 bar is 3 to 4.5 bar. Prior 60%.

### Outcome of 5q (`experiments/spool_function_sizing.py`, `experiments/spool_function_sizing_results.txt`, 32 s, seeded)
- Checks: the constant 6 bar path reproduces 5p's; per-cycle sizing never picks a smaller spool than per-phase sizing, and envelope sizing never a smaller one than per-cycle (asserted on 200 cycles). 5p's own script still reproduces its results file byte for byte after a one-line fix (its S1 line used a nested-quote f-string that only Python 3.12+ parses; it failed to import under 3.11).
- Envelope spools: arm B (22.5 L/min), bucket, boom and swing D (45 L/min).
- **F1 holds.** Envelope sizing: break-even dp_ls **23.0 bar** (orifice), 22.7 (power law).
- **F2 holds.** Energy-weighted extra open-centre drop while moving 2.33 bar (orifice), 2.18 (power law).
- **F3 holds.** Per-cycle function sizing: break-even 25.6 bar (orifice), 26.2 (power law), between envelope (23.0) and 5p's per-phase 26.5.
- **F4 holds.** Envelope sizing: LS/OC median 1.037 at 25 bar (LS worse in 60.6% of cycles), 0.830 at 14 bar (worse in 5.3%).
- **F5 holds.** Constant-drop sweep: break-even 20.6 bar at a zero drop, rising 1.24 bar per bar of drop (21.8, 23.0, 24.3, 25.5, 26.7, 28.0 at 1 to 6 bar); a 25 bar margin breaks even at a **3.60 bar** open-centre drop.
- Reading: with sections sized the way a machine is built (once per function), the PVG 32 full-travel floor is about 2.3 bar and the break-even margin 23 bar, so the 25 bar thesis margin loses to the best possible open-centre valve, while the 14 bar Rexroth margin wins. The stronger statement comes from the zero-drop row: **no open-centre spool, however sized or however it closes, moves the break-even below 20.6 bar** in this setup (PVG 32 neutral drop, load factor 0.346, 2 L/min standby), so any load-sensing margin under about 20 bar wins on these cycles, and the full-travel floor route cannot say more for margins between 20.6 and 28 bar. The measured loader's 24.9 bar margin (5n) sits inside the 23.0 to 25.6 bar band the two function-sizing rules span; on these synthetic cycles it is a coin toss decided by the real partial-lever drop, which needs the partial-travel curves (3d) or a measured open-centre machine, not a better floor.
- Limits: function mapping is my reading of the phase names; envelope flows assume a single function can take the whole pump; synthetic force-model cycles (2c); no time penalty (3i: charging it raises the break-even by 6 to 11 bar, against open-centre); a floor, so real sections add drop at partial travel.
- Next: the partial-travel drop at the sizes used here, from the PVG 32 spool-travel curves; run `spool_floor.py` on the loader record (R1 to R5) when Zenodo is reachable.

## 2026-11-11 · Milestone 5p: a sourced lower bound on the open-centre spool drop, from the PVG 32 full-travel P-to-A curves (predictions registered before the first run)
- Why: 5o left the open-centre answer on the measured loader spanning 1.37 to 1.67, and the largest remaining knob was `dp_spool`, the drop from pump to the moving chamber, swept 0 to 15 bar with no source. 5o's floor set it to zero. It cannot be zero while oil flows: an uncompensated spool at travel x passes `q = K(x) sqrt(p_pump - p_in)`, and `K(x) <= K(full travel)`, so `p_pump >= p_in + (q / K_full)^2` for every moving section, whatever the operator does with the lever or how the bypass closes. 3j read `K_full` for each PVG 32 spool from the manual's P-to-A flow at full travel (10 and 20 bar margin, `data/pvg32_flow_full_travel.csv`). This step puts that bound on the measured trace: a tighter, sourced floor than 5o's `dp_spool = 0`.
- Method (`experiments/spool_floor.py`): each section (lift, tilt) gets the smallest PVG 32 spool (A to E) whose rated flow covers its p99.9 positive P-port flow (3g's rule); per sample the open-centre pump pressure while moving is `max_i (p_i + dp_i(q_i))` with `dp_i = 10 bar x (q_i / q10_i)^2` (orifice through the 10 bar point) or `10 x (q_i / q10_i)^(1/n_i)` (power law through both measured points, 3j's exponent n). Motion as 5o (`q_min` at the measured noise floor); p_neutral 5 and 10 bar; same fixed 63 cc pump, shaft model and Willans slope as 5n/5o. Sizing rule swept (p99, p99.9, max flow, one size up).
- **Predictions (written before any statistic of this step was computed):**
  - R1: the orifice floor (p99.9 sizing, p_neutral 5 bar) gives OC/LS pump hydraulic energy in 1.41 to 1.48 (5o's zero-drop floor is 1.40). Prior 65%.
  - R2: the hydraulic-energy-weighted mean floor drop while moving is under 5 bar, half of 5n's central 10 bar. Prior 70%.
  - R3: the two-point power-law variant differs from the orifice variant by under 0.03 in the R1 ratio. Prior 60%.
  - R4: across the four sizing rules the R1 ratio spans under 0.05. Prior 60%.
  - R5: the floor is at least 1.20 in each of the 18 Y-cycles (5o's zero-drop minimum was 1.19). Prior 65%.
- **Not run this session:** zenodo.org (and doi.org, figshare, data.caltech.edu) answered the egress proxy's CONNECT with 403 at 09:12 and 09:13 UTC, so the 96 MB record could not be fetched; the 5n and 5o runs earlier today had it. R1 to R5 stay registered and unrun; `experiments/spool_floor.py` is written and smoke-tested on a synthetic trace, ready for a run that can reach Zenodo.
- **What runs instead (synthetic part, `experiments/spool_floor_synthetic.py`):** the same bound on the programme's force-model cycles, in 2n's setup (45 L/min pump, PVG 32 neutral drop 10.91 bar, load factor 0.346, standby 2 L/min, eta 0.883). Since 2d the open-centre spool drop there has been a constant 6 bar from Juza and Hermanek's measured block, a different machine and valve from the PVG 32 that sets the neutral drop; this replaces it with the PVG 32's own full-travel floor, each actuator on the smallest spool covering its demand. Being a floor, it is the best case for open-centre. Predictions, written before the script was first run:
  - S1: 2n's break-even dp_ls (28.0 bar with 6 bar) moves to 24 to 29 bar. Prior 65%.
  - S2: load-sensing still wins at the Rexroth 14 bar margin, LS/OC median under 0.85 (2n: 0.759). Prior 85%.
  - S3: the energy-weighted mean extra open-centre drop while moving is 3 to 7 bar. Prior 60%.

### Outcome of 5p, synthetic part (`experiments/spool_floor_synthetic.py`, `experiments/spool_floor_synthetic_results.txt`, 14 s, seeded; 4 new tests)
- Check: the constant-6-bar path reproduces 2n exactly (break-even 28.0 bar, LS/OC 0.759 at 14 bar, same cycles).
- Per actuator the PVG 32 floor (orifice law, smallest covering spool) is a median **4.78 bar** (5 to 95%: 1.92 to 7.70); spools used A 496, B 9,446, C 8,310, D 2,748 actuator-phases.
- **S1 holds.** Break-even dp_ls **26.5 bar** with the orifice floor (27.2 with the two-point power law), against 28.0 with the constant 6 bar.
- **S2 holds.** LS/OC at 14 bar is 0.778 (orifice) and 0.768 (power law); load-sensing worse in 1.6% and 1.3% of cycles.
- **S3 holds.** Energy-weighted extra open-centre drop while moving 4.77 bar (orifice), 5.26 (power law).
- Not predicted, and the largest lever: **spool sizing.** One spool size up (a section oversized for its demand) cuts the floor to 1.64 / 2.16 bar and the break-even to **22.6 / 22.9 bar**, so at the thesis 25 bar margin load-sensing then loses (LS/OC 1.046, worse in 61.7% of cycles), while at the Rexroth 14 bar margin it still wins (0.837 / 0.830, worse in 8.6% / 7.6%).
- Reading: Juza and Hermanek's measured 6 bar open-centre block loss, used since 2d, sits just above the least drop any PVG 32 section could have at these flows (4.8 to 5.3 bar), so it was not a pessimistic choice for open-centre, and 2n's ranking (load-sensing ahead at 14 bar, near break-even at 25) survives replacing it with the same valve family's own physical floor. The ranking at the 25 bar margin depends on how tightly the open-centre spools are sized to the demand, a design choice, more than on the orifice law (0.7 bar of break-even) or the source of the drop.
- Limits: a floor, so real partial-travel sections add more (this favours open-centre); the 3j figure reading (band middle, "theoretical performance", pump margin taken as the P-to-A drop); synthetic force-model flows and pressures (2c), one load factor, no time penalty (3i showed charging it raises the break-even by 6 to 11 bar); sizing per phase demand, where a real machine sizes per function for its largest demand (closer to the "one size up" row for light phases).
- Next: run `experiments/spool_floor.py` on the loader record when Zenodo is reachable (R1 to R5 registered); size per function (largest demand over the cycle) on the synthetic cycles; fold 5p into paper section 3.6.

## 2026-11-11 · Milestone 5o: what counts as motion on the measured loader? A noise floor from the data and an open-centre floor (predictions registered before the first run)
- Why: 5n's open-centre counterfactual moved most with `q_min`, the flow below which a section counted as still, which I chose after seeing the first run (0.5 / 2 / 5 L/min). It is a property of the trace, not of the valve: the authors' flows come from piston velocity (5 Hz low-pass), so a held boom carries small spurious flows. This step measures the noise floor instead of choosing it, checks it against a motion test built from piston position alone, and adds the physical floor of any open-centre valve.
- Why no orifice closing law: for any open-centre spool, flow can enter a chamber at p_i only if pump pressure exceeds p_i, and the fixed pump delivers its whole flow at that pressure (the bypass passes the rest). So while a loaded section moves, the open-centre pump power is at least `Q_fix x p_load`, whatever the bypass closing law (3a to 3d); the law only sets the extra drop, which 5n swept as `dp_spool`. With the PVG 32 bypass closing inside the dead band (3d), the 5n step model is the large-k limit. So `dp_spool = 0` is a closing-law-free floor for open-centre while moving.
- Method (`experiments/measured_motion.py`): stationary windows are 1 s windows in which a cylinder's position range is under 0.5 mm (mean speed under 0.5 mm/s, about 0.3 L/min at the lift cylinders' 100.5 cm2 piston area); the noise floor of a section is the 99th percentile of its |P-port flow| in those windows. Position-based motion: a section moves when its piston travels more than d_min over a centred 0.5 s window (d_min 0.5 / 1 / 2 mm).
- **Predictions (written before any of these statistics was computed):**
  - Q1: the noise floor (p99 in stationary windows) of both lift and tilt sections lies in 0.5 to 5 L/min, the range 5n swept. Prior 60%. Refuted if either is outside.
  - Q2: with `q_min` at each section's measured noise floor (dp_spool 10, p_neutral 10 bar), open-centre over load-sensing pump hydraulic energy is 1.3 to 1.6. Prior 55%.
  - Q3: the open-centre floor (dp_spool 0, p_neutral 5 bar, the low end of 5n's sweep, noise-floor `q_min`) still needs at least 1.2 times the measured load-sensing pump energy. Prior 70%.
  - Q4: the position-based motion test at d_min 1 mm gives an open-centre ratio within 0.10 of Q2's. Prior 55%.

### Outcome of 5o (`experiments/measured_motion.py`, `experiments/measured_motion_results.txt`, about 60 s; 4 new tests)
- **Q1 holds.** Stationary windows cover 16.4% (lift) and 22.5% (tilt) of the record. Their |P-port flow| has p99 **0.80 L/min (lift) and 1.76 L/min (tilt)**, both above the 0.30 / 0.38 L/min that the window's own 0.5 mm range allows, so the floor is filter noise, not slow motion. It depends on the window rule: 0.31 to 4.0 (lift) and 1.47 to 4.46 L/min (tilt) over windows 0.5 / 1 / 2 s and ranges 0.25 / 0.5 / 1 mm, with the 1 mm rows rising because they admit real creep.
- **Q2 holds.** With `q_min` at each section's floor (dp_spool 10, p_neutral 10 bar) open-centre uses **1.54 times** the measured load-sensing pump hydraulic energy (per cycle 1.46 to 1.63, 95% bootstrap over 18 cycles), 1.45 times the shaft energy, and adds **14.7% fuel** (per cycle 12.9 to 16.2%). This replaces 5n's central 1.46 / 12.3%, which used a 2 L/min threshold above the lift floor.
- **Q3 holds.** The closing-law-free open-centre floor (dp_spool 0) gives **1.37 / 1.40 / 1.43** at p_neutral 0 / 5 / 10 bar (shaft 1.30 to 1.35, extra fuel 9.7 to 11.5%); per cycle at 5 bar 1.32 to 1.48, minimum single cycle 1.19. On this cycle no open-centre valve on a 63 cc fixed pump gets within 30% of the measured load-sensing pump's hydraulic energy.
- **Q4 holds.** Motion from piston position alone (travel over 0.5 s above 1 mm) gives 1.51 against Q2's 1.54 (difference 0.03); d_min 0.5 / 2 mm give 1.67 / 1.46. Two independent motion definitions agree to within 0.03 at their central settings and span 1.46 to 1.67 over their sweeps.
- Reading: 5n's largest unsourced knob is now measured, and the open-centre answer narrows: LS uses 65% of open-centre pump hydraulic energy at the central settings and at most 73% for any open-centre closing law and neutral drop down to zero. The remaining spread (1.37 to 1.67) is the spool drop while moving (0 to 10 bar) and the motion rule, not the bypass law. The fuel saving of load-sensing on this machine is 10 to 18% of measured fuel for that range.
- Limits: unchanged from 5n (wheel loader, one record, counterfactual open-centre on the same motion and pump, Willans slope from the ECU's fuelling-based power estimate); the floor is the noise of the authors' filtered velocity signal, not of any valve; the position test uses the same position sensor the velocity was derived from, so the two rules are not fully independent. Huova et al. (2018, doi:10.1115/FPMC2018-8869) was not read (ASME, paywalled), so there is still no published fuel figure from this data to compare against.
- Next: paper section 3.6 now carries 5n and 5o; find an open excavator pressure trace (H4) or a second loader record; sweep spool drop with the PVG 32 P-to-A curves read in 3h.

## 2026-11-11 · Milestone 5n: the first measured pressure-and-fuel trace, a 6 t load-sensing wheel loader (Zenodo 10639199); predictions registered before the first run
- Why: BLOCKED since 2026-11-03 for want of a measured pressure/fuel trace. A Zenodo search ("wheel loader bucket filling") found Linjama, Huova, Tammisto, Tervonen and Ahola, "6-ton wheel loader short Y-cycle data for 440 seconds loading of gravel", Zenodo doi:10.5281/zenodo.10639199 (CC BY 4.0, 2024): 2 ms samples of lift, tilt and stabilizer cylinder positions and chamber pressures, work-pump outlet pressure, engine speed and ECU fuel rate on a 63 cc variable work pump and an 83 kW diesel, measured 2017-08-02 at Tampere University. Not an excavator, but the first real machine in this programme with pump pressure, actuator pressures and fuel on one clock.
- **Predictions (written before any statistic was computed):**
  - P1: during boom or bucket motion (P-port flow above 5 L/min), the median of pump pressure minus the highest moving-actuator inlet pressure lies in 14 to 30 bar, the margin range swept since 2d. Prior 60%. Refuted if outside.
  - P2: the model's load-sensing rung, `Q x (p_max + dp_ls)` with the median measured margin, reproduces the measured work-pump hydraulic energy of the record within 15%. Prior 50%. (Weak test: both sides share the same flow, so only the constant-margin assumption is tested.)
  - P3: on this measured cycle a fixed 63 cc open-centre pump uses 1.5 to 3.0 times the load-sensing pump hydraulic energy (milestone 1's median, 47%, is a ratio of 2.1). Prior 55%.
  - P4: through a Willans line fitted to the record's own fuel rate against engine power, open-centre working hydraulics would raise the machine's fuel by 10 to 30% over the measured fuel. Prior 50%.
- Run (`experiments/measured_loader.py`, `experiments/measured_loader_results.txt`; `src/heavy_hydraulics/measured.py`, 9 new tests). The machine behaves as load-sensing: with no P-port flow the pump sits at a median 30.8 bar standby. 18 Y-cycles (mean 21.2 s) are cut at upward crossings of lift position 0.30 m; intervals are 95% bootstraps over cycles.
- **P1 holds.** Median pump-minus-load margin during motion is **24.9 bar** (p25 17.6, p75 29.6; 24.7 bar when flow exceeds 20 L/min). 9.1% of moving samples have a negative margin; they sit at low flow (median 9.7 L/min) near standby and carry 1.6% of the measured hydraulic energy, which I read as lag in the authors' velocity-derived flow (not checked further). This is the first *measured* margin in the programme, inside the 14 to 25 bar pair from manuals used since 2d and close to the 28 bar break-even of 2n.
- **P2 holds (weak test).** The constant-margin load-sensing rung gives 1.023 of the measured pump hydraulic energy (per cycle 1.013 to 1.034). Measured useful-to-pump ratio is 0.733 (0.720 to 0.745); the ideal metered rung would use 0.765 of the measured pump energy.
- **P3 and P4: the first run had a bug, and the corrected answer depends on a threshold I had not foreseen.** The first run counted any positive filtered flow as motion, so the open-centre pump never saw its neutral drop and was charged full flow at holding pressure for all 440 s; it gave OC/LS 2.29 hydraulic, 2.08 shaft and +35.5% fuel (P3 held, P4 refuted high). The fix makes a section count as moving for open-centre only above `q_min` L/min, which I chose after seeing that run and which the predictions did not anticipate. With `q_min` 2 L/min (central), dp_spool 10, p_neutral 10 bar: OC/LS **1.46 hydraulic, 1.38 shaft** (per cycle 1.39 to 1.53, 1.32 to 1.43) and open-centre adds **12.3% fuel** (per cycle 10.7 to 13.6%). Over the 27-cell sweep (dp_spool 6/10/15, p_neutral 5/10/15, `q_min` 0.5/2/5) the hydraulic ratio runs 1.24 to 1.79 (**P3 holds in 11/27 cells, refuted overall**) and extra fuel 6.2 to 21.7% (**P4 holds in 19/27 cells, refuted overall**). `q_min` moves the answer most (1.7 at 0.5 L/min to 1.3 at 5 L/min); the two valve drops move it by under 0.1 each.
- Reading: on a real machine trace load-sensing saves about a quarter to two fifths of open-centre pump energy (LS is 56 to 81% of OC), less than milestone 1's synthetic median (47%), and about 6 to 22% of whole-machine fuel. The open-centre counterfactual hinges on how the bypass behaves at small spool openings, because the real cycle spends much of its time at small flows, which piecewise-constant cycles hide. The Willans line from the record (fuel L/h = -0.94 + 0.233 x engine kW, r 0.957, 1 s means) uses the ECU's engine power estimate, which is itself derived from fuelling, so only the marginal 0.233 L/kWh is used.
- Limits: a 6 t wheel loader, not an excavator; one operator, one 440 s record; open-centre is a counterfactual on the same 63 cc pump and the same motion (a real open-centre machine would be driven differently); valve drops at about 100 L/min not sourced for this size; the pump torque-loss model was read from a garbled PDF equation and matches the authors' torque estimate to a median 0.6 N m (p99 3.9 N m, mean torque 68.6 N m); flows are the authors' estimates from piston velocity; the fixed pump falls short of demand for 65 s at low engine speed and the demand is charged instead. H4 as written (a measured fuel ratio of a machine pair) is still not testable; this tests the model's load-sensing rung on a measured machine instead.
- `BLOCKED` removed: the programme has a measured trace to work on.
- Next: replace the `q_min` step with the PVG 32 bypass-closing law of 3d on this trace; read Huova et al. (2018, doi:10.1115/FPMC2018-8869), which used this data, for a published baseline fuel figure to compare against; add 5n to paper section 3.

## 2026-11-03 · Milestone 5m: where the CWA electric-excavator profile comes from (a reading step, no experiment)
- Read the draft CWA (CEN-CENELEC workshop on BATSS, draft CWA XXXXX, section 6, https://www.cencenelec.eu/media/CEN-CENELEC/News/Workshops/2026/20260625_BATTS/draft_cwa_xxxxx_-e.pdf; text extracted with pdftotext). 5l's open question is answered, and it corrects how 5l and the load factor 0.346 were framed.
- Zenodo 18862594 is the **theoretical** reference profile, not a measurement: digging and grading power computed for a **75 t, 410 kW diesel** excavator (after Fecke's PhD thesis, Tranplhyn project) and scaled linearly to a 100 kW motor (section 6.1, 6.4; the 133 kW peak is the grading case). The CWA's own Table 2: theoretical digging 93 kW peak / 35 kW mean, grading 133 / 56. Two **real** BATSS electric excavators: 13 t class 81 kW peak / 40 kW mean, and 20 t class 92 / 49 kW (1 min sampling).
- Load factors (mean/peak) from that table: theoretical digging 0.376, grading 0.421; real 0.494 and 0.533. The 0.346 used in 2e to 2g (mean over peak of the full 1,779 s synthetic profile) is therefore a synthetic machine's value and sits below both real ones. No result changes: `ranking_map_lf_results.txt` already sweeps load factor to 0.50 and 0.70, where the break-even dp_ls moves only 19.3 to 21.7 bar (p_n 1.44 bar) and 23.9 to 23.3 bar (p_n 6.44 bar); LS-versus-open-centre conclusions are insensitive to the choice. The real traces themselves are not public (they are in BATSS deliverable D2.1), so they could not replace the profile.
- The CWA also states commercial excavators have no hydraulic energy recovery, consistent with 5l's 0.001% negative energy, which is therefore a property of the synthetic profile, not evidence about real machines.
- Limits: only the draft text was read (figures and Table 2 as extracted); mean/peak from rounded table values.
- Next: an excavator cylinder pressure trace (H4) is still missing; paper wording on the 0.346 is corrected in `paper/draft.md`.

## 2026-11-03 · Milestone 6g: paper section 3.4a gains 5k and 5l (a writing step, no experiment)
- Added the measured 2.10 s Cat 305.5E2 lowering time with the re-rated recovery power (979 / 3,478 W; 1.21 / 4.31 kW at 2.1 s; 5.33 kW at 1.7 s) and the published electric-excavator power profile (peak/mean 2.89, best 3 s window 1.85 x mean, 0.001% negative energy) to the draft, with their limits. 9 new rows in `paper/check_numbers.py`, 81 claims, 0 missing.
